# Deferred Work

> ## ⏸ Parked as of 2026-08-13 — the database entries and the flat/legacy backlog
>
> **What is parked, and only this:** the two DW entries about the sqlite/drift
> history store (DW-1, DW-4), and the pre-DW-format legacy backlog, now
> migrated into DW-94 … DW-113. Everything else is `open` and schedulable.
>
> **Nothing here is fixed.** `parked` means "known, filed, and not scheduled".
>
> **Correction to the earlier parking.** An earlier pass on this same date
> flipped all 87 open DW entries to parked under a blanket "the MVP epic is
> done, set the whole ledger aside" rationale. That was broader than the
> decision actually taken, and it also missed its own target: the legacy
> entries carry no `status:` line, so the sed that did the parking could not
> touch them — and the substantive database items (a corrupt `history.sqlite`
> has no recovery path; schema v1 ships with no `drift_schemas/` snapshot) were
> living in exactly those legacy entries. 85 DW entries have been returned to
> `status: open`.
>
> **The legacy entries were migrated, not just annotated.** A `status:` line
> added to a legacy bullet does nothing — the parser keys on `### DW-<n>:`
> headings, and `bmad-loop sweep --dry-run` still counted all 20 as open. They
> are now canonical DW-94 … DW-113 with `status: parked 2026-08-13` (two that
> were already `done 2026-08-07` kept that status and their `resolution:`).
> Every `summary:`, `evidence:` and `resolution:` body was carried across
> verbatim into `reason:` and body lines; nothing was dropped. This also closes
> the gap the earlier header flagged — a manual sweep can no longer migrate
> them back into circulation as `open`.
>
> **Counts after this re-scoping:** 113 DW entries — 89 open, 20 parked
> (DW-1, DW-4 and eighteen of the migrated legacy entries), 4 done.
>
> **A separate defect, filed here and not fixed:** 55 further flat
> `- source_spec:` entries are interleaved *between* DW entries throughout this
> file, appended in flat form by dev sessions and never normalized. The
> orchestrator counts none of them — they are neither DW entries nor part of
> the leading legacy block it parses — so they are invisible to triage and to
> the TUI alike. They now carry `status: parked 2026-08-13` for a human reader,
> but that line is decorative until they are migrated the way DW-94 … DW-113
> were. The earlier header's "20 legacy entries" figure counted only the
> leading block and missed all 55.
>
> **Why parking makes a sweep skip an entry:** bmad-loop treats an entry as
> open only when the first word of its `status:` is exactly `open`. Any other
> word is invisible to triage. The entries themselves are untouched — this
> ledger is append-only, and parking flips a field rather than deleting
> anything.
>
> **To un-park the database and legacy work** when you want to pick it up:
>
> ```sh
> sed -i 's/^status: parked 2026-08-13$/status: open/' \
>   _bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/deferred-work.md
> ```
>
> To un-park a single entry, change just that entry's status line back to
> `open`. New entries filed by future stories are unaffected and will arrive
> `open` as normal.

### DW-94: ARCHITECTURE-SPINE.md's Stack table still lists `drift_flutter | 0.3.1` after story 2 dropped it

origin: migrated from legacy ledger (pre-DW-format entries above DW-1), 2026-08-13
source_spec: `_bmad-output/specs/spec-hotkey-grammar-corrector/stories/2-drift-history-persistence.md`
location: n/a
reason: ARCHITECTURE-SPINE.md's Stack table still lists `drift_flutter | 0.3.1`, but story 2 removed drift_flutter from pubspec.yaml entirely — the spine and the codebase now disagree on a Stack row, and the pin-renegotiation paragraph still describes the EOL transitive resolution as current.
status: parked 2026-08-13
evidence: Story 2 closed the drift/drift_flutter EOL deferred item by dropping drift_flutter rather than by keeping it (see that story's Spec Change Log). Resolution is now drift 2.34.3 + sqlite3 3.5.1 with no drift_flutter, no sqlite3_flutter_libs, no sqlcipher_flutter_libs and no jni in pubspec.lock; `flutter build linux --release` bundles lib/libsqlite3.so (1.8 MB) from sqlite3's Dart build hooks, and 224 tests plus `flutter analyze` pass. Recorded rather than hand-edited into the spine, following the same convention story 1 used for AD-16's line-count drift.
note_on_the_closed_eol_item: For the record, so the answer is not lost with the deleted entry — the `+eol` packages were never a live hazard. `sqlite3_flutter_libs 0.6.0+eol` and `sqlcipher_flutter_libs 0.7.0+eol` are deliberately empty tombstones published by drift's own author ("Not used anymore, update to version 3.x of package:sqlite3 instead"); they carry no native code. Native sqlite already came from `sqlite3 3.5.1`'s Dart build hooks either way. So the item was closable on the evidence alone, and dropping drift_flutter (whose only use here was the `driftDatabase()` path helper, which this epic does not use — AppPaths owns path resolution) was the stronger of two valid closures, not a necessary fix.

### DW-95: PanelVisibility must flip its isVisible mirror synchronously inside show()/hide()

origin: migrated from legacy ledger (pre-DW-format entries above DW-1), 2026-08-13
source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-application-layer-controllers.md`
location: n/a
reason: The window_manager PanelVisibility adapter must flip its isVisible mirror synchronously inside show()/hide(), not only from window events, or a fast double hotkey press shows twice and never hides.
status: done 2026-08-07
resolution: Closed by story 5. `WindowManagerPanelVisibility` sets the mirror and emits on `changes` before the first await of `show()`/`hide()`, and two further mechanisms turned out to be necessary for the *panel* — not just the mirror — to end hidden: requests are serialised (`windowManager.show()` awaits an `isMinimized()` hop while `hide()` invokes immediately, so unserialised requests reach the platform out of order), and a request whose intent the mirror no longer holds is abandoned without touching the window at all (`focus()` is `gtk_window_present`, which maps a hidden toplevel, so even the trailing half of a superseded show re-shows the panel). Window events move the mirror only while no request of ours is outstanding. The lag is modelled by `test/fakes/fake_panel_window.dart`, which models window *state* as well as call order; the double-press row runs in both pre-hop modes and asserts `window.visible == visibility.isVisible` plus `wasEverVisible == false`. The negative controls — the exact mutation edits and the number of tests each fails — are recorded once, in the doc comment at the head of `test/infrastructure/panel/window_manager_panel_visibility_test.dart`. They are deliberately not restated here: an earlier version of this line carried its own counts, three of the four disagreed with the test file's, and two contradictory records of the story's stated gate are worse than one.
evidence: AD-8 requires the mirror be synchronous and the toggle reads it without awaiting (the spine's own sketch). A lagging mirror is invisible to the controller and to FakePanelVisibility, which flips synchronously — so this can only be caught in the adapter slice, and its fake/test must model the lag.

### DW-96: PanelVisibility.changes does not declare itself broadcast, and two consumers need it

origin: migrated from legacy ledger (pre-DW-format entries above DW-1), 2026-08-13
source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-application-layer-controllers.md`
location: n/a
reason: PanelVisibility.changes does not declare whether it is broadcast, but the panel widget and the correction controller will both need it — a single-subscription adapter would throw at startup.
status: done 2026-08-07
resolution: Closed by story 5 with the single authorised domain edit — a doc comment on `PanelVisibility.changes` in the same terms `ConfigStore.changes` uses, naming the panel widget and `CorrectionController` as the two independent consumers. Members and signatures are untouched, so AD-8's verbatim declaration still holds. The shipped adapter backs it (`StreamController<bool>.broadcast()`) and `test/infrastructure/panel/panel_visibility_broadcast_test.dart` pins both halves: two independent subscriptions each receive every transition and neither throws, and a late listener receives only what follows. `PanelController`'s doc, which justified not re-exposing the stream with "the port does not declare its stream broadcast", now states the reason that survives the edit (application-ring layering).
evidence: This slice keeps exactly one listener (PanelController deliberately does not re-expose the stream), so nothing breaks today. ConfigStore.changes already documents itself as broadcast; PanelVisibility should too, which is a domain edit and therefore human-gated by this spec's "Ask First".

### DW-98: a hand-edited config file never reaches a resident daemon — ConfigStore.changes emits only on its own writes

origin: migrated from legacy ledger (pre-DW-format entries above DW-1), 2026-08-13
source_spec: `_bmad-output/specs/spec-hotkey-grammar-corrector/stories/1-config-store-app-paths-and-system-ports.md`
location: n/a
reason: A config file edited by hand while the daemon is resident never reaches it — ConfigStore.changes emits only after a write() the daemon itself made, so the file half of CAP-8 takes effect on the next restart.
status: parked 2026-08-13
evidence: The port documents `changes` as "emits after every successful write", and JsonConfigStore implements exactly that; nothing watches the file. CAP-8 says switching provider or preset "changes which backend serves the next correction", which a restart satisfies, so this is a gap in immediacy rather than a contract breach — but the class doc invites hand-editing, so the mismatch should be decided deliberately (file watch, or documented restart requirement) when the composition root lands.

Raised independently by the adversarial and edge-case lenses.

### DW-104: history growth is unbounded and unclearable, and every row holds the user's plaintext

origin: migrated from legacy ledger (pre-DW-format entries above DW-1), 2026-08-13
source_spec: `_bmad-output/specs/spec-hotkey-grammar-corrector/stories/2-drift-history-persistence.md`
location: n/a
reason: History growth is unbounded and unclearable — the daemon writes one row per correction forever, including the full plaintext of everything the user corrects, with no cap, no expiry, and no operation on the port that can delete any of it.
status: parked 2026-08-13
evidence: CAP-7 requires the record exist and survive restart; nothing in the epic SPEC or this story's intent decides retention. The port is save + recent only (the Never list forbids widening it here), so even a settings-level "clear history" has nothing to call. This becomes user-visible the moment the composition root starts writing on every correction, and the privacy dimension (pasted private text accumulating in a default-umask file) makes it a product decision rather than a cleanup.

### DW-105: corrections_created_at_idx does not cover recent()'s (created_at DESC, id DESC) tie-break

origin: migrated from legacy ledger (pre-DW-format entries above DW-1), 2026-08-13
source_spec: `_bmad-output/specs/spec-hotkey-grammar-corrector/stories/2-drift-history-persistence.md`
location: n/a
reason: AD-7's corrections_created_at_idx covers created_at alone, but recent() orders by (created_at DESC, id DESC), so sqlite falls back to a temp B-tree for the tie-break as the table grows — the index AD-7 exists to provide does not fully serve the only query that reads the table.
status: parked 2026-08-13
evidence: The schema test proves the index exists, never that the query uses it, and no test would notice the plan changing. Correcting it means either indexing (created_at, id) or dropping the tie-break, and AD-7's CREATE INDEX text is mandated verbatim by the spine — so this needs a spine renegotiation rather than a local edit. Low impact until history is large; recorded so the next Stack/AD-7 review sees it.

### DW-106: history.sqlite is created at the process umask, so every local account can read the plaintext

origin: migrated from legacy ledger (pre-DW-format entries above DW-1), 2026-08-13
source_spec: `_bmad-output/specs/spec-hotkey-grammar-corrector/stories/2-drift-history-persistence.md`
location: n/a
reason: history.sqlite is created at the process umask (0644 under the common 022) and holds the verbatim plaintext of everything the user ever corrects, so on a shared machine every local account can read it.
status: parked 2026-08-13
evidence: AppDatabase.file hands the path straight to drift's NativeDatabase, which creates the file with no explicit mode, and no test asserts one. This is distinct from the retention entry above, which decides how long history lives — this decides who can read it while it lives, and is fixable independently. dart:io has no chmod, so the fix is a deliberate one (create the file restricted before opening, or set the mode on the data directory in AppPaths), and it belongs with whoever wires the composition root. For a clipboard-driven corrector the stored text routinely includes private messages and credentials.

### DW-107: nothing observes that AppDatabase.file actually runs on a background isolate

origin: migrated from legacy ledger (pre-DW-format entries above DW-1), 2026-08-13
source_spec: `_bmad-output/specs/spec-hotkey-grammar-corrector/stories/2-drift-history-persistence.md`
location: n/a
reason: Nothing observes that AppDatabase.file actually runs on a background isolate — reverting NativeDatabase.createInBackground to the synchronous NativeDatabase leaves the entire suite green, so the AGENTS.md §6 / CAP-1 justification for that constructor has no regression guard.
status: parked 2026-08-13
evidence: Verified by mutation during the follow-up review: with `NativeDatabase(file)` substituted, all persistence tests and the full suite still pass. The two file-constructor tests added in this pass prove beforeOpen and the cascade reach the shipped connection, which is what could be asserted stably; isolation itself needs either a timing probe (a Timer.periodic counter that must keep ticking during a large save) or an executor-kind assertion via createBackgroundConnection, and both are judgement calls about flakiness this pass did not want to make unilaterally. Until then the regression would surface only as an intermittently janky hotkey toggle once history grows.

### DW-109: nothing exercises CorrectionController against a repository whose save fails

origin: migrated from legacy ledger (pre-DW-format entries above DW-1), 2026-08-13
source_spec: `_bmad-output/specs/spec-hotkey-grammar-corrector/stories/2-drift-history-persistence.md`
location: n/a
reason: Nothing exercises CorrectionController against a repository whose save fails, so the one mitigation this story shipped for that case — making DriftCorrectionRepository.save async, so the rejection arrives as a failed future rather than a synchronous throw — has no regression guard, and folding the validation back into a non-async save would ship a panel that never leaves CorrectionStatus.running.
status: parked 2026-08-13
evidence: Verification-gap review probed the real CorrectionController against a failing repository in both shapes: the async form leaves the controller at CorrectionStatus.completed, the synchronous-throw form strands it at CorrectionStatus.running — a spinner that never resolves after a correction that actually succeeded. Both forms pass the suite today, because test/fakes/fake_correction_repository.dart implements save as `async { saved.add(record); }` with no failure path and is the only CorrectionRepository the application layer is ever tested against. Distinct from the "port failures are unmodelled across all three controllers" entry above, which is the design decision; this is the missing test for the mitigation already implemented. Closing it means giving the fake a failing mode or a local failing double in correction_controller_test.dart's _Harness.

### DW-110: no code owns AppDatabase's lifecycle, so a daemon that forgets close() never exits

origin: migrated from legacy ledger (pre-DW-format entries above DW-1), 2026-08-13
source_spec: `_bmad-output/specs/spec-hotkey-grammar-corrector/stories/2-drift-history-persistence.md`
location: n/a
reason: No code owns AppDatabase's lifecycle — nothing in lib/ constructs or closes it, and an unclosed AppDatabase.file keeps its background isolate alive, so a daemon that forgets close() never exits on logout or `systemctl stop` until it is SIGKILLed.
status: parked 2026-08-13
evidence: Adversarial review probed it: a program that opens AppDatabase.file, runs one query and returns from main never terminates (killed at a 30 s timeout, exit 124). NativeDatabase.createInBackground — adopted in the previous review pass for the AGENTS.md §6 / CAP-1 reason — is what makes the isolate outlive main. No test can catch it because every test in the suite closes explicitly, and CorrectionController.dispose() awaits pending saves but holds no reference that could close the database. The owner is the composition root (story 4), which must await AppDatabase.close() after CorrectionController.dispose(); the constructor should also document the obligation.

### DW-111: SqliteException escapes DriftCorrectionRepository as a vendor type across the port boundary

origin: migrated from legacy ledger (pre-DW-format entries above DW-1), 2026-08-13
source_spec: `_bmad-output/specs/spec-hotkey-grammar-corrector/stories/2-drift-history-persistence.md`
location: n/a
reason: SqliteException, a package:sqlite3 type re-exported by drift, escapes DriftCorrectionRepository through the CorrectionRepository port, which AGENTS.md §6 forbids ("never let an exception cross an abstraction boundary as a vendor type") — and this story's own I/O matrix is what mandates it.
status: parked 2026-08-13
evidence: The intent-contract's rollback row reads "Transaction rolls back; the drift exception propagates to the caller", and drift_correction_repository_test.dart asserts `isA<SqliteException>()` as the contract. So the spec and the house rule disagree, and the spec won; recorded rather than patched because changing it means choosing a domain-owned failure type, which is the same contract decision the "port failures are unmodelled" entry is waiting on. Consequence while it stands: any caller wanting to distinguish disk-full from constraint-violation must import drift, and swapping the storage engine becomes a breaking change for the application layer.

### DW-112: recent() cannot distinguish a zero-suggestion correction from one whose suggestion rows were lost

origin: migrated from legacy ledger (pre-DW-format entries above DW-1), 2026-08-13
source_spec: `_bmad-output/specs/spec-hotkey-grammar-corrector/stories/2-drift-history-persistence.md`
location: n/a
reason: recent() cannot distinguish a correction legitimately saved with no suggestions from one whose suggestion rows were lost, because `suggestionsByCorrectionId[id] ?? const []` substitutes an empty list silently and the corrupt-history guards never check the completed-with-zero-suggestions shape.
status: parked 2026-08-13
evidence: Raised independently by the adversarial and edge-case lenses. _toRecord guards three corrupt shapes (unknown enum, failed/failureKind mismatch, failed-with-suggestions) but not this one, and the chunk-boundary test's own comment concedes the failure mode — "the second mutation silently returns suggestion-less records rather than failing loudly". Not patched because CorrectionRecord's asserts permit a completed record with zero suggestions, so rejecting it would invent a contract the domain does not have; AD-3 guarantees one Suggestion per register, so the right fix is likely to make that a domain invariant first. Related to, but narrower than, the already-filed entry about CorrectionCompleted carrying missing or duplicated registers, which is about the write side.

### DW-113: AppDatabase's public constructor is the unguarded door back to a synchronous NativeDatabase

origin: migrated from legacy ledger (pre-DW-format entries above DW-1), 2026-08-13
source_spec: `_bmad-output/specs/spec-hotkey-grammar-corrector/stories/2-drift-history-persistence.md`
location: n/a
reason: AppDatabase's public `AppDatabase(QueryExecutor)` constructor exists only so tests can inject NativeDatabase.memory(), but nothing enforces that — it is the unguarded door through which a synchronous NativeDatabase(file) can return to production, undoing the background-isolate fix with the suite still green.
status: parked 2026-08-13
evidence: The doc comment says "which is how tests reach an in-memory database" but the analyzer does not enforce prose. The companion entry above already records that substituting NativeDatabase(file) leaves the entire suite passing, so the regression has neither a test nor a compile-time guard. The obvious fix — @visibleForTesting — needs package:meta as a direct dependency, and this story's intent forbids adding one ("Never widen the pubspec pins or add a dependency"); meta is currently used nowhere in the codebase. So it needs either a pubspec decision or a different seam (e.g. a test-only factory in test/).

### DW-2: GlobalHotkey.bind diverges from AD-9's verbatim declaration

origin: story 3-controller-failure-contract.md, 2026-08-07
location: lib/src/domain/hotkey/global_hotkey.dart:24 vs ARCHITECTURE-SPINE.md AD-9
severity: low
reason: AD-12 requires a failed registration to resolve to a `HotkeyUnavailable` *state*, and the Consistency Conventions name it a modelled value alongside `CorrectionEvent` and `HotkeyRegistration`. `HotkeyRegistration` is a `final class` fixed verbatim by AD-9 and has no arm for "no binding exists at all" (`effective: null` already means "the backend cannot report it"), so the only shape that satisfies AD-12 without rewriting a spine-verbatim declaration is widening the return type to the new sealed `HotkeyBindOutcome` (`HotkeyBound` | `HotkeyUnavailable`). `HotkeyRegistration` and `BindingAuthority` are unchanged and still byte-identical to AD-9. Recorded rather than hand-edited into the spine, following the convention stories 1 and 2 used for AD-16's line count and the Stack table.
status: done 2026-08-14
resolution: resolved by sweep bundle dw-spine-currency-refresh
decision: 2026-08-14 Refresh the spine once — engineering call, and this entry owns it. Verified: `global_hotkey.dart:63` declares `Future<HotkeyBindOutcome> bind(HotkeyBinding binding)` where ARCHITECTURE-SPINE.md:264 fixes `Future<HotkeyRegistration>`; `HotkeyRegistration`'s declared fields are still byte-identical (spine :253-256 vs code :7-11). Five stories recorded spine drift rather than hand-editing, which was right per story and is wrong as a standing state — the spine is now stale in six places at once (this entry, DW-7, DW-24, DW-41, DW-56, DW-65, plus DW-40's envelope clause and DW-90's Stack-table pin). One `/bmad-architecture` update pass carries all of them. The spine is not SPEC.md — AGENTS.md's no-hand-edit rule names SPEC.md alone — but it is a generated artifact, so it is regenerated rather than patched. No CAP and no frozen block is touched: every divergence is a member-level addition already shipped and already justified.

- source_spec: `_bmad-output/specs/spec-hotkey-grammar-corrector/stories/3-controller-failure-contract.md`
  summary: `CorrectionController.dispose()` awaits `Future.wait(_pendingSaves)` with no bound, so a history write that never settles — as opposed to one that rejects — leaves the daemon unable to exit.
  evidence: Story 3 made a *rejecting* save survivable (`_save` catches, so `Future.wait` no longer throws) and pinned that with a test, but the await is still unbounded. A drift write on a background isolate against a locked database can hang rather than reject. The provider-cancel path is bounded (AD-19's `_killGrace`, SIGTERM then SIGKILL); this one is not, and `dispose()`'s own doc argues "a daemon that cannot exit is a worse failure than a lost history row", which the hang case contradicts. Pre-existing rather than introduced here, and picking the timeout is a policy decision (how long is a save allowed to take before shutdown abandons CAP-7's record?) that this story's intent does not carry. Related to the already-filed entry that no code owns `AppDatabase`'s lifecycle.

- source_spec: `_bmad-output/specs/spec-hotkey-grammar-corrector/stories/3-controller-failure-contract.md`
  summary: The new AD-3 register-set check counts registers but not content, so a `CorrectionCompleted` carrying all three registers with empty texts is still persisted as a completed correction that produced nothing.
  evidence: `_registerMismatch` returns null whenever the suggestions cover exactly one entry per `SuggestionRegister` value; three distinct registers whose `text` are all `''` pass, are written with `outcome: completed`, and render as three blank suggestions. Raised by the edge-case lens. Not patched because AD-3 guarantees the register *set*, not non-empty text, so rejecting it would invent a contract the domain does not have — the same reasoning that left the already-filed read-side entry ("`recent()` cannot distinguish a correction legitimately saved with no suggestions from one whose suggestion rows were lost") open. The two are the write and read halves of one contract decision and should be settled together, most likely by making non-empty text an AD-3 invariant first.

- source_spec: `_bmad-output/specs/spec-hotkey-grammar-corrector/stories/3-controller-failure-contract.md`
  summary: `PanelController.onHotkeyActivated()` has no post-dispose guard, so a direct call after shutdown still reads the visibility mirror and fires `show()`/`hide()` at a torn-down window adapter — both sibling controllers guard that transition and this one does not.
  evidence: `CorrectionController` guards with `_disposed` (`submit`, `retry`, `_setState`) and `SettingsController` with `_changes.isClosed`; `PanelController.dispose()` only cancels the activations subscription, which closes the *stream* path (covered by "a disposed controller stops responding to activations") but not the public method the panel widget calls directly. Pre-existing — the method was unguarded before this story too — and the fix is three lines plus a test, but it is a shutdown-ordering concern that belongs with whoever owns the composition root's teardown sequence, since that is where the call ordering between widget disposal and controller disposal is actually decided.

- source_spec: `_bmad-output/specs/spec-hotkey-grammar-corrector/stories/3-controller-failure-contract.md`
  summary: The new clipboard-read warning fires once per panel show with no dedupe, so on a display server that denies clipboard access a resident daemon writes one stderr warning per hotkey press for the life of the session.
  evidence: `_seedFromClipboard`'s guard logs a warning on every failure and `_beginSession()` runs on every `show()`. The failure mode it covers is systemic rather than transient — a portal permission denial or a missing selection owner does not fix itself — so the volume is bounded only by how often the user presses the hotkey. Not patched because suppressing repeats is a logging-policy decision with no spec basis (log every occurrence, log first-per-process, or log first-per-distinct-error), and the same question applies to the other guards this story added. Worth settling once, for all of them, when the composition root makes the daemon actually resident.
  status: parked 2026-08-13

- source_spec: `_bmad-output/specs/spec-hotkey-grammar-corrector/stories/3-controller-failure-contract.md`
  summary: `SettingsController` has no mutation-generation guard, so two overlapping `changeHotkey` calls resolve last-completion-wins — the superseded combination is written to config and rendered as the effective one, discarding the choice the user actually made last.
  evidence: `changeHotkey` awaits `_bind`, then writes, then `_setState`, with nothing stamping which request it belongs to. AD-11 makes a seconds-long portal dialog normal, so the window is wide: request Alt+Space (portal parks), change your mind to Meta+F1 (binds fast, lands in state and config), then the first bind resolves and overwrites both. Reproduced by two independent review lenses with a `Completer`-gated fake. `CorrectionController` solves exactly this shape with `_sessionToken`/`_isCurrent`; `SettingsController` has no equivalent, and `json_config_store.dart:38-41` names this same in-process window while guarding only the file layer. Pre-existing — verified against the story's baseline `e431b6a`, where `changeHotkey` has the identical await-then-write-then-set shape — and out of this story's scope, whose intent is the port *failure* contract, not mutation ordering. It also subsumes the single-`failure`-slot variant: while a slow bind is parked, an unrelated preset write can fail and set `configWriteFailed`, and the bind completing then clears it, reporting success for a mutation that never landed. The non-concurrent half of that (a preset switch retiring a `hotkeyBindFailed`) was patched in this pass; the concurrent half needs the generation guard.
  status: parked 2026-08-13

- source_spec: `_bmad-output/specs/spec-hotkey-grammar-corrector/stories/3-controller-failure-contract.md`
  summary: A `PanelController.dispose()` whose subscription cancel rejects now returns silently with the subscription still live, so the controller keeps toggling a torn-down window adapter — the stream-path variant of the already-filed missing `_disposed` guard.
  evidence: The already-filed entry covers the *direct-call* variant ("the public method the panel widget calls directly"). This is the other half: `dispose()`'s new AD-15 catch swallows a rejected `_activations.cancel()`, and `test/fakes/cancel_failing_stream.dart` documents that the inner subscription is deliberately left live in that case, so a later activation still reaches `onHotkeyActivated()` and fires `show()`. Confirmed by probe: one `show()` call after `dispose()`. The swallow is new in this story and correct on its own terms (shutdown must complete), but it converts a loud failed teardown into a silent live controller. `CorrectionController` is materially better here — its `_disposed` flag stops state emission — though it still issues clipboard IPC on the same path. Filed with its sibling rather than patched: both are one three-line `_disposed` guard, and the fix belongs with whoever owns the composition root's teardown ordering.
  status: parked 2026-08-13

- source_spec: `_bmad-output/specs/spec-hotkey-grammar-corrector/stories/3-controller-failure-contract.md`
  summary: `SettingsController._write` catches `on ArgumentError` to distinguish a refused value from a failed write, so any `ArgumentError` raised by a bug inside a future `ConfigStore` implementation is reported to the user as `configRejected` and swallowed instead of surfacing as the programmer error it is.
  evidence: `JsonConfigStore.write` deliberately throws `ArgumentError.value` for a cross-field-invalid config before any I/O, and the type is the only signal available — the `ConfigStore` port declares no typed rejection. So a stray `int.parse`, a bad substring, or a null-check bug in a future adapter's serializer becomes "that change was refused as invalid" and the daemon carries on, against the Consistency Conventions rule that exceptions signal programmer error only. Not patched: rethrowing would contradict AD-13 ("an unwritable config never takes the daemon down"), and the correct fix is a typed rejection on the port, which this story's Never list rules out ("no new port methods"). Root cause is a pre-existing gap in the `ConfigStore` contract, surfaced by this story having to guess at it.
  status: parked 2026-08-13

- source_spec: `_bmad-output/specs/spec-hotkey-grammar-corrector/stories/3-controller-failure-contract.md`
  summary: `CorrectionController`'s pre-existing stream-error path interpolates the raw provider error into `CorrectionFailed.message`, which CAP-13 renders inline in the panel, so a vendor exception's `toString()` reaches the user's screen.
  evidence: `correction_controller.dart` builds `'the provider stream errored: $error'`. Verified pre-existing: the same line is present at the story's baseline `e431b6a`. Its new sibling — the `correct()`-throws guard added by this story — had the identical defect and was patched in this pass to a fixed user sentence, so the two are now inconsistent within one file. Left filed rather than patched because the surviving site is not this story's change and altering the message pinned by existing stream-error tests is a behaviour edit outside the failure contract. The exposure is the panel surface only: `_failedRecord` does not persist `message`, so CAP-7 history is unaffected.
  status: parked 2026-08-13

- source_spec: `_bmad-output/specs/spec-hotkey-grammar-corrector/stories/3-controller-failure-contract.md`
  summary: `PanelController.onHotkeyActivated()` decides on the visibility port's synchronous mirror with no record of an in-flight intent, so under real window-manager latency a fast double press shows twice instead of toggling.
  evidence: AD-8 forbids awaiting on the decision path, and `_fire` correctly does not await — but the mirror only flips once the WM answers, so a second press arriving inside that window reads `isVisible == false` again and fires a second `show()`. Unreachable in the suite by construction: `FakePanelVisibility.show()` sets `_visible` synchronously before its first await point, so the fake cannot express the latency, and this story added failure seams to it but no timing seam. Raised as PLAUSIBLE rather than confirmed for that reason. Belongs with the real `PanelVisibility` adapter story, which is where both the latency and the fix (tracking intended visibility and reconciling from the `changes` stream) can actually be exercised.
  status: parked 2026-08-13

- source_spec: `_bmad-output/specs/spec-hotkey-grammar-corrector/stories/3-controller-failure-contract.md`
  summary: `SettingsController.changeHotkey` binds before it writes, so a config write that fails or is refused leaves the global shortcut already changed while the surface tells the user nothing was saved — the live binding and the persisted preference diverge silently and the change reverts at the next launch.
  evidence: `changeHotkey` awaits `_bind(binding)` and only then `_write(...)`. Probed against the current tree: with `ConfigStore.write` refusing, `bindCalls` is 1, `hotkeyBindOutcome` is `HotkeyBound(effective: Alt+Space)`, `config.hotkeyBinding` is still Ctrl+Shift+G, and the rendered failure reads "that change was refused as invalid, so nothing was saved." Each sentence is true in isolation — nothing *was* saved — but together, on one surface, they describe a shortcut that did change as one that did not. Pre-existing in shape (the same await-then-write order is at the story's baseline `e431b6a`), and this story only made it renderable by replacing the previous `rethrow` with a modelled failure. Not patched because the honest fixes are to write first and bind only on success (changing which side wins under AD-10's preference semantics) or to unbind after a failed write — a cascade over a failed port call, which this story's Never list rules out. Related to the already-filed mutation-generation race: both are `changeHotkey` needing a notion of which request owns the outcome.
  status: parked 2026-08-13

- source_spec: `_bmad-output/specs/spec-hotkey-grammar-corrector/stories/3-controller-failure-contract.md`
  summary: `SettingsController._ownWrites` is drained on both failure arms and by the echo, but never for a write that succeeds and produces no echo, so a resident daemon leaks one `AppConfig` per such write for the life of the process.
  evidence: `_write` adds `config` to the identity set before writing and removes it in the `on ArgumentError` and `on Object` arms; the success arm returns null and relies entirely on `_onConfigChanged` receiving the echo to call `_ownWrites.remove(config)`. `json_config_store.dart:75-77` resolves the write successfully but skips `_changes.add(config)` when its stream is closed, so the entry is never claimed. The set has no other drain and no bound. The leak cannot cause a wrong echo — a non-identical instance never matches — so it is growth only, which is why no behavioural test can observe it; the two failure-arm removes were both individually deletable with the suite green before this pass and remain unobservable. Raised by two lenses independently. Fixing it properly means keying the set by a write id rather than by instance identity, which is a shape change to the echo-suppression mechanism rather than a failure-contract concern.
  status: parked 2026-08-13

- source_spec: `_bmad-output/specs/spec-hotkey-grammar-corrector/stories/3-controller-failure-contract.md`
  summary: `CorrectionController._save` catches `on Object`, so an `ArgumentError` raised by a bug inside a repository implementation is reported as a lost history row and swallowed instead of surfacing as the programmer error it is — the correction-side twin of the already-filed `_write` entry, and the two are inconsistent with each other.
  evidence: `DriftCorrectionRepository.save` throws `ArgumentError` for a self-contradictory record before any I/O, exactly as `JsonConfigStore.write` does for an invalid config. `SettingsController._write` was given an `on ArgumentError` arm this story to tell a refused value from a failed write; `_save` was not, so the same class of error takes opposite paths in two controllers written to one contract. The already-filed entry argues the settings side over-catches; this is the other half — the correction side cannot tell them apart at all, and a `completed` correction whose record was refused for being malformed is logged as "the history write failed; this correction is not in history", which misdirects an operator to the disk. Both need the same resolution (a typed rejection on the ports), which this story's Never list rules out.
  status: parked 2026-08-13

- source_spec: `_bmad-output/specs/spec-hotkey-grammar-corrector/stories/3-controller-failure-contract.md`
  summary: A second full-suite concurrency flake may exist beyond the documented `single_instance_lock_test.dart` one, in `claude_agent_sdk_correction_provider_test.dart`'s "AD-19: sidecar stderr lines are forwarded to the Logger port".
  evidence: Reported by a review lens that saw it fail in 1 of 3 full-suite runs and pass 3 of 3 in isolation. It did **not** reproduce here in 9 consecutive full-suite runs on the post-patch tree, so it is filed as unconfirmed rather than established. It matters beyond its own file: a second flaky test makes any mutation-verification claim drawn from a single full-suite run unsound, since a false red reads as a killed mutation. A separate lens reported exactly that happening to it twice this pass. Every mutation claim in this pass's triage log was therefore taken from a battery run against a confirmed-green baseline, with contaminated results re-run; that discipline is worth keeping until both flakes are settled.
  status: parked 2026-08-13

### DW-8: the last six lines of `main()` are still pinned by no test

origin: story 4-composition-root-and-riverpod-wiring.md, 2026-08-07
location: lib/main.dart
severity: low
reason: Filed when the whole startup and shutdown sequence lived inline in `main()`, where nothing could execute it. That was addressed in the same story by splitting it across the ring boundary: `DaemonStartup` (infrastructure, pure `dart:io`) owns the pre-Flutter startup order, `DaemonGraph` (application/composition, Riverpod) owns the container-touching teardown, and `DaemonLifecycle` (infrastructure, no Riverpod — the container work arrives as callbacks) owns the show-request wiring and the adapter close order. All three are covered, and every ordering assertion was mutation-verified by transposing adjacent steps in the source: all six adjacent swaps in `DaemonLifecycle.shutdown()`, the correction/panel swap in `DaemonGraph.disposeControllers()`, moving the config load ahead of the lock in `DaemonStartup.begin()`, and dropping the `await` on either controller-teardown step — nine mutations, nine failures.
  What is left in `main()` is the part that genuinely needs a platform and therefore still has no test: `WidgetsFlutterBinding.ensureInitialized()`, the `exit(0)` on a non-daemon launch, `_container()`'s override list, `_createHiddenWindow()`, `runApp`, and `_installSignalHandlers`. Two of those are worth naming. The override list is the only place a seam could be forgotten — a missing one throws at startup by construction (AD-17), but which seams exist is unasserted. And the signal handlers are the only trigger for `DaemonLifecycle.shutdown()`, so a daemon that lost them would exit without the ordered teardown and nothing would notice (see DW-12, which is the same surface from the other side). Closing the rest means an end-to-end launch harness, which needs a display this container does not have (DW-9).
status: done 2026-08-14
decision: 2026-08-14 Close — already resolved for the part that mattered — engineering call. The entry named two residues worth naming: the override list, where a seam could be silently forgotten, and the signal handlers, the only trigger for the ordered teardown. Both are now pinned by source-scan rows this entry predates.
resolution: already resolved: `test/architecture/composition_wiring_test.dart:20` asserts every port seam declared in `port_providers.dart` is overridden in `main.dart`, and `:40` asserts the converse — so the override list is checked in both directions, which is exactly the gap this entry called unasserted. `:657` pins the signal handlers as the only path to the ordered teardown, `:513` the `exit(0)` on a non-daemon launch, and `:534` that every step after the address is held sits inside the abort guard. `port_providers.dart` declares ten seams (:40-95) and `main.dart:388-405` overrides ten. What genuinely remains untested in `main()` is `WidgetsFlutterBinding.ensureInitialized()`, `_createHiddenWindow()` and `runApp` — all three need a display, and they are itemised on DW-9's runtime checklist.

### DW-9: AD-8's "no visible window" was not observed at runtime — no X display is reachable in this container

origin: story 4-composition-root-and-riverpod-wiring.md, 2026-08-07
location: linux/runner/my_application.cc, lib/main.dart, test/architecture/hidden_window_test.dart
severity: medium
reason: The story's Verification anticipated that no window *enumerator* exists here (`xwininfo`, `xdotool`, `wmctrl`, `xprop` are all absent) and fell back to a launch-sanity check against `DISPLAY=:10`. That fallback could not run either: `:10` answers with "Authorization required, but no authorization protocol specified" and there is no `~/.Xauthority`, no `XAUTHORITY`, and no `Xvfb`/`xvfb-run` to stand up a display of our own; every other socket under `/tmp/.X11-unix` is dead ("cannot open display"). So the built binary was never launched, and **none** of the runtime claims were observed: not "no window is mapped", not "the process stays resident", not "a second launch exits 0". What *is* proven: `flutter build linux --debug` succeeds with the edited runner and `main.dart` linked in, and `test/architecture/hidden_window_test.dart` pins both edits statically (the runner shows no toplevel and has no first-frame handler; nothing under `lib/` calls `windowManager.show()`). The runtime observation is owed and should be made on a machine with a real session before the daemon is called done.
status: done 2026-08-14
resolution: resolved by sweep bundle dw-runtime-observation-checklists
decision: 2026-08-14 This entry owns the runtime checklist — engineering call, extending the procedure the 2026-08-14 answer on DW-88 created rather than inventing a second one. Verified still true: `main.dart:419-424` sets only `setTitle` and `setSkipTaskbar` and can map nothing, `linux/runner/my_application.cc:71` realizes the view with no toplevel shown, and the file contains no `first_frame` handler at all. One committed checklist beside `test/platform/` enumerates every claim this container cannot observe, with an expected result per step: no toplevel mapped at startup, the process stays resident, a second launch exits 0 (this entry); the panel becomes visible and focused and hides on focus loss (DW-26); a real keybinder grab, a CAP-1 summon inside 100 ms and a CAP-12 live rebind (DW-44); the window-event echo ordering end to end into Dart (DW-25); the chosen panel size and first-run position (DW-50); how the settings affordance actually lands over the editor (DW-72); and the FFI registrar's GTK-main-thread marshalling (DW-39). Building a headless harness was rejected: three stories have now tried, and this container has no compositor, no xdg-desktop-portal, no session bus and no reachable X display.

### DW-10: the composition root installs the ProviderScope over a hand-built container, not `ProviderScope(overrides:)`

origin: story 4-composition-root-and-riverpod-wiring.md, 2026-08-07
location: lib/main.dart
severity: low
reason: The story's Tasks line spells the last step as `runApp(ProviderScope(overrides: …, child: DaemonApp()))`. What shipped is `ProviderContainer(overrides: …)` installed through `UncontrolledProviderScope`, because two of the story's own Acceptance Criteria need `main` to hold the container: it must read the three controllers eagerly (a controller is a subscription — building `CorrectionController` lazily on first read would mean the first `show()` is the event that creates its listener, and so the one that is missed), and it must dispose them in a specified order before disposing the container. `ProviderScope` owns its container privately and exposes it only through a `BuildContext`, which would push the wiring into the UI ring. The override list, the seams and the AD-17 shape are otherwise exactly as specified. Recorded so the divergence from the literal task text is a decision on the record rather than a silent substitution.
status: done 2026-08-14
resolution: skipped by the 2026-08-14 sweep triage: A record of a deliberate substitution, not a defect. main.dart:388-405 still builds `ProviderContainer(overrides:)` installed through `UncontrolledProviderScope` (main.dart:166) rather than `ProviderScope(overrides:)`, because two of story 4's own acceptance criteria require main to hold the container -- eager controller reads so the first show is not the event that creates the listener, and disposal in a specified order. The code is correct as shipped and the divergence from the literal task text is now on the record; nothing is buildable.

### DW-11: the placeholder ports reject every call, so a resident daemon logs on a schedule set by the user

origin: story 4-composition-root-and-riverpod-wiring.md, 2026-08-07
location: lib/src/infrastructure/{panel/unimplemented_panel_visibility,clipboard/unimplemented_clipboard,tray/unimplemented_tray}.dart
severity: low
reason: The three placeholders report their unimplemented state honestly by rejecting rather than no-op'ing, which is what keeps AGENTS.md §8 satisfied and routes each failure through the guards the controllers already have. The cost is volume: `UnimplementedTray.setHotkeyUnavailable` produces one warning per startup (bounded, fine), but `UnimplementedPanelVisibility.show()` and `UnimplementedClipboard.readText()` produce one line per attempt for the life of the process. Today only a second launch's show request can trigger the first, and nothing triggers the second (no visibility change can occur, so no session begins), so the volume is zero in practice — but this makes the already-filed "the new clipboard-read warning fires once per panel show with no dedupe" entry newly reachable the moment story 5 lands a real `PanelVisibility` while the clipboard is still a placeholder. The two should be settled together, and both disappear when stories 5 and 6 replace these files.
status: done 2026-08-08
resolution: Closed by story 6, which deletes the last of the three. Story 5 replaced `unimplemented_panel_visibility.dart` and `unimplemented_clipboard.dart`; this story deletes `lib/src/infrastructure/tray/unimplemented_tray.dart` and its test, and binds `trayProvider` to `TrayManagerTray` instead. No placeholder port remains under `lib/`, so the rejection volume this entry measured is gone at its source: the real adapter accepts `install()` and `setHotkeyUnavailable()` and pushes them at the indicator. What it does **not** close is the sibling entry it names — "the new clipboard-read warning fires once per panel show with no dedupe" — which is a logging-policy question about the real adapters and stays open on its own terms.

### DW-12: shutdown runs only on SIGINT/SIGTERM; no other exit path reaches the ordered teardown

origin: story 4-composition-root-and-riverpod-wiring.md, 2026-08-07
location: lib/main.dart (`_installSignalHandlers`)
severity: low
reason: `_Daemon.shutdown()` is the only place the eight teardown steps run, and the only thing that calls it is a `ProcessSignal.sigint`/`sigterm` watch — which covers `systemctl stop`, a logout, and Ctrl-C, the ways a tray daemon is actually stopped. It does not cover a window-manager close of the (currently never-mapped) window, nor `windowManager`'s own close path, both of which become reachable once story 5 maps the window and story 9 puts a panel in it; there is no `setPreventClose` and no `WindowListener`. The consequence would be an exit that skips `AppDatabase.close()` and abandons a history write in flight (CAP-7). Filed rather than pre-solved because the right hook is a `window_manager` concern and belongs with whoever lands that adapter.
status: done 2026-08-14
resolution: resolved by sweep bundle dw-single-exit-path-tray-quit
decision: 2026-08-14 Close is a hide, not an exit — engineering call. Verified: `main.dart:446-450` watches SIGINT, SIGTERM and SIGHUP (the entry predates the SIGHUP addition) and nothing else reaches `DaemonLifecycle.shutdown()`; there is no `setPreventClose` and no `WindowListener`, and `window_manager_panel_visibility.dart:174-189` reconciles the mirror on a `close` while explicitly declining to run any teardown. The fix is not to route a window close into shutdown — a user pressing the close button on a tray daemon's panel means "put this away", not "stop the daemon", which is the same intent CAP-14 already gives focus loss. So `setPreventClose(true)` plus a close listener that calls `PanelVisibility.hide()`: the toplevel is never destroyed, no CAP-7 write is abandoned because nothing exits, and the daemon keeps exactly two exit routes — a signal and DW-114's Quit — both reaching the ordered teardown. This also retires the caveat in the `close` arm's doc, since the destroyed-toplevel case stops arising. Lands with DW-114 and DW-31.

### DW-14: a second launch cannot avoid creating a GTK window — the embedder builds it before Dart runs

origin: story 4-composition-root-and-riverpod-wiring.md, 2026-08-07
location: linux/runner/my_application.cc (`my_application_activate`), lib/main.dart
severity: low
reason: The story's I/O matrix says a second launch "exits 0 without building a graph, a window, or a database". Two of the three hold exactly: `main()` acquires the AD-14 lock before it loads config, opens `AppDatabase`, resolves the active pair, builds the `ProviderContainer` or touches `windowManager`, and it now calls `exit(0)` rather than returning (returning from `main` does **not** end a Flutter process — the embedder's GTK loop is already running, which would have left every second launch resident forever; found and fixed in this pass). The window is the exception and is not `main.dart`'s to control: `my_application_activate` constructs the `GtkWindow` and the `FlView` and realizes them *before* the Dart entrypoint is invoked, so a second launch has a realized — never mapped, never shown — toplevel for the few milliseconds between engine start and `exit(0)`. Nothing is displayed and nothing is leaked, but the matrix row is literally true only of the Dart-side window. Avoiding even that means moving the singleton check into the C++ runner (a `G_APPLICATION_HANDLES_COMMAND_LINE` / GApplication-uniqueness design), which is a different mechanism from AD-14's socket and a deliberate choice someone should make knowingly.
status: done 2026-08-14
decision: 2026-08-14 Close -- nothing is displayed -- the second launch's toplevel is realized but never mapped, so no user sees it, and AD-14's socket already stops the second instance before config, database or windowManager. Two instance authorities is a worse state than one invisible toplevel.
resolution: closed by human decision 2026-08-14: the realized-but-never-mapped toplevel at linux/runner/my_application.cc:20-21 is accepted. AD-14's socket lock, acquired in main() before any resource, remains the single instance authority; no GApplication uniqueness is added.

### DW-15: Dart refuses a second unix `ServerSocket.bind` per process, and then connects the caller to the wrong socket

origin: story 4-composition-root-and-riverpod-wiring.md, 2026-08-07
location: lib/src/infrastructure/system/single_instance_lock.dart:102-114
severity: low
reason: Found while writing `daemon_startup_test.dart`. Binding two *different* abstract-namespace addresses from one Dart process fails on the second with `OS Error: The shared flag to bind() needs to be `true` if binding multiple times on the same path` — despite the paths differing — and `Socket.connect` to the second address then *succeeds*, landing on the socket bound at the first. `SingleInstanceLock` cannot tell that apart from a genuine holder, so it reports `alreadyRunning` for an address nobody holds. Probed directly with raw `ServerSocket`/`Socket` to confirm it is the SDK and not the lock. Unreachable in the daemon, which binds exactly one address for its whole life, so this is a test-authoring hazard rather than a defect: any suite that wants two live locks in one isolate gets a false `alreadyRunning`, and the tests added here work around it by keeping one startup live at a time (noted in the source). The lock's own comment already anticipates the same-path case — "a rebind from within this process and one from a second process fail with different Dart-level messages, and the recovery is the same either way" — but not the different-path one, where the recovery is *not* the same. Worth revisiting if the daemon ever needs a second socket, and worth `shared: true` or an errno branch then.
  Consequence for the gate: `dart test` and `flutter test` both run suites as isolates inside one process, so `single_instance_lock_test.dart` and `daemon_startup_test.dart` cannot overlap — they failed together and passed under `--concurrency=1`. A `dart_test.yaml` pinning `concurrency: 1` was added, which both runners honour and which is the only setting that expresses this (there is no per-tag concurrency). It costs wall time — the binding-free split went from about 10 s to 17 s, the full `flutter test` to 33 s — and it also retires the two full-suite concurrency flakes already filed against this ledger. Revisit if the suite grows enough for the serialisation to hurt, and note that the alternative is putting every socket-binding test in one file.
  seen-again: 2026-08-10, story 9 — the retirement claim in the sentence above is false as written; both suites still flake under `concurrency: 1`. See DW-46 for the runs that show it and for what the fix actually needs to be.
status: done 2026-08-14
resolution: skipped by the 2026-08-14 sweep triage: A Dart SDK hazard for test authors, not a defect in this code. single_instance_lock.dart:103 binds exactly one address for the process's whole life, so the different-path rebind case is unreachable in the daemon, and the catch at :109-113 already documents why it deliberately does not branch on errno. The workaround is recorded in the lock's source and in the suites that need two live locks; DW-46 carries the correction to this entry's false claim that it retired the full-suite flakes.

### DW-16: the active (provider, preset) pair is fixed at startup, but `changeActivePreset` promises it serves the next correction

origin: story 4-composition-root-and-riverpod-wiring.md review pass, 2026-08-07
location: lib/main.dart (`_container`), lib/src/application/settings_controller.dart
severity: medium
reason: `activeCorrectionProviderProvider` and `activePresetProvider` are bound with `overrideWithValue` from `startup.active` and never rebuilt, so `CorrectionController` holds one pair for the life of the process. `SettingsController.changeActivePreset` is live and its doc says it "[s]witches which preset — and so which provider — serves the next correction (CAP-8)". After this wiring a user switches presets, the config file is written, the settings surface confirms it, and every subsequent correction still runs the old model with the old prompt until the daemon restarts. Nothing logs it and no test notices, because `composition_root_test.dart` injects the pair directly. AD-5 does say the composition root resolves the pair, so startup-time resolution is what the story's intent asks for — the gap is CAP-8's immediacy, which is the same shape as the already-filed "a config file edited by hand never reaches the resident daemon" entry. Settling it means either deriving the pair from `configStoreProvider` so a config change rebuilds `CorrectionController`, or documenting a restart requirement on `changeActivePreset` and in the settings UI (story 10). Raised independently by the adversarial lens.
status: done 2026-09-26
resolution: Plan 02-01 updates the active provider/preset pair after a committed Settings change in `lib/src/application/composition/daemon_graph.dart` and captures the pair for each run in `lib/src/application/correction_controller.dart`. The next correction uses the new pair while an in-flight run keeps its original pair. This implements the requirement with a stable controller rather than the earlier decision's provider rebuild; plan 02-08 pins the stable panel binding. Evidence is source inspection and existing tests, not a live daemon observation.
decision: 2026-08-14 Make the active pair live -- activePresetProvider and activeCorrectionProviderProvider are derived from configStoreProvider so a config change rebuilds correctionControllerProvider. Lands together with DW-62 (ref.listen re-bind) and removes preset_choice_list.dart's restart notice, closing DW-70's remaining half.

### DW-17: AD-12's settings half is unwired — the startup bind outcome is discarded

origin: story 4-composition-root-and-riverpod-wiring.md review pass, 2026-08-07
location: lib/main.dart (`_finishStartup`), lib/src/application/composition/controller_providers.dart
severity: medium
reason: `startup.bindHotkey(tray: tray)` returns a `HotkeyBindOutcome` and `_finishStartup` throws it away. `SettingsController` accepts an `initialBindOutcome`, and `settingsControllerProvider` passes none — the parameter is used by one test and nothing in `lib/`. AD-12 names two consumers of the unavailable state, the tray and the settings screen; the tray is told and the settings surface renders `hotkeyBindOutcome: null` for the life of the daemon unless the user happens to change the hotkey. Not user-visible yet (story 10 builds the settings screen) and the story's own I/O matrix row names only the tray, which is why this is filed rather than patched — but the seam is here, and the fix needs an ordering decision: the graph is built before `bindHotkey` runs, so either the controller is constructed after the bind, or `SettingsController` gains an `applyStartupOutcome`. The outcome is no longer discarded; the unused `initialBindOutcome` constructor seam is now a documented deliberate absence at lib/src/application/settings_controller.dart:45-46.

### DW-18: the tray is never installed, while four user-facing strings promise "the tray menu still opens the panel"

origin: story 4-composition-root-and-riverpod-wiring.md review pass, 2026-08-07
location: lib/src/infrastructure/hotkey/{x11,wayland_portal}_global_hotkey.dart, lib/src/infrastructure/system/daemon_startup.dart, lib/src/application/settings_controller.dart
severity: medium
reason: `TrayPort.install()` has no caller anywhere in `lib/`, and nothing subscribes to `panelRequests`. Meanwhile both stub hotkey adapters, `DaemonStartup.requestBinding` and `SettingsController._bind` all tell the user that the hotkey is inactive but "the tray menu still opens the panel". Combined with both stubs returning `HotkeyUnavailable` unconditionally, the only reachable route to `PanelController.showPanel()` in this build is a second launch's socket request — so the sentence names an affordance that does not exist. `UnimplementedTray.install()`'s own doc says "the composition root logs each rejection", and since it is never called that rejection is silent too. The wording is AD-12's designed degradation and becomes true when story 6 lands the tray; filing rather than rewording so the two are settled together, since rewording now means rewording back. Raised independently by the adversarial and edge-case lenses.
status: done 2026-08-08
resolution: Closed by story 6, on this entry's own terms — the four strings are untouched, and they are true whenever `install()` succeeds, which needs a host with a StatusNotifier/AppIndicator implementation; where it does not, the failure is one logged line naming the tray as unavailable rather than a sentence that quietly misleads. `TrayManagerTray` implements `TrayPort` over a `TrayIcon` seam confined to `tray_manager_tray_icon.dart`; `main.dart` awaits a guarded `tray.install()` after `lifecycle.start()` and before `startup.bindHotkey(...)`, and joins `tray.panelRequests` to `DaemonLifecycle`'s existing `onShowRequest` handler, which is `PanelController.showPanel()` and so reaches `PanelVisibility.show()` (AD-8). So the tray menu now genuinely opens the panel, and it is a second route rather than the socket one: `test/infrastructure/system/daemon_lifecycle_test.dart` pins the delivery, the fired-not-awaited rule, the AD-15 stream-error backstop naming the tray, and the post-shutdown silence. AD-12's visible degradation is on both tray surfaces — the icon swaps to `assets/tray/hotkey-grammar-corrector-hotkey-unavailable.png` and a disabled statement line is appended, while the open-panel entry stays enabled — and `test/architecture/composition_wiring_test.dart` pins the wiring and the install ordering by source index. **Not** observed here: that an indicator ever appears. This container has no StatusNotifier host, no compositor and no reachable X display, so `test/platform/tray_live_test.dart` is an unconditionally skipped test with a `fail()` body naming what is owed on a real session — see DW-9/DW-26 for the same gap on the window. The `install()` rejection this entry calls silent is now one logged error and a daemon that still starts.

### DW-19: show requests arriving between `acquire()` and `DaemonLifecycle.start()` are dropped

origin: story 4-composition-root-and-riverpod-wiring.md review pass, 2026-08-07
location: lib/src/infrastructure/system/single_instance_lock.dart, lib/main.dart
severity: medium
reason: `SingleInstanceLock.acquire()` binds and starts serving immediately, and `_onConnection` adds to a broadcast `StreamController` with no buffer. Its only subscriber is `DaemonLifecycle.start()`, which `main` does not reach until after the config load, `AppDatabase.file`, `ActiveCorrection.resolve` and `graph.build()`. A second launch inside that window completes the handshake cleanly, logs that it asked the holder to show its panel, and exits 0 — while the holder raises nothing. Reachable by a double-click on a launcher. The narrow fix is a buffer (a non-broadcast controller, or a `pendingShowRequest` flag `start()` drains), which is an edit to story 1's lock rather than to the composition, and is why this is filed rather than patched. Raised by the edge-case lens.
  update_2026-08-08: Story 5 widened this window, deliberately. `lifecycle.start()` moved from immediately after the lifecycle is constructed to after `runApp`, because that story is what puts a real mapping adapter behind the show request: a request served before `runApp` would map an untitled, taskbar-listed, widget-tree-less toplevel. So the drop window now also covers `_createHiddenWindow()` and `runApp`. The trade was taken because a dropped request raises nothing and self-corrects on the next press, while an empty window on screen does neither, and it is stated at the call site in `main.dart`. The buffer is still the fix and still belongs to the lock, so this entry stays open and now owns both halves.
status: done 2026-08-14
resolution: resolved by sweep bundle dw-daemon-startup-and-abort-path
decision: 2026-08-14 Buffer in the lock — engineering call, taking the fix this entry names against its own file. Verified: `single_instance_lock.dart:79-80` is a broadcast `StreamController` with no buffer and `:105` starts serving the moment the address is bound, while `main.dart:188` reaches `lifecycle.start()` only after `_createHiddenWindow()` and `runApp`. `SingleInstanceLock` holds a pending-show flag that the first subscriber to `showRequests` drains, so a launcher double-click landing anywhere in startup raises the panel instead of raising nothing. Widening the window in story 5 was the right trade and is not reopened — an empty untitled toplevel is worse than a dropped request — but the drop itself was never the acceptable half, and it belongs to the lock rather than to the composition.

### DW-20: `DaemonLifecycle._step` guards a teardown step that throws, not one that hangs

origin: story 4-composition-root-and-riverpod-wiring.md review pass, 2026-08-07
location: lib/src/infrastructure/system/daemon_lifecycle.dart
severity: medium
reason: `_step` wraps `await run()` in try/catch, which does nothing for a step that never completes — and two of them can: `_disposeControllers` reaches `CorrectionController.dispose()`, which awaits `Future.wait(_pendingSaves)` with no bound (already filed), and `_closeDatabase` awaits drift's background isolate. Both signal handlers' `exit(0)` and `_abort`'s `exit(1)` now sit behind that single un-timed future, so one wedged adapter yields a daemon that neither serves nor exits while still holding the AD-14 address. The unbounded wait is filed separately; what is new here is that every exit path in the process is now gated on it. Choosing a per-step timeout is a policy decision (how long is a history write allowed to take before it is abandoned) rather than a mechanical fix. Raised by the edge-case lens.
status: done 2026-08-14
resolution: resolved by sweep bundle dw-bounded-teardown-and-window-timeouts
decision: 2026-08-14 Bounded per-step timeout -- engineering call. DaemonLifecycle._step gets a documented policy timeout, logs the step that exceeded it by name, continues the remaining teardown steps, and always reaches exit(0)/exit(1). The code's own doc already argues a daemon that cannot exit is the worse failure than a lost history row.

### DW-21: `runApp` and framework errors bypass `_abort`, which claims to cover them

origin: story 4-composition-root-and-riverpod-wiring.md review pass, 2026-08-07
location: lib/main.dart (`_abort`, `main`)
severity: low
reason: `_abort`'s doc says it covers "a window or a widget tree that will not come up". `runApp` does not throw for a failing tree: build and framework errors go to `FlutterError.onError`, and uncaught async errors to `PlatformDispatcher.instance.onError`. Neither is installed, so the guard only ever sees a rejection from `_createHiddenWindow` or `bindHotkey` — the widget-tree half of the promise is unwired. Not simply patched, because installing a handler that calls `exit(1)` makes any single failing build kill a resident daemon, which is arguably worse than the gap; the deliberate choice is which framework failures are fatal to a daemon and which are logged. Until then the doc comment overstates what the guard covers. Raised by the edge-case lens.
status: done 2026-08-14
resolution: resolved by sweep bundle dw-daemon-startup-and-abort-path
decision: 2026-08-14 Install handlers that log, never exit -- engineering call. FlutterError.onError and PlatformDispatcher.instance.onError route framework and uncaught async errors to the Logger port (error type only, per the Logger contract) without exiting; `_abort`'s doc narrows to what it actually guards. A single failing build must not kill an all-day resident daemon.

### DW-22: removing `first_frame_cb` also removed the runner's only first-frame signal, and nothing replaced it

origin: story 4-composition-root-and-riverpod-wiring.md review pass, 2026-08-07
location: linux/runner/my_application.cc, lib/main.dart (`_finishStartup`)
severity: low
reason: AD-8 required that the stock handler stop showing the toplevel, and deleting it does that. The side effect is that the runner no longer consumes the `"first-frame"` signal at all, so nothing in the process observes that the engine produced a frame. `_finishStartup` returns — and `startup.bindHotkey` runs, and CAP-1's "the window is warm before the first toggle" claim is made — with no evidence behind the warmth. `gtk_widget_realize(GTK_WIDGET(view))` is still there and is what the claim rests on, which is weaker than a rendered frame. Keeping a first-frame handler that records readiness without showing anything, or awaiting `WidgetsBinding.instance.endOfFrame`, would restore the signal. Distinct from DW-9, which is about the runtime observation never being made at all. Raised by the edge-case lens at medium confidence.
status: done 2026-08-14
resolution: resolved by sweep bundle dw-daemon-startup-and-abort-path
decision: 2026-08-14 Await the first frame in Dart — engineering call, taking the option that needs no runner edit. Verified: `linux/runner/my_application.cc` contains no `first_frame` handler and no `first-frame` signal anywhere, and `:71`'s `gtk_widget_realize(GTK_WIDGET(view))` is the whole of what CAP-1's warmth currently rests on; `_finishStartup` awaits no frame. `main.dart` awaits `WidgetsBinding.instance.endOfFrame` after `runApp` and before `lifecycle.start()`, so "the window is warm before the first toggle" rests on a rendered frame. Restoring a C first-frame handler that records readiness without showing was rejected: that handler is precisely what AD-8 exists to have deleted, and a version that records is one line away from a version that shows.

### DW-23: `ConfigLoadResult.warning` interpolates an exception's `toString()` into a line the Logger now emits

origin: story 4-composition-root-and-riverpod-wiring.md review pass, 2026-08-07
location: lib/src/infrastructure/config/json_config_store.dart, lib/src/infrastructure/system/daemon_startup.dart
severity: low
reason: `DaemonStartup.begin` forwards `ConfigLoadResult.warning` straight to `logger.warning`, and that message is composed with an error interpolated in — `'could not be read ($error)'`, and the same shape in `_seed`. The `Logger` port's own doc bans exactly that ("never log an error's `toString()`"), and every other guard added in this story instead passes `context: {'error_type': ...}`. This diff is what first routes those strings to the `Logger`, so the convention is broken by the composition root on its first startup path. The payloads are filesystem exceptions today, which is why it is low rather than a privacy defect — the risk is the idiom being copied to a path where the exception carries user text. Fixing it means `ConfigLoadResult` carrying the error type separately from the message, which is an edit to story 1's contract. Raised by the adversarial lens.
status: done 2026-09-26
resolution: Plan 02-04 separates detailed `ConfigLoadResult.warning` from the generic daemon log sentence in `json_config_store.dart` and `daemon_startup.dart`, logging only a safe error type; plan 02-15 removes the remaining caught-error and stderr interpolation in the Claude provider. Source inspection and the existing suite support the closure; no new logging gate or runtime observation was added.
decision: 2026-08-14 Split the error type off the message — engineering call. Verified: `json_config_store.dart:144` returns `_defaultsWithWarning('could not be read ($error)')` and `_seed` does the same at `:169-170`; `daemon_startup.dart:96-98` forwards that string straight to `logger.warning`, and the `Logger` port's own doc bans it in terms (`logger.dart:7-14`, "never log an error's `toString()`"). `ConfigLoadResult` gains an error-type field beside its message, the message names the file and the problem without the exception, and `DaemonStartup` passes `context: {'error_type': ...}` like every other guard added in the same story. The payloads are filesystem exceptions today, so this is fixing the idiom before it is copied onto a path where the exception carries the user's text — which is the only reason it is worth an edit to story 1's contract.

reason: The intent names `main.dart` as the actor seven times and AD-17 names `application/composition/` as the composition root; the startup order, the teardown order and the AD-5 pair resolution live in three other types, two of them in infrastructure. The reason is sound and is the reason the ordering assertions exist at all — `main()` is reachable by no test, so anything left inside it is unverifiable — and AD-1 is not violated on any reading (`ad1_import_rule_test.dart` is untouched and still green). But `DaemonLifecycle` taking `disposeControllers`/`disposeGraph`/`showPanel` as bare callbacks is a dependency inversion with exactly one implementation behind it, existing to satisfy the import gate rather than to admit a second implementation, and `port_providers.dart`'s doc already has to concede that its file "is not the only file that *names*" an adapter. DW-10 records the `ProviderScope` substitution and DW-8 the untested residue; this records the structural question those two circle, which is where a reader should expect to find "the composition root". A spine currency question rather than a defect. Raised by the adversarial lens and by the intent-alignment audit as its dominant axis.
status: done 2026-08-14
resolution: resolved by sweep bundle dw-spine-currency-refresh
decision: 2026-08-14 Record, do not restructure — engineering call, carried by DW-2's spine refresh. Verified: the composition genuinely spans `main.dart`, `application/composition/{daemon_graph,port_providers,controller_providers}.dart` and `infrastructure/system/{daemon_startup,daemon_lifecycle}.dart` plus `infrastructure/correction/active_correction.dart`. The split is what makes the ordering assertions possible at all — `main()` is reachable by no test, and `composition_wiring_test.dart` now carries 26 rows over what was moved out of it — and AD-1 is not violated on any reading. What is wrong is the spine's one-line claim that the composition root *is* `application/composition/`; the refresh states the four-part shape and names where a reader should expect to find each half. `DaemonLifecycle`'s bare callbacks stay: they are what keep the import gate honest, and inverting them into a second interface would be an abstraction with one implementation, which AGENTS.md §4.2 forbids.

### DW-25: the `_outstanding` guard rests on an echo-ordering assumption no test here can check

origin: story 5-clipboard-and-panel-visibility-adapters.md, 2026-08-07
location: lib/src/infrastructure/panel/window_manager_panel_visibility.dart, test/fakes/fake_panel_window.dart, test/platform/panel_visibility_live_test.dart
severity: low
reason: The adapter ignores a `show`/`hide`/`minimize`/`restore` window event while a request of its own is outstanding, because such an event is its own echo and the mirror already reported the transition. That is sound only if the echo arrives *while* the request is still in flight, rather than after its method-channel reply resolves. If the real Linux plugin reverses it, the echo of a superseded `show` lands with `_outstanding == 0`, is believed, and `changes` emits a spurious `true`; under AD-18 that is a cleared editor and a fresh clipboard read against a panel on its way out.
update_2026-08-08: Downgraded from medium after the review pass read the plugin source this entry should have read when it was filed. The assumption is **not** asserted only by `FakePanelWindow`: `on_window_show` and `on_window_hide` are GTK signal handlers that call `_emit_event` synchronously (`window_manager-0.5.2/linux/window_manager_plugin.cc:959`, `:987`, `:993`), and they fire inside the `gtk_widget_show`/`gtk_widget_hide` that the method-call handler itself invokes — so `fl_method_channel_invoke_method` for `onEvent` is called before that handler returns its `FlMethodResponse`, on the same channel. The fake matches the plugin rather than merely matching the adapter. What remains unchecked is only the last link — that the engine preserves that ordering end to end into Dart — which is a one-line observation on a real session rather than a design risk. Confirm alongside DW-9; do not re-file as uncheckable.
status: done 2026-08-14
resolution: resolved by sweep bundle dw-runtime-observation-checklists
decision: 2026-08-14 One line on DW-9's checklist — engineering call. Verified: the guard is `window_manager_panel_visibility.dart:210-213`, unchanged. The entry's own 2026-08-08 update already retired the design risk by reading the plugin source — the GTK signal handlers call `_emit_event` synchronously from inside the very `gtk_widget_show`/`gtk_widget_hide` the method-call handler invokes — so what is left is one link, whether the engine preserves that ordering end to end into Dart. That is an observation, not a decision, and it is owed on a real session alongside DW-9. Do not re-file it as uncheckable.

### DW-27: the focus-loss hide fires on any blur, including one caused by a dialog the panel itself opened

origin: story 5-clipboard-and-panel-visibility-adapters.md, 2026-08-07
location: lib/src/infrastructure/panel/window_manager_panel_visibility.dart (`_onBlur`)
severity: low
reason: CAP-14 says the panel hides when it loses focus, and the adapter implements exactly that: a `blur` event with the mirror true and nothing outstanding hides the panel. `window_manager` reports one `blur` for every loss of keyboard focus, and does not distinguish "the user clicked another application" from "a modal, a tooltip window, or a portal dialog took focus". The panel has no such child window today (story 9 builds the widgets and story 10 the settings surface), so the case is unreachable — but the moment either opens anything focusable, the panel underneath it will dismiss itself. Not pre-solved because the honest fix needs a signal the port does not carry (which window took focus), and inventing one is a second domain edit this story was explicitly not authorised to make.
