### DW-28: the visibility adapter's request queue is unbounded and never drained on dispose

origin: story 5-clipboard-and-panel-visibility-adapters.md, 2026-08-07
location: lib/src/infrastructure/panel/window_manager_panel_visibility.dart (`_enqueue`, `dispose`)
severity: low
reason: `_enqueue` chains every request onto `_queue`, and `_apply` returns immediately for a request the mirror has superseded — so a burst of presses costs one microtask each and nothing accumulates in practice. What is unhandled is a window call that never *settles*, as opposed to one that rejects: it parks the whole chain behind it forever, so every later press resolves to nothing and the panel stops responding until restart. That is the same unbounded-await shape already filed against `CorrectionController.dispose()` and `DaemonLifecycle._step` (DW-20), and picking a per-request timeout is the same policy decision DW-20 is waiting on. Separately, `dispose()` does not await the tail of the queue — it flips `_disposed`, and every queued or resuming `_apply` then returns early — so a request in flight at teardown stops where it is rather than running to completion. That is the intended behaviour (the review pass confirmed by probe that the earlier shape let a mapping `focus()` run *after* disposal, and the mid-flight re-check added in response is what stops it), but it means the future a caller holds resolves without the window having been moved, and nothing tells the caller which happened. Right for a daemon that must exit; recorded because it was not chosen against a stated policy.
status: done 2026-08-14
resolution: resolved by sweep bundle dw-bounded-teardown-and-window-timeouts
decision: 2026-08-14 Timeout plus a stated contract -- engineering call, taking DW-20's policy timeout rather than inventing a second one. Each queued window call is bounded, the request that exceeds it is logged, and PanelVisibility.show/hide state what a resolved future means for a superseded or disposal-abandoned request.

### DW-29: `test/platform/` runs only under `flutter test` and is in no other gate

location: test/platform/, dart_test.yaml
severity: low
reason: The two channel-level suites this story adds are the only proof of the routing and call-sequence assumptions the binding-free tests take on trust, and they live outside the `dart test` command the spec's verification uses (`test/application test/architecture test/domain test/infrastructure test/fakes_smoke_test.dart`). They are picked up by `flutter test`, which is also in the gate, so nothing is unrun today — but the directory is discovered by convention rather than named anywhere, exactly like `test/composition/`, and the repo still has no CI to make either mandatory (already filed against story 2). If someone verifies with `dart test` alone, the only tests that touch a real `MethodChannel` silently do not run.
status: open
decision: 2026-08-14 Add a `flutter test` job to CI — engineering call, and this entry is now sharper than when it was filed. Verified: `.github/workflows/ci.yml` exists and runs `dart analyze --fatal-infos`, `tool/provision_sidecar.sh` and the scoped binding-free `dart test`; it excludes `flutter test` deliberately and says so (ci.yml:16-19). So the mitigation this entry rested on — "`flutter test` is also in the gate, so nothing is unrun today" — is no longer true of the gate that now matters. ci.yml gains a second job running `flutter test`, which is the only command that executes `test/platform/`, `test/ui/` and `test/composition/`; the existing job stays as the fast binding-free check. One edit closes DW-51 and DW-74 with it. Its cost is real and is the reason it is a decision rather than an oversight — but a channel-level suite that nothing runs is not a gate.

### DW-30: a `restore` moves the mirror to true, which under AD-18 clears whatever the user had typed

origin: story 5-clipboard-and-panel-visibility-adapters.md, 2026-08-08 (review pass 2)
location: lib/src/infrastructure/panel/window_manager_panel_visibility.dart (`_onWindowEvent`, `restore` arm), lib/src/application/correction_controller.dart
severity: medium
reason: `restore` reconciles the mirror to true, `_setMirror` emits that transition on `changes`, and `CorrectionController` starts a fresh session on every `true` — `_setState(CorrectionState.empty)` plus a re-seed from the current clipboard. So a user who types into the panel, iconifies it from the task bar and restores it gets their text replaced by the clipboard. This is spec-conformant and follows AD-18 literally ("changes emits exactly on a transition of isVisible, in both directions and whatever caused it, because CorrectionController re-seeds a fresh session on every true"), and the `minimize`/`restore` pair is required by this story's tasks — `gtk_window_iconify` emits no GTK `hide`, so without it an iconified panel leaves the mirror claiming it is up. The trade was therefore made under AD-18's authority rather than overlooked, but it was made without being stated: no test pairs a restore-sourced `true` with the controller, `FakePanelVisibility` has no restore concept (`show`/`hide`/`loseFocus` only) so the application ring cannot express it, and separating "the panel came back" from "a new session starts" needs a distinction the port does not carry — a domain edit beyond the single doc comment this story was authorised to make. Decide it deliberately when story 9 builds the panel widget. Raised by the adversarial and verification-gap lenses independently.
status: done 2026-08-20
resolution: resolved by sweep bundle dw-panel-session-reason-and-marker
decision: 2026-08-14 Distinguish restore from a new session -- the human set a three-way rule rather than choosing an offered option: a minimise to the *tray icon* ends the session (text gone, clipboard re-seeded on the next summon); a minimise to the *task bar* or a loss of focus does not (typed text survives the restore). PanelVisibility must therefore carry why the window went away, three ways, not two -- FakePanelVisibility learns the same distinction.

### DW-31: the `_outstanding` guard also discards `minimize` and `close`, which can never be an echo of ours

origin: story 5-clipboard-and-panel-visibility-adapters.md, 2026-08-08 (review pass 2)
location: lib/src/infrastructure/panel/window_manager_panel_visibility.dart (`_reconcile`)
severity: medium
reason: The guard's justification is that a window event arriving while a request of ours is in flight is our own echo, already reported by the mirror. That holds for `show`, `hide` and `restore` — the last because `windowManager.show()` restores a minimised window as part of its own implementation. It does not hold for `minimize` or `close`: the adapter's only platform calls are `show`, `hide` and `focus`, and the Linux plugin emits `minimize` and `close` from GTK state changes and `delete-event`, never from those three. Both are therefore structurally external and are nonetheless discarded whenever `_outstanding > 0`. Reproduced by the edge-case lens against the shipped tree: with the panel up and an AD-14 second-launch `show()` in flight, `minimize` and `close` both leave `isVisible` true. `close` is the worse half — the toplevel is destroyed (`on_window_close` returns `_is_prevent_close`, false by default), so no later event can ever correct the mirror. Consequence is a stale mirror and one wasted press, not a visible panel, which is why it is filed rather than patched: the fix means letting `_reconcile` distinguish echo-capable events from structurally external ones, and that is a change to the guard the whole three-mechanism design rests on — worth making deliberately, with its own rows, rather than inside a review pass.
status: done 2026-08-15
resolution: resolved by sweep bundle dw-panel-window-event-reconciliation
decision: 2026-08-14 Split echo-capable events from structurally external ones — engineering call. Verified: `_reconcile` (`window_manager_panel_visibility.dart:210-213`) returns for *every* event while `_outstanding > 0`, and both `minimize` (:190-194) and `close` (:174-189) route through it, while the adapter's only platform calls are `show`, `hide` and `focus` (`_apply`, :140-159) — so neither can ever be an echo of ours. The guard is taken only for `show`, `hide` and `restore`, and the other two move the mirror unconditionally. This is a change to the mechanism the whole three-part design rests on, so it gets its own rows in both directions rather than riding along in a review pass. Lands with DW-12, whose close-is-a-hide stops `close` arriving on the ordinary path at all.

### DW-32: a blur suppressed while a request is outstanding is dropped, not deferred

origin: story 5-clipboard-and-panel-visibility-adapters.md, 2026-08-08 (review pass 2)
location: lib/src/infrastructure/panel/window_manager_panel_visibility.dart (`_onBlur`)
severity: medium
reason: `_onBlur` returns when `_outstanding > 0`, on the reasoning that "a user cannot have dismissed a panel that is still being mapped". That reasoning holds for the first `show`, when the panel is not yet on screen. It does not hold when `show()` is called on a panel that is *already* visible — which AD-14's second launch and AD-12's tray "open the panel" both do, and which the suite explicitly pins as issuing a full `show` + `focus` round trip. For that whole round trip the panel is up and focused, so a blur is a genuine dismissal; it is discarded, and nothing re-evaluates it. Reproduced by the edge-case lens: panel visible and settled, `show()` issued, `blur` during the round trip leaves `isVisible` true and the window visible, with calls `[show, focus, show, focus]`. CAP-14 did not fire for that blur and never will. The I/O matrix row this implements ("Blur during a show → Ignored") is inside `<intent-contract>` and was followed exactly, so this is a boundary the intent drew rather than a deviation from it; the fix is to latch a suppressed blur and re-run `_onBlur` when `_outstanding` reaches zero, re-checking `_visible` then.
status: done 2026-08-15
resolution: resolved by sweep bundle dw-panel-window-event-reconciliation
decision: 2026-08-14 Latch a suppressed blur and re-evaluate — engineering call. Verified: `_onBlur` (`window_manager_panel_visibility.dart:220-225`) returns whenever `_outstanding > 0`. Its stated justification — "a user cannot have dismissed a panel that is still being mapped" — is true of the first show and false of a `show()` on an already-visible panel, which is what AD-14's second launch and AD-12's tray entry both issue. The blur is remembered and `_onBlur` re-runs when `_outstanding` reaches zero, re-checking `_visible` then. The I/O matrix row this implements sits inside a story's `<intent-contract>`, so the change is recorded as a deliberate widening of that row rather than as a deviation from it. Lands with DW-33, whose focus tracking the latch has to consult.

### DW-33: a `blur` with no preceding `focus` dismisses a panel that never held the keyboard

origin: story 5-clipboard-and-panel-visibility-adapters.md, 2026-08-08 (review pass 2)
location: lib/src/infrastructure/panel/window_manager_panel_visibility.dart (`_onWindowEvent`, `_onBlur`)
severity: medium
reason: The Linux plugin emits `focus` and `blur` as a pair, from `focus-in-event` and `focus-out-event`. The adapter handles `blur` destructively and ignores `focus` entirely, so it never knows whether the window ever held focus — and reads any `focus-out` as a user dismissal. That state is reachable on a real session: `show()` is `gtk_widget_show` + `gtk_window_present`, and a window manager with focus-stealing prevention maps the window without giving it the keyboard, after which a transient `focus-out-event` arrives with the mirror true and nothing outstanding. Reproduced against the shipped tree with a bare `blur` after a settled `show()`: `isVisible` false, `window.visible` false, calls `[show, focus, hide]` — the panel the user just asked for takes itself down. Not observable in this container (no compositor, DW-9/DW-26), and distinct from DW-27, which is about a blur caused by a child window the panel itself opened. The fix is to track focus state from the `focus` event and require it before `_onBlur` acts.
status: done 2026-08-15
resolution: resolved by sweep bundle dw-panel-window-event-reconciliation
decision: 2026-08-14 Require focus before a blur acts — engineering call, and the most user-visible of the three blur entries. Verified: `_onWindowEvent` (`window_manager_panel_visibility.dart:166-197`) has no `focus` case at all while handling `blur` destructively at :195-196, so the adapter never knows whether the window held the keyboard. On a window manager with focus-stealing prevention that means the panel takes itself down at the moment it is summoned — the one failure that makes the product's core gesture unusable. The adapter tracks focus from the `focus` event and `_onBlur` returns unless the window is known to have held it. Not observable here (no compositor), so the fix ships with fake-driven rows and the real behaviour goes on DW-9's checklist.

### DW-34: an empty clipboard that answers `{'text': null}` reaches `readText()` as a rejection, not as absence

origin: story 5-clipboard-and-panel-visibility-adapters.md, 2026-08-08 (review pass 2)
location: lib/src/infrastructure/clipboard/system_clipboard.dart, test/platform/system_clipboard_test.dart
severity: low
reason: `ClipboardPort.readText` promises that absence is a modelled value and never a failed read, and `SystemClipboard` delegates to Flutter's `Clipboard.getData`, which ends `return ClipboardData(text: result['text'] as String);` — a non-null cast, confirmed in this SDK at `packages/flutter/lib/src/services/clipboard.dart:56`. A platform reply of `{'text': null}` therefore throws a `TypeError` before `data?.text` is evaluated, and CAP-2 logs a read failure for what is an ordinary empty clipboard. The suite's "no plain text" row mocks a top-level `null`, which is the other of the two shapes a platform can send, so the contract is verified for one and unverified for the other. Filed rather than patched because reachability is unknown: whether the Linux embedder ever sends the inner-null shape was not established, the story's task pins the adapter's body to the two-line delegation, and mapping a `TypeError` to `null` is a judgement about malformed payloads that should be made against evidence from the engine rather than defensively.
status: done 2026-08-14
decision: 2026-08-14 Close — unreachable on the target platform — engineering call, decided on engine evidence rather than defensively, which is what the entry asked for.
resolution: closed 2026-08-14 on verified evidence: the inner-null reply shape cannot occur on Linux. The Dart half of the claim holds — `Clipboard.getData` ends in the non-null cast `return ClipboardData(text: result['text'] as String);` at `/home/vscode/flutter/packages/flutter/lib/src/services/clipboard.dart:55` — but the GTK embedder never sends it: `fl_platform_channel_respond_clipboard_get_data` builds a map only `if (text != nullptr)` and otherwise responds with a null result outright (`flutter/engine/src/flutter/shell/platform/linux/fl_platform_channel.cc:284-293`). An empty clipboard therefore arrives as the top-level null that `system_clipboard.dart:24-27`'s `data?.text` already models as absence, and that is exactly the shape the suite's existing "no plain text" row mocks. Re-file only against a second embedder, if this ever ships on one.

### DW-35: `show()`/`hide()` resolve identically whether the window moved, the request was superseded, or the adapter was disposed

origin: story 5-clipboard-and-panel-visibility-adapters.md, 2026-08-08 (review pass 2)
location: lib/src/domain/panel/panel_visibility.dart, lib/src/infrastructure/panel/window_manager_panel_visibility.dart
severity: low
reason: Three outcomes are indistinguishable to the caller: the window was mapped and focused; a later press superseded the request so it issued nothing; the adapter was torn down and the call was a no-op. All resolve as a plain success, and `PanelController._fire` attaches only a `catchError`. That is correct for the toggle, which is fire-and-forget by AD-8, and DW-28 records the queue-stall half of this as a timeout-policy question. The contract half is separate and is present on every press: story 9's panel widget will await `show()` to know when to render, and the future cannot tell it. Resolving it means stating on `PanelVisibility.show`/`hide` that the future means "the intent was accepted", not "the window moved" — a second domain edit, which this story's Block If names as a renegotiation rather than an unattended choice.
status: done 2026-08-14
decision: 2026-08-14 Close — subsumed by DW-28 — engineering call, contingent on that entry's build.
resolution: closed by engineering decision 2026-08-14, contingent on DW-28: the 2026-08-14 answer on DW-28 already commits to bounding each queued window call and to stating on `PanelVisibility.show`/`hide` what a resolved future means for a superseded or disposal-abandoned request. That statement is precisely this entry's contract half, and the queue-stall half was always DW-28's, so the two are one piece of work with one owner. Verified unchanged meanwhile: `show()`/`hide()` (`window_manager_panel_visibility.dart:87-105`) return the enqueued future, and `_apply` (:130-160) returns without issuing anything on both the superseded and the disposed branches, so all three outcomes still resolve alike.

### DW-36: the window-event subscription handles `onError` but not `onDone`

origin: story 5-clipboard-and-panel-visibility-adapters.md, 2026-08-08 (review pass 2)
location: lib/src/infrastructure/panel/window_manager_panel_visibility.dart (constructor)
severity: low
reason: The subscription carries an `onError` backstop with an explicit AD-15 rationale — "every later reconciliation depends on it" — and no `onDone`. That rationale applies verbatim to completion, and the failure mode is worse: an errored stream logs a line, a completed one is silent. If `_window.events` closes while the adapter is live, reconciliation stops permanently, the adapter looks healthy, and CAP-14's focus-loss hide, the external-hide correction and `minimize` all quietly stop working. Not reachable today — the adapter owns the seam and cancels its own subscription before disposing it, so nothing else closes the stream — but `PanelWindow.dispose()`'s doc advertises that it closes `events`, so the shape is reachable by design rather than by accident.
status: done 2026-08-14
decision: 2026-08-14 Add the `onDone` backstop — engineering call. Verified: the subscription at `window_manager_panel_visibility.dart:47-58` carries `onError` with an explicit AD-15 rationale — "every later reconciliation depends on it" — and no `onDone`. That rationale applies verbatim to completion, and the failure is worse in the direction that matters: an errored stream leaves a log line, a completed one leaves nothing while the adapter goes on looking healthy and CAP-14's focus-loss hide, the external-hide correction and `minimize` all stop working. One line, the same shape as the arm beside it. Unreachable today, but `PanelWindow.dispose()`'s doc advertises closing `events`, so the shape is reachable by design rather than by accident.
resolution: closed 2026-08-14 by the fix this entry names. `lib/src/infrastructure/panel/window_manager_panel_visibility.dart` now carries an `onDone` arm beside `onError` on the constructor's `_window.events.listen(...)`, routed through `_log` like every other emit in the file, logging one `error` line — "the panel window event stream closed; window events can no longer reconcile the panel visibility mirror" — with no context map, because there is no error to describe. It states the consequence rather than the event, which is the whole difference from `onError`: an errored stream leaves a line and a live subscription, a completed one leaves a permanently dead adapter that still reports healthy. The adjoining AD-15 comment now covers completion as well as error. Log-only by decision: no recovery, resubscription, retry or dead-stream flag, and the arm is deliberately unguarded by `_disposed` to match `onError` — on the ordinary path such a guard would be dead code, since `dispose()` cancels the subscription before `_window.dispose()` closes the stream. Three rows in `test/infrastructure/panel/window_manager_panel_visibility_test.dart` pin it, next to the existing error row: a stream closed under a live adapter emits exactly one `error` line naming the close and the lost reconciliation; an ordinary `dispose()` emits none, because the cancel precedes the close; and a `Logger` whose `error` throws (a `_ThrowingLogger` local to that file, not a mode on the shared `FakeLogger`) lets nothing escape as an uncaught async error. Reaching the live-adapter close needed one test affordance, `FakePanelWindow.closeEvents()`, which closes the events controller without disposing the window. The throwing-logger row uses the shared `ThrowingLogger` from `test/fakes/throwing_logger.dart` and asserts its `attempts`, not a file-local copy: an earlier revision duplicated the fake and asserted only that nothing escaped, which stayed green when the `onDone` arm was deleted outright. Negative controls, each run against the scoped binding-free panel suite and reverted — scoped rather than full-suite deliberately, per DW-46's finding that a mutation claim drawn from a single full-suite run is unsound: deleting the `onDone` arm — 2 failures (the close row and the throwing-logger row); bypassing `_log` and calling `_logger.error` directly — 1 failure; moving the subscription cancel after `_window.dispose()` in `dispose()` — 1 failure. So none of the three rows is vacuous. `dart analyze` clean; `dart test test/infrastructure/panel/` green at 34 rows. The wider `dart test test/infrastructure/ test/application/ test/domain/` is **not** reliably green on this tree and was not made so here: `wayland_portal_global_hotkey_test.dart` fails a varying subset of rows run to run, reproduced on the untouched baseline and in isolation, and imports nothing from the panel slice. That is DW-46, still open.

### DW-37: an abort before the lifecycle exists leaves the controllers and the container undisposed

origin: story 5-clipboard-and-panel-visibility-adapters.md, 2026-08-08 (review pass 2)
location: lib/main.dart (`_abort`, `_releaseWithoutLifecycle`)
severity: medium
reason: `DaemonGraph.build()` reads its providers in sequence, so a throw on the second or third leaves earlier controllers constructed — including `CorrectionController`, already subscribed to `PanelVisibility.changes` — while `lifecycle` is still null. `_releaseWithoutLifecycle` then closes the panel adapter, the database, the config store and the lock, and never calls `disposeControllers()` or `container.dispose()`. So `changes` is closed under a live subscriber and the database is closed under a controller that was never drained, which is the inverse of the ordering `DaemonLifecycle.shutdown()` exists to guarantee. Bounded by the `exit(1)` immediately after, so nothing outlives it — which is why the review pass corrected `_abort`'s doc (it had claimed no controllers exist on that path) rather than restructuring the teardown. The real fix is to pass the nullable graph into `_releaseWithoutLifecycle` so controllers and container come down first, in the same order the lifecycle uses.
status: done 2026-08-14
resolution: resolved by sweep bundle dw-daemon-startup-and-abort-path
decision: 2026-08-14 Pass the graph into the release path — engineering call, taking the fix the entry names. Verified: `main.dart:304-315` sends a null-lifecycle abort to `_releaseWithoutLifecycle`, whose step list (:331-363) closes the panel adapter, the tray, the hotkey adapter and seam, the database, the config store and the lock — and calls neither `disposeControllers()` nor `container.dispose()`. `DaemonGraph.build()` reads its providers in sequence, so a throw partway through leaves `CorrectionController` constructed and subscribed to `PanelVisibility.changes` while that stream is closed underneath it. Controllers and container come down first, in the order `DaemonLifecycle.shutdown()` uses. Bounded by the `exit(1)` at :316, which makes this cheap rather than optional: the teardown list is the one place the ordering is written down and it should not be written down wrong.

  evidence: `tray_manager-0.5.3/lib/src/tray_manager.dart:124-133` replaces the joined path with `iconPath` verbatim when `runningInSandbox()` holds, and `helpers/sandbox.dart` makes that true for `FLATPAK_ID`, `SNAP`, a non-empty `container` env var, or `/.dockerenv` — the last of which exists in this very container. The native `set_icon` returns true regardless. Not fixed here because the spine's Deferred section leaves the packaging format undecided (and notes Flatpak also changes AD-11's handshake), so the correct icon-name and its install location are packaging decisions story 11 owns. `tray_icon.dart`'s doc now names the branch and states it is not implemented. Distinct from the two install entries already filed: those are about a host appearing *later* and a host that *answers never*, and both assume the failure is observable. - source_spec: `_bmad-output/specs/spec-hotkey-grammar-corrector/stories/6-tray-adapter.md`
  summary: A stop signal arriving during `await tray.install()` runs `dispose()` concurrently with a suspended `_push()`, which rechecks `_disposed` after neither await — so a late `setIcon` can re-activate an indicator that teardown already set passive, with nothing left to undo it.
  evidence: `main.dart` installs the signal handlers before `_finishStartup`, so SIGTERM/SIGINT/SIGHUP during the newly-awaited install reaches `shutdown()` → `closeTray` → `dispose()` while `_push()` is suspended at `await _icon.setIcon(...)`. `_push()` then resumes and writes `_installed`/`_rendered` on a torn-down adapter and invokes `setMenu` on a disposed seam. Worse natively: `destroy` only calls `app_indicator_set_status(PASSIVE)` and leaves the static `indicator` non-null (`tray_manager_plugin.cc:106-111`), so a `setIcon` landing after it calls `app_indicator_set_status(ACTIVE)` and re-shows the icon after teardown, with `_disposed` latched so nothing sets it passive again. Distinct from the overlapping-pushes entry above, which is about two *port* callers racing; this is teardown racing an in-flight install, and it has a real production trigger today. Not patched here because the fix is a concurrency-model change (holding the in-flight push future and awaiting or invalidating it in `dispose()`) to a state machine that has just been through two repair passes. Raised by the adversarial lens.
  status: parked 2026-08-13

- source_spec: `_bmad-output/specs/spec-hotkey-grammar-corrector/stories/6-tray-adapter.md`

  evidence: `DaemonLifecycle.start()` guards `_shuttingDown` and `TrayManagerTray` guards `_disposed`, but `_finishStartup` itself has no `lifecycle.isShuttingDown` check between the install and the bind. A signal handled during the install therefore leaves `await startup.bindHotkey(tray: tray)` running against a graph in teardown — either registering a global grab for a process on its way out, or producing a rejection that `requestBinding` quietly reduces to a value. The window is pre-existing in shape but this story widened it by putting a new platform round-trip on that stretch of the critical path, and it is unbounded until the no-timeout entry above is settled. Adjacent to that entry but distinct: this is about the continuation running after teardown, not about the stall. Raised by the edge-case lens.
  status: parked 2026-08-13

- source_spec: `_bmad-output/specs/spec-hotkey-grammar-corrector/stories/6-tray-adapter.md`
  summary: Teardown step 1 is now two sequential `_step` calls, so a show-request cancel that hangs means the tray subscription is never cancelled at all — tray picks keep reaching a graph being disposed, which is the exact condition step 1 exists to prevent.
  evidence: `daemon_lifecycle.dart`'s `shutdown()` issues `('cancel show requests', ...)` and `('cancel tray requests', ...)` back to back, and `_step` bounds failure but not duration — it swallows a rejection, but awaits a cancel that never completes forever. `SingleInstanceLock`'s socket stream is the one whose cancel can plausibly block. Step 1's stated guarantee ("nothing reaches a controller mid-teardown") therefore now depends on an unrelated adapter's cancel completing first, on the path this story added. `Future.wait` over both cancels inside one step, or two independently-timed steps, closes it. Related to the general absence of a timeout in `_step`, which is pre-existing. Raised by the adversarial lens.
  status: parked 2026-08-13

  evidence: `lib/src/domain/tray/tray_port.dart:3-4` states the once-only expectation; `TrayManagerTray.install()` carries no `_installed` guard, and `tray_manager_tray_test.dart`'s failed-install recovery row calls it twice on purpose. Not fixed here because the Code Map marks `tray_port.dart` **Read-only — no edit, no widening**. It is the second port-vs-adapter doc contradiction on this file (the "on this compositor" entry above is the first) and the two should be settled in one edit: story 10's author reads the port, and taking the "once" promise at face value means either adding a redundant guard or relying on a promise the recovery design specifically depends on breaking. Raised by the adversarial lens.

- source_spec: `_bmad-output/specs/spec-hotkey-grammar-corrector/stories/6-tray-adapter.md`
  summary: Both tray icons are 32x32 full-colour PNGs, but Linux tray hosts overwhelmingly expect symbolic, recolourable icons scaled to panel height — so on a shell that recolours tray icons the amber warning corner, which is the entire icon half of AD-12, is flattened away.
  evidence: `app_indicator_set_icon_full` hands the path straight through with no size negotiation, so a HiDPI panel upscales a 32x32 bitmap. The degraded state's only visual difference from the available one is colour (desaturated glyph plus an amber corner marker), which is precisely what a monochrome-recolouring host discards — leaving the menu line as the sole surviving surface and reducing AD-12's deliberate two-surface design to one. Not fixed here: shipping a symbolic mark, multiple sizes, or a themed SVG installed under an icon name is the same packaging decision the sandbox icon-name entry above is waiting on, and none of it is observable in this container. The story's own residual risks concede icon legibility is untested, but record it as "one human look at 32px" rather than as an unexamined icon-convention assumption. Raised by the adversarial lens.
  status: parked 2026-08-13

- source_spec: `_bmad-output/specs/spec-hotkey-grammar-corrector/stories/6-tray-adapter.md`
  summary: The native `set_context_menu` builds a fresh `GtkMenu` and never destroys the previous one, so every menu push leaks a menu plus its items and signal connections in a process designed to run for the whole session.
  evidence: `tray_manager_plugin.cc`'s handler assigns `menu = _create_menu(...)` — `gtk_menu_new()` with fresh `gtk_menu_item_new_with_label` children — over the old static pointer with no `gtk_widget_destroy`. Bounded at two pushes per session today (install plus the one bind outcome), so harmless now, but the adapter's design is that every state change re-pushes the whole menu, and story 10's settings screen is named as the second caller: a user toggling a hotkey binding would leak one menu per toggle. Same root cause as the `menu_base` id-churn entry above — re-pushing the whole menu on every change — and the declined entry-equality short-circuit would bound both, which is worth revisiting once a second caller exists. A vendor defect, so the realistic remedies are an upstream fix or bounding the push count here. Raised by the adversarial lens.
  status: parked 2026-08-13
