Existing widget evidence and source inspection support the action; native pointer behavior was not observed.
decision: 2026-08-14 Make the cards tappable -- clicking a suggestion card selects it exactly as the shortcut does, with the selected register held in CorrectionController. Keyboard selection is unchanged. Lands with DW-57, which the human scoped as one piece of work with this.

### DW-53: the panel's minimum-height floor is a widget-side fallback, not a window constraint

origin: story 9-correction-panel-ui.md review pass, 2026-08-11
location: lib/src/ui/panel/correction_panel.dart (`CorrectionPanel.minimumPanelHeight`), lib/main.dart (`_createHiddenWindow`)
severity: medium
reason: The review pass measured a RenderFlex overflow below roughly 220 logical pixels of height (clean at 480×240, 0.8 px over at 480×220, 8.8 px at 480×200), so the panel now carries a documented floor of 240: at and above it the two panes split the surface as CAP-10 requires, below it the whole panel scrolls at the floor's layout rather than clipping. Two rows pin it (`correction_panel_layout_test.dart` at 480×160, for the variants and for the inline error). What that does **not** do is stop a user resizing into it — nothing sets a size or a minimum size on the toplevel, which is DW-50 and still open. So CAP-10 is claimed at and above the floor only, the floor is enforced by the panel rather than by the window, and a window dragged shorter shows a scrolling panel instead of a side-by-side one. Closing it needs the `window_manager` `setMinimumSize` call that `test/architecture/hidden_window_test.dart`'s allowlist deliberately does not admit, plus someone who can see the result.
status: done 2026-08-14
decision: 2026-08-14 Close — answered by DW-50 — the human's geometry answer is exactly what this entry asked for.
resolution: closed by human decision 2026-08-14 (DW-50): the floor stops being widget-side. DW-50 sets a real toplevel minimum size equal to `CorrectionPanel.minimumPanelHeightFor`, so a window cannot be dragged into the range where CAP-10 fails. Two corrections to this entry's own text, for the record: the floor is **300**, not the 240 stated here — `correction_panel.dart:65`, with the measurement rationale at :54-63 explaining that 240 is merely where overflow stops and 300 is where the editor gets a full line — and it scales with the reader's text size via `minimumPanelHeightFor` (:78-81), which DW-61 added. Verified unchanged until DW-50 lands: `main.dart:419-424` sets no size and no minimum.

### DW-54: "a new session started" is inferred from a state value, not carried by one

origin: story 9-correction-panel-ui.md review pass, 2026-08-11
location: lib/src/ui/panel/correction_panel.dart (`_onStateChanged`), lib/src/application/correction_state.dart
severity: low
reason: The panel has to know when a show began a fresh session, because that is when the caret must return to the editor — `autofocus` fires once per mount and the tree is never rebuilt (AD-8), so without it the second summon eats the first keystroke (fixed this pass, pinned by the AD-18 second-show row). The signal it uses is `state == CorrectionState.empty`, which is exactly what `_beginSession` emits and nothing else *builds* — but it is a value comparison, so an idle session whose editor the user clears by hand produces the same value and re-requests editor focus. That is harmless today (focus is already in the editor in that case, and requesting it again is a no-op) and it is why the inference was accepted rather than the state widened. It stops being harmless the moment anything else keys off "a session began" — a session counter or an explicit event on `CorrectionState` is the honest fix, and it is an application-ring change this review pass was not scoped to make.
status: done 2026-08-20
resolution: resolved by sweep bundle dw-panel-session-reason-and-marker
decision: 2026-08-14 Carry an explicit session signal, landing with DW-30 — engineering call, forced by the human's DW-30 answer. Verified: the comparison is now named rather than repeated — `CorrectionState.isFreshSession => this == empty` (`correction_state.dart:84`) — and it has acquired the second consumer this entry predicted would end its harmlessness: `correction_panel.dart:214` returns the caret to the editor and `daemon_home.dart:110` returns the window to the panel view. Both are still idempotent under a hand-cleared editor, so nothing is broken today. What settles it is DW-30: the human's three-way rule requires `PanelVisibility` to say *why* the window went away, so the controller will know explicitly when a session begins and can stamp it. `CorrectionState` gains the marker; two consumers stop inferring it from an `==`.

### DW-55: two overlapping copies reach the clipboard in whatever order the platform chose

origin: story 9-correction-panel-ui.md review pass, 2026-08-11
location: lib/src/application/correction_controller.dart (`copySuggestion`, `_copyToken`), lib/src/ui/panel/correction_panel.dart (`_copy`)
severity: low
reason: CAP-14 keeps every variant copyable and the panel does not serialise or disable copy buttons while a write is in flight, so a user can press two in quick succession. This pass fixed the *verdict* half — a copy token bumped by every attempt and invalidated by a new run and a new session, so only the newest copy's outcome is ever rendered (four rows pin it, in both completion orders). What no layer can fix from here is which text ends up on the clipboard: two `ClipboardPort.writeText` calls are two pieces of display-server IPC and the last one to be *applied* wins, which need not be the last one issued. The panel then shows the newest copy's verdict over a clipboard that may hold the other variant. Bounded in practice (the two presses are a human's, hundreds of milliseconds apart, and both variants are ones the user asked for), and the honest fixes are either serialising writes in the controller or having the port report the applied value back — the second is a port widening, the first costs the "every variant stays copyable" immediacy CAP-14 asks for. Recorded rather than chosen.
status: done 2026-09-26
resolution: Plan 02-07 serializes `CorrectionController` clipboard writes and uses request generations so the last requested valid copy owns feedback; its eight-second bound reports a stalled write. Source inspection and existing controller/widget tests support ordering, but a timed-out platform write cannot be canceled through the current `ClipboardPort`, and native clipboard timing was not observed.
decision: 2026-08-14 Serialise writes in the controller -- engineering call, taking the option that needs no port widening. ClipboardPort.writeText calls are chained in CorrectionController so the last press is the last write applied; copy buttons stay enabled; rows drive two overlapping copies in both completion orders.

### DW-56: the spine's Capability→Architecture map places CAP-4 and CAP-11 in `ui/panel`; their behaviour now lives in `application/`

origin: story 9-correction-panel-ui.md review pass, 2026-08-11
location: ARCHITECTURE-SPINE.md "Capability → Architecture Map" rows for CAP-4 and CAP-11 vs lib/src/application/correction_controller.dart (`selectSuggestion`, `copySuggestion`, `_completedTextOf`) and lib/src/application/correction_state.dart (`selectedRegister`, `copyFailure`)
severity: low
reason: The map lists CAP-4 as living in `domain/correction`, `ui/panel` and CAP-11 in `ui/panel`, `domain/clipboard` — neither names an application component, while CAP-13's row explicitly does. What shipped puts the selection state and the copy action in the controller, because the Consistency Conventions "State mutation" row makes one controller the owner of a surface's state (which is also what gives AD-18's clear-on-show, clear-on-new-run and clear-on-failure for free) and AGENTS.md §3 forbids clipboard access inside a widget class. Both readings are defensible and the story chose deliberately; the consequence is that the two capabilities' behaviour-bearing code and most of their tests are one ring inward of where a reader starting from the spine's map would look. A spine currency item alongside DW-2, DW-7 and DW-41, recorded rather than hand-edited into the spine, following the convention stories 1, 2 and 4 used. Raised by the intent-alignment lens as its dominant axis.
status: done 2026-08-14
resolution: resolved by sweep bundle dw-spine-currency-refresh
decision: 2026-08-14 Carried by DW-2's spine refresh — engineering call. Verified: the map's CAP-4 row (ARCHITECTURE-SPINE.md:549) reads `domain/correction`, `ui/panel` and CAP-11's (:555) reads `ui/panel`, `domain/clipboard`, while CAP-13's row (:557) does name an application component — so the omission is inconsistent within the table itself. What shipped is right: the Consistency Conventions make one controller the owner of a surface's state, which is also what gives AD-18's clear-on-show for free, and AGENTS.md §3 forbids clipboard access inside a widget class. The map is what is stale, and a reader starting from it looks one ring outward of where the behaviour lives.

### DW-57: the selecting digits are logical keys, so on a non-QWERTY layout the `1`/`2`/`3` hint promises a key that selects nothing

origin: story 9-correction-panel-ui.md follow-up review pass, 2026-08-11
location: lib/src/ui/panel/register_key_slot.dart (`digitKeyForIndex`), lib/src/ui/panel/correction_panel.dart (`_selectionShortcuts`), lib/src/ui/panel/suggestion_card.dart (`keyHint`)
severity: medium
reason: AD-6 says the selecting key **is** `SuggestionRegister.values.indexOf(r) + 1`, and the story derives it as `LogicalKeyboardKey(LogicalKeyboardKey.digit1.keyId + index)` — a *logical* key, i.e. the character the layout produces. On a layout whose unshifted top row is not the digits (AZERTY produces `& é "`, and Dvorak-style variants differ too) those events carry other logical keys, so no row is selected; the shifted form does carry `digit1` but arrives with Shift down, which a bare `SingleActivator` rejects. Meanwhile `SuggestionCard.keyHint` prints an un-dimmed `1`/`2`/`3` whenever the variant is actionable, so the panel promises a keystroke it will ignore — the DW-3 affordance failure this story exists to remove, narrowed to a class of users. The keypad twin only helps with Num Lock on. Not patched here because the fix is a keyboard-model decision with more than one defensible answer: matching `PhysicalKeyboardKey.digit1.usbHidUsage + index` selects by key *position* (right for QWERTY and AZERTY, wrong for anyone who remaps deliberately), adding a shifted activator per slot collides with whatever the layout puts on Shift+digit, and deriving the hint from the layout's actual character needs a mapping Flutter does not expose synchronously. `test/platform/correction_panel_live_test.dart` already owes "a real keyboard"; this is the specific claim it owes. Whoever decides should make the hint degrade too — no hint is honest, a wrong hint is not.
status: done 2026-09-26
resolution: Plan 02-06 adds physical top-row digit positions alongside logical and keypad shortcuts in `lib/src/ui/panel/correction_panel.dart`, so the displayed `1`/`2`/`3` names the physical positions. The phase spec chose this narrower route over the earlier full-configurable-shortcuts decision recorded above. Source inspection against the installed Flutter SDK supports it; no real non-QWERTY desktop layout was observed.
decision: 2026-08-14 Full configurable shortcuts -- the human scoped past the offered options: three per-variant bindings, each with modifiers, editable on the settings screen and stored in the config file through the same edit-and-apply flow as the main hotkey, active only while the window is focused, defaulting to Alt+1/Alt+2/Alt+3. The digit half is matched by physical key position so the default works on AZERTY and Dvorak (the agent's call on how, the human's on what). THIS RENEGOTIATES CAP-4, which reads 'pressing 1, 2, or 3 selects the corresponding variant' -- a fixed capability becomes a configurable one plus a second shortcut surface. The SPEC change is flagged here and is not applied by an implementation session; SPEC.md is regenerated by /bmad-spec.

### DW-58: the panel's height floor was measured at text scale 1.0 and nothing reads `MediaQuery.textScaler`

origin: story 9-correction-panel-ui.md follow-up review pass, 2026-08-11
location: lib/src/ui/panel/correction_panel.dart (`CorrectionPanel.minimumPanelHeight`, the `LayoutBuilder` in `build`), test/ui/panel/correction_panel_layout_test.dart
severity: low
reason: `minimumPanelHeight` is a logical-pixel constant, raised to 300 this pass because that is where the editor's flex share first exceeds one line of its 16 px text (7.2 px at the old 240, probe-confirmed, which is why the class doc's CAP-10 claim was false at its own floor). The chrome it was measured against — the `labelMedium` caption, the `ElevatedButton`'s tap target, the card's label and hint — all scale with `MediaQuery.textScaler`, which no widget here reads and no test sets. With a desktop set to large text (GNOME's 1.25–1.5×) the fixed chrome grows while the editor's share does not, so the floor stops being the height at which CAP-10 holds and can overflow above it. Not patched because the honest fix is to derive the floor from measured intrinsics rather than a constant, which changes how the panel decides its layout rather than what the number is; a layout row at `TextScaler.linear(1.5)` should land with it. Related to DW-50 and DW-53: the floor is still a widget-side fallback for a window nothing sizes.
status: done 2026-08-14
resolution: already resolved: lib/src/ui/panel/correction_panel.dart:78-81 `static double minimumPanelHeightFor(TextScaler textScaler) => minimumPanelHeight * textScaler.scale(_measuredAtFontSize) / _measuredAtFontSize;`, called with `MediaQuery.textScalerOf(context)` at :292-294; test/ui/panel/correction_panel_layout_test.dart:359 drives `TextScaler.linear(1.5)` and :384 asserts a full editor line. The entry's claim — that the floor is a constant and nothing reads MediaQuery.textScaler — is no longer true. The residual 'the floor is a widget-side stand-in for a window constraint' half is DW-61's and stays open in the panel-window-geometry bundle.

### DW-59: a successful copy renders nothing, so a working clipboard and a hung write look the same

origin: story 9-correction-panel-ui.md follow-up review pass, 2026-08-11
location: lib/src/application/correction_controller.dart (`copySuggestion`, `_clearCopyFailure`), lib/src/ui/panel/correction_panel.dart (the `copyFailure` notice)
severity: medium
reason: `copySuggestion` emits no state at all when the write succeeds (`_clearCopyFailure` returns early when there is no notice to drop), and the panel has no success affordance — so the panel is byte-identical before and after a copy that worked. The story's I/O matrix specifies the copy path's failure surface ("a rejected write logs at error level (type only) and shows one inline notice; the panel stays open") and asks for nothing on success, so this is out of that scope rather than a breach, and it is the ordinary behaviour of a copy button. What makes it worth filing is the neighbouring case: a `ClipboardPort.writeText` that never resolves (display-server IPC can hang) produces the same nothing, so the user pastes stale content believing it is the variant they pressed — the same silent lie `copyFailure` was added to prevent, in the one shape the notice cannot cover. The panel already fixed the analogous "running looks like idle" gap with a progress affordance, which is the precedent for how this would be closed. Any fix has to stay inside CAP-13's constraint that this story renders no toast, snackbar or dialog: a short-lived inline confirmation in the existing live region, or a per-card "copied" state. Interacts with DW-55, which records that overlapping copies are ordered by the platform.
status: done 2026-09-26
resolution: Plan 02-07 gives each suggestion card distinct Copying, Copied, and failure feedback from `CorrectionController` state; Copied appears only after a completed write, while a stalled write gets bounded failure. Existing tests and source inspection support the states; no native stalled clipboard write was observed.
decision: 2026-08-14 Per-card "Copied" state on the button — the human's call. Verified the gap: `copySuggestion` (`correction_controller.dart:208-215`) emits no state at all on a successful write, so the panel is byte-identical before and after a copy — and a `ClipboardPort.writeText` that never resolves produces exactly the same nothing, which means a hung clipboard and a working one look alike and the user pastes stale text believing otherwise. The pressed suggestion's own copy button reads Copied, and only once the write actually resolves, so an unresolved write stays visibly unconfirmed. Stays inside CAP-13's constraint that this surface renders no toast, snackbar or dialog, and it says which of the three variants the user now holds. Ordering under overlapping presses is already settled by DW-55's answer — writes are serialised in the controller — so the confirmation follows the same last-press-wins rule and does not need a second policy.

### DW-60: the AD-1 ui symbol gate is a substring denylist, so it closes the doors that have been thought of

origin: story 9-correction-panel-ui.md follow-up review pass 2, 2026-08-11
location: test/architecture/ad1_import_rule_test.dart (`_uiBannedSymbols`, `_bannedUiSymbols`, `_stripComments`, `_declaredTopLevelFinals`)
severity: medium
reason: Three independent bypasses of this gate were found and closed in this pass, each with every AD-1 row green beforehand: `BasicMessageChannel('flutter/platform', …)` and `defaultBinaryMessenger.send(…)` reached the system clipboard past `ClipboardPort` (the denylist named only `MethodChannel`, `EventChannel` and `SystemChannels`); a banned call sharing a line with any string containing `//` was deleted by the comment stripper before the scan saw it, so `final u = 'https://x'; Clipboard.setData(…);` passed while the same call alone on a line failed; and the seam-classification row discovered seams with `^final (\w+Provider) =`, so a seam declared with an explicit type or named without the `Provider` suffix was classified by nothing — falsifying that row's stated purpose of failing the gate when a later story adds a seam. All three are now closed and each has a checker self-test. What remains true is the shape of the thing: it is a substring denylist over source text, so it forbids what someone has enumerated and cannot forbid what nobody has thought of yet. This is the third consecutive review pass to find a hole in it, which is the datum worth recording. A genuinely closed version needs an allowlist rather than a denylist — the ui ring may name *these* platform symbols and no others — or an analyzer-level rule over resolved element URIs rather than text. Related to DW-51 (the gate runs where `test/ui` does not).
status: open
decision: 2026-08-14 Replace the denylist with an analyzer-level rule — engineering call, taking the option that closes the shape rather than one more hole. Verified: `_uiBannedSymbols` (`test/architecture/ad1_import_rule_test.dart:504` onward) is a list of substrings scanned over source text, and the three bypasses this entry names are closed — `MessageChannel` and `BinaryMessenger` were added at :517-518 to cover `BasicMessageChannel` and `defaultBinaryMessenger`. A custom lint over *resolved element URIs* asks which library a symbol comes from instead of whether a piece of text appears, so a ui file naming a platform symbol nobody enumerated fails without anyone having thought of it first. An allowlist over source text was the other candidate and is rejected: same text-scanning fragility, polarity flipped. Three consecutive review passes each finding a hole is the datum that settles it — the gate's own history is the argument. Closes DW-75's residual half.

### DW-61: the panel's height floor now scales with the text, but is still a widget-side stand-in for a window constraint

origin: story 9-correction-panel-ui.md follow-up review pass 2, 2026-08-11
location: lib/src/ui/panel/correction_panel.dart (`minimumPanelHeightFor`), test/ui/panel/correction_panel_layout_test.dart
severity: low
reason: DW-58 recorded that the floor was measured at text scale 1.0 while everything it was measured against scales with `MediaQuery.textScaler`, and predicted an overflow above the floor. Two review layers reproduced the effect and it is not an overflow: nothing overflows, the editor's flex share silently shrinks — 31.2 px at 1.0×, 23.2 at 1.5×, 15.2 at 2.0×, against lines of 16, 24 and 32 px — so the floor certified CAP-10 at heights where the original could not show one line, from roughly 1.45× up (GNOME's large-text range is 1.25–1.5×). Patched here by scaling the floor proportionally with the text rather than by the intrinsics rework DW-58 assumed was needed, with a layout row at `TextScaler.linear(1.5)`. What this does **not** fix, and what keeps a ledger entry alive: the floor is still enforced by the panel rather than by the window (DW-50, DW-53), the scale factor is proportional rather than derived from measured intrinsics, and the whole family of claims is asserted at surfaces a test chose rather than observed on a desktop.
status: done 2026-08-14
decision: 2026-08-14 Close — answered by DW-50 — same terms as DW-53.
resolution: closed by human decision 2026-08-14 (DW-50): the scaling half of this entry shipped and is verified — `CorrectionPanel.minimumPanelHeightFor` (`correction_panel.dart:78-81`) grows the floor with `MediaQuery.textScaler`, with the probe-measured editor shares (31.2 px at 1.0×, 23.2 at 1.5×, 15.2 at 2.0× at 480×300) recorded in its doc at :68-77. The two halves this entry kept alive are now owned elsewhere: the floor being enforced by the panel rather than by the window is closed by DW-50's toplevel minimum size, and "asserted at surfaces a test chose rather than observed on a desktop" is an itemised step on DW-9's runtime checklist. The remaining nuance — that the scale factor is proportional rather than derived from measured intrinsics — is deliberately accepted: it is measured against the claim it certifies, and an intrinsics rework buys precision nobody has asked for.

### DW-62: the panel binds its controller once, and only today's provider graph makes that safe

origin: story 9-correction-panel-ui.md follow-up review pass 2, 2026-08-11
location: lib/src/ui/panel/correction_panel.dart (`_controller`, `initState`, `_onStateStreamClosed`), lib/src/application/composition/controller_providers.dart
severity: low
reason: `_CorrectionPanelState.initState` does `ref.read(correctionControllerProvider)` once and keeps the instance for the panel's life, justified by the provider being a plain `Provider` whose value never changes. That is a fact about the current graph, not about the type: `correctionControllerProvider` watches `activePresetProvider` and `activeCorrectionProviderProvider`, which `main.dart` supplies as constant value-overrides. Story 10 is the settings surface; if it makes either of them mutable, the provider rebuilds, the controller read here is disposed and its `changes` stream closes — and the panel's only handler for that is `_onStateStreamClosed`, which is written for shutdown and deliberately keeps the last state rather than re-binding. The panel would then be frozen on its final frame and no later summon would reach it. Not patched: re-binding is only correct once something can trigger it, and a path nothing can trigger is a path no test can pin — this pass has removed several such properties rather than adding one. Whoever makes a watched seam mutable owns this: either re-bind with `ref.listen`, or keep the seam constant and say so where it is declared. The doc comment now states the condition rather than asserting the value never changes.
status: done 2026-09-26
resolution: Plans 02-01 and 02-08 keep one `CorrectionController` identity for the daemon lifetime and replace its active provider/preset pair atomically, so `CorrectionPanel`'s single subscription cannot go stale on a Settings change. Plan 02-08 records and checks that graph property in `controller_providers.dart`, `daemon_graph.dart`, and `correction_panel.dart`. This supersedes the earlier `ref.listen` rebind decision without claiming a provider-graph rebuild occurred.
decision: 2026-08-14 Re-bind with ref.listen -- carried by the human's DW-16 answer, which the entry says it must land with. CorrectionPanel re-reads and re-subscribes when correctionControllerProvider rebuilds, preserving nothing across the swap that AD-18 would not, with a row that rebuilds the provider and drives a summon afterwards.

### DW-65: the `GlobalHotkey` port gained a member the spine's AD-9 block does not show

Declared **fields** of `HotkeyRegistration`, `HotkeyBinding` and the cases of `HotkeyBindOutcome` are untouched, so this is a member-level addition of exactly the class story 4 made when it gave those types value equality (DW-7) — recorded rather than hand-edited into the spine, following the convention stories 1, 2, 4, 7 and 9 used. It is typed `Stream<HotkeyBindOutcome>` rather than `Stream<HotkeyRegistration>` because the signal carries two different facts, a rebind and a drop, and the sealed pair already models both — so AD-12's degradation reaches the screen through the same value the initial bind uses.

### DW-66: a Wayland `effective` is still never a combination, so AD-10's *display* rule is honoured by a sentence rather than a read-back

origin: story 10-settings-ui.md, 2026-08-11
location: lib/src/ui/settings/hotkey_status_view.dart, lib/src/infrastructure/hotkey/wayland_portal_global_hotkey.dart
severity: low
reason: The story-8 entry "AD-10's 'displays the effective combination read back from the portal' is unimplementable through `HotkeyRegistration`" asked for either a third field on the type or a second value beside it. This story built neither and does not need to: the screen renders "This backend cannot report the combination in effect", which is AD-10's own `effective: null` vocabulary turned into a user sentence, and `_onShortcutsChanged` pushes the same `effective: null` upward. So what is **closed** is the half about having nowhere to put the fact; what stays **open** is the underlying measurement — the portal returns only a localized `trigger_description`, nothing parses it back into a binding, and therefore a Wayland user never sees their combination in this app. Closing it needs a machine-readable trigger from the portal or a deliberate decision to display the localized description as text (which would be the first place in this codebase where vendor prose is shown to a user). The companion story-8 entry — "AD-11 mandates the `ShortcutsChanged` subscription but the port has no way to push a changed registration upward" — is closed by construction by DW-65 and its C1/C2 rows; closing it with `status: done` is the orchestrator's call, following DW-4's convention.
status: done 2026-09-02
resolution: 2026-09-02 Closed by plan 01-06 (`57749dc`, `47da214`, `810aac7`). **This entry's own `decision:` line (2026-08-14) already ratified the approach taken** — "Render the localized description ... clearly labelled as the desktop's own wording rather than this app's ... Parsing the phrase back was rejected as silently wrong on a translated string" — so what is recorded here is **agreement with a standing decision, not a new choice.** What landed under it: the portal's `trigger_description` is now carried *out* of the adapter instead of only logged, on `HotkeyStatus.backendDescription` (`lib/src/domain/hotkey/hotkey_status.dart`), cached from three sources — the `BindShortcuts` read-back (after the discard check, never before it), every `ShortcutsChanged` that still holds this app's shortcut id, and a new explicit `ListShortcuts` re-read routed through `_callThroughRequest` — and reaches the screen through `SettingsState.hotkeyBackendDescription`, rendered on a line of its own under a line naming whose words it is, never interpolated into an app-authored sentence. `effective` stays **null** on Wayland and all three `effective: null` construction sites survive (`lib/src/infrastructure/hotkey/wayland_portal_global_hotkey.dart`, verified `grep -n 'effective: null' | grep -v '///'` -> 3): no field of any GlobalShortcuts reply carries a machine-readable combination, so nothing was synthesised and nothing is parsed — `grep -nE 'split\(|RegExp\(|parse\(' ` over the adapter matches nowhere. So the underlying measurement this entry held open is answered the only way the protocol allows: a Wayland user now sees the compositor's own wording for what is in effect, which is not the same thing as seeing their combination, and the screen says which it is. `ListShortcuts` has exactly one production caller — a bind the compositor granted whose read-back carried no wording — because an unconditional call would add a fifth step to AD-11's fixed four-step sequence, and its failure is a log line rather than a refusal: the shortcut is already bound and failing a bind over the wording for it would take a working hotkey away over a sentence. **Owed and NOT closed:** every Wayland claim here is from the specification and the fake portal, never from a run — this container has no session bus, no `xdg-desktop-portal` and no compositor. Three human checks stay owed (`.planning/phases/01-hotkey-truth/01-06-SUMMARY.md`; filed in `.planning/WINDOWS.md`), and the `ListShortcuts` re-read in particular is **unexercised** because the fake portal always sends a description, so the branch that calls it never runs in the suite. Also amended rather than closed: the regime label this entry's neighbourhood assumes is **gone from the UI** per D-03, which amends AD-10's ratified display rule at `ARCHITECTURE-SPINE.md:620` — filed for Phase 7 as its own open entry appended 2026-09-02.
decision: 2026-08-14 Render the localized description -- the portal's trigger_description is displayed on the settings screen, clearly labelled as the desktop's own wording rather than this app's. Noted in the widget doc as the first place vendor prose reaches a user. Parsing the phrase back was rejected as silently wrong on a translated string.

### DW-67: `effective: null` still cannot distinguish an honoured bind from an abandoned rebind — the screen narrows it, on X11 only

origin: story 10-settings-ui.md, 2026-08-11
location: lib/src/ui/settings/hotkey_status_view.dart (`_effectiveLines`), lib/src/infrastructure/hotkey/wayland_portal_global_hotkey.dart (`_closeSessionBeforeRebinding`)
severity: medium
reason: Two filed entries — story 7's X11 abandoned rebind and its Wayland twin — describe an outcome that is accurate field by field and silent about the user's request. Both were filed with the note that the fix is "a controller-side comparison … decisions for the surface that renders them". The surface exists now and does the comparison: it shows the effective combination, the stored preference, and a "That differs from your preference, X" line when `HotkeyBinding`'s set-based `==` says they differ (pinned by two rows, one per authority, and by mutation gate 1). That closes the **X11** case: an abandoned rebind there carries the previous combination, so the difference is visible. It does **not** close the Wayland case, which is strictly worse and was filed as such: there `effective` is null on every path, so a bind the compositor accepted and a rebind abandoned because `Session.Close` was refused produce the byte-identical value `HotkeyBound(HotkeyRegistration(effective: null, authority: compositor))` and no comparison is possible at all. The screen says the backend cannot report the combination, which is true and is all that can be said. Closing it needs the third arm on the outcome, or DW-66's read-back.
status: done 2026-08-14
decision: 2026-08-14 Close -- subsumed by DW-66 -- engineering call, contingent on the human's DW-66 answer. Once the settings screen renders the portal's own trigger_description, a Wayland user reads the combination actually in effect after any rebind, so the honoured-vs-abandoned distinction is no longer invisible to them -- and AD-9's HotkeyBindOutcome shape is not widened for it.
resolution: closed by engineering decision 2026-08-14, contingent on DW-66: no arm is added to HotkeyBindOutcome. The three `effective: null` constructions in wayland_portal_global_hotkey.dart (:340-344, :871-878, :969-974) stay as they are; DW-66's rendering of the portal's trigger_description gives the user the live answer instead.

### DW-68: the `SettingsController` mutation-generation race and the bind-before-write divergence are now user-reachable; this story narrowed one and rendered the other

origin: story 10-settings-ui.md, 2026-08-11
location: lib/src/application/settings_controller.dart (`changeHotkey`), lib/src/ui/settings/settings_screen.dart (`_mutating`)
severity: medium
reason: Both entries were filed against a controller with no caller. There is a caller now, so both are reachable by a user. What this story did about each, exactly. **The race** — two overlapping `changeHotkey` calls resolving last-completion-wins, with a portal dialog making the window seconds wide by design — is narrowed to the cases a user cannot cause from this screen: `_mutating` gates the callbacks and disables Apply, the modifier chips, the key field and the preset list while a mutation is in flight, pinned by three rows (in-flight disabled, two presses in one flight are one mutation, a failed mutation re-enables) and by mutation gate 13. It is **not closed**: a config file edited on disk, a second surface, or a `bindingChanges` emission arriving mid-mutation can still interleave, and the controller still has no generation stamp. The single-`failure`-slot variant the race subsumes is untouched. **The bind-before-write divergence** — a refused write leaving the shortcut already changed while the surface says nothing was saved — is not fixed either, and is now *visible* rather than silent: the screen renders the returned `effective` beside the stored preference and says they differ, so the user sees "In effect: Alt+Space", "That differs from your preference, Ctrl+Shift+G" and the write-failure sentence together, which is three true statements describing exactly what happened. That is an improvement in honesty, not a fix; the ordering decision (write first and bind on success, or unbind after a failed write) is still owed.
status: done 2026-09-26
resolution: Plan 02-02 makes `SettingsController.changeHotkey` persist the backend's structured effective binding, retaining the previous working shortcut after a refused rebind, and serializes external config edits through disk refresh and retry in `JsonConfigStore`. This closes the named divergence by effective-binding persistence rather than the earlier write-first/rollback decision. DW-84's 2026-08-14 done status was explicitly contingent on DW-68; this closure satisfies that dependency for the refused-before-backend case. Wayland localized descriptions are not parsed into structured bindings, and native watcher/rebind behavior was not observed.
decision: 2026-08-14 Write first, bind on success -- engineering call. SettingsController.changeHotkey persists the preference first and binds only on a successful write, rolling the write back if the bind then fails, so the stored preference and the grabbed combination cannot diverge. This also closes DW-84's permanently-refused-key case.

### DW-69: a settings rebind still never reaches the tray, and now there is a second surface that knows better

origin: story 10-settings-ui.md, 2026-08-11
location: lib/src/application/settings_controller.dart, lib/src/domain/tray/tray_port.dart (`setHotkeyUnavailable`), lib/main.dart
severity: medium
reason: AD-12 names two consumers of an unavailable hotkey. The startup path tells both as of this story — `DaemonStartup.bindHotkey` calls `setHotkeyUnavailable` and `main.dart` now hands the same outcome to the settings controller — but nothing after startup does. A user who changes the hotkey to something the backend refuses, or whose desktop drops the shortcut (a `ShortcutsChanged` this story newly delivers), gets a settings screen stating unavailability while the tray icon still says hotkeys are available. Before this story the gap was invisible because neither surface was reachable from a rebind; now exactly one of the two is right. Not fixed here because this story's Never list rules out all tray work, and the fix is a decision about who owns the fan-out: `SettingsController` calling `TrayPort` would give the settings surface a second port and a second responsibility, while a composition-root subscription to `SettingsController.changes` (or to `GlobalHotkey.bindingChanges`, which is now the honest source for the compositor-initiated half) keeps both surfaces downstream of one value. The second shape is the better one and belongs with whoever owns the composition root's wiring.
status: done 2026-09-26
resolution: Plan 02-03 keeps `SettingsController` as the binding-change subscriber and forwards its typed hotkey status through `DaemonGraph` to `TrayPort` after startup, including refusal and restoration. Source inspection and the existing Settings/tray suites support the fan-out. A retained Wayland bind refusal without a typed refusal signal still has no temporary tray refusal line (WINDOWS entry 41); no live StatusNotifier host was observed.
decision: 2026-08-14 Fan out from the composition root — engineering call, taking the shape this entry itself argues is the better one. Verified: `grep -rn setHotkeyUnavailable lib/` finds exactly one caller, `daemon_startup.dart:184` inside `bindHotkey`, which runs once at startup; `SettingsController` holds no `TrayPort` and nothing under `lib/src/application/` names `trayProvider`. The composition root subscribes to one value — `SettingsController.changes` for an app-initiated rebind, `GlobalHotkey.bindingChanges` for a compositor-initiated one — and drives both AD-12 surfaces from it. `SettingsController` acquiring a second port is rejected: it gives the settings surface a second responsibility and leaves two independent writers of one fact, which is the state that produced this entry. Closes DW-79's fan-out half; DW-79 owns the row that proves the two surfaces agree.

### DW-70: CAP-8's restart requirement is now stated on screen, which makes DW-16 a documentation decision rather than an invisible gap

origin: story 10-settings-ui.md, 2026-08-11
location: lib/src/ui/settings/preset_choice_list.dart, lib/main.dart (`_container`), lib/src/application/settings_controller.dart (`changeActivePreset`)
severity: low
reason: DW-16 records that the active `(provider, preset)` pair is fixed at startup while `changeActivePreset`'s own doc says it "[s]witches which preset — and so which provider — serves the next correction". DW-16 named two ways out: derive the pair from `configStoreProvider` so a config change rebuilds `CorrectionController`, or document a restart requirement "on `changeActivePreset` and in the settings UI (story 10)". This story took the second, on screen: the preset section always states that a change is saved straight away and that the app keeps using the previous provider and model until it is restarted (A16). So the user is no longer misled — but CAP-8's success criterion says switching "changes which backend serves the next correction", and a restart is a weaker reading of that than the immediacy the wording suggests. DW-16 stays open for the first option, and note what taking it would cost: making `activePresetProvider` mutable rebuilds `correctionControllerProvider`, which is exactly the condition DW-62 says the panel is not written for — the panel would be left bound to a disposed controller. The two must be settled together. `changeActivePreset`'s own doc comment is still the pre-story wording and was left alone deliberately (the story's Code Map does not open it).
status: done 2026-08-14
resolution: skipped by the 2026-08-14 sweep triage: Superseded by DW-16. The half this entry describes as done has shipped -- preset_choice_list.dart states on screen that a preset change is saved immediately and takes effect after a restart -- and the remaining question, whether CAP-8 needs the immediacy its wording implies, is DW-16's and is triaged there as a decision, together with the DW-62 interaction this entry correctly flags.

### DW-71: the key field is un-curated, so the seven divergent labels are offered by name on both display servers

origin: story 10-settings-ui.md, 2026-08-11
location: lib/src/ui/settings/hotkey_preference_field.dart, lib/src/infrastructure/hotkey/hotkey_key_catalogue.dart (`labelsThatBindTheWrongKey`)
severity: medium
reason: The screen takes the key as free text and reports whatever the adapter answers, which is deliberate and is what the story's Block If required: `HotkeyKeyCatalogue` lives two rings out and AD-1 puts it beyond the ui ring's reach, and the seven labels that bind on Wayland while being refused on X11 (DW-43, and the story-8 entry on cross-display-server portability) are an open product decision. The consequence, now that a user can type into that field: on X11 `Space`, `Tab`, `Enter` and `F1`–`F4` come back `HotkeyUnavailable` and the screen states it honestly, while on Wayland the same labels are accepted as a preference — so the same config means different things on the two display servers and the screen cannot warn about it. Nothing here tells the user which labels this build can register at all: a typo (`Spce`) and a genuinely unbindable key (`Space` on X11) produce the same class of answer, one attempt later. Closing it means either exposing the catalogue through a port the ui ring may read, refusing the seven on both display servers, or presenting the per-display-server difference — all three are product decisions this story's intent does not carry. Related: the bare-printable-key entry story 7 filed ("a hotkey configured with no modifiers and an ordinary typing key takes a system-wide grab"), which this screen now offers directly, since every modifier chip can be turned off.
status: done 2026-09-02
resolution: 2026-09-02 Closed by plan 01-07 (`d22851b`, `a2f2189`). **This entry's own `decision:` line (2026-08-14) already ratified the mechanism** — "Expose the catalogue through a port ... supplied by SettingsController rather than reached for across AD-1's rings ... validated before Apply" — so this closure records **agreement**, not a new choice. What landed under it: the key vocabulary is a domain value `RegistrableKeys` (`lib/src/domain/hotkey/registrable_keys.dart`) behind a `registrableKeysProvider` seam, overridden in `lib/main.dart` with `HotkeyKeyCatalogue.registrableKeys()`, arriving on `SettingsController.captureValidator` as a field — the ui ring reads a sanctioned application-ring value and never reaches across AD-1's rings. **Two things changed under that decision and are recorded rather than glossed.** (1) *The per-display-server difference the `decision:` line says to state "where it applies" no longer exists.* The seven divergent labels were an artefact of the removed vendor package's own reverse lookup — `hotkey_manager` sent `physicalKey.keyCode`, which `uni_platform` resolved by scanning Flutter's `kGtkToLogicalKey` for the *first* entry whose value was the logical key, and that table lists the keypad/ISO/3270 variants first — and not of X11, keybinder or GTK. Measured three ways on 2026-09-01 against the installed `libX11.so.6`, `libgdk-3.so.0` and `libgtk-3.so.0` plus a keycode round trip on a live `Xvfb`: `XStringToKeysym("space")` -> 0x20, `"Tab"` -> 0xff09, `"Return"` -> 0xff0d, `"F1"` -> 0xffbe, `"F4"` -> 0xffc1, and `gtk_accelerator_parse("<Control><Shift>space")` -> keyval=0x0020 — no `KP_Space`, no `ISO_Left_Tab`, no `3270_Enter`, no `KP_F1`-`KP_F4` anywhere. With the plugin gone and `X11KeyGrabRegistrar` resolving keys by keysym *name*, all seven bind correctly on X11, so `Space`, `Tab`, `Enter` and `F1`-`F4` mean the same thing on both display servers. `labelsThatBindTheWrongKey` and `bindsTheWrongKey` were **removed outright rather than left as an empty set** — an always-false predicate leaves branches that can never run, which AGENTS.md §1 rules out — and the measurement survives as a doc paragraph at `lib/src/infrastructure/hotkey/hotkey_key_catalogue.dart:120-146`. (2) *The validation moved from Apply to capture (D-15), which is earlier than the `decision:` line says.* The free-text key field and its four modifier toggles are gone: `hotkey_capture_field.dart` (516 lines) replaces `hotkey_preference_field.dart` (260 lines, deleted), the user presses the combination, and `event.physicalKey.usbHidUsage` — the same integer the key table and the grab already use — is the lookup. A five-subject validator in the application ring (`lib/src/application/hotkey_capture.dart`) refuses each unbindable combination **at capture** with its own user-facing reason, so a typo and a genuinely unbindable key no longer produce the same class of answer one attempt later — which is the specific consequence this entry's `reason:` names. Apply still requests whatever the control displays, so re-applying a stored binding stays the recovery path the adapter suite relies on; only a *capture* can be refused. **Live evidence, unusually strong for this phase:** six of seven human checks were OBSERVED against a real daemon on `Xvfb :78` driven with `xdotool` and read off screenshots — including Ctrl+Tab captured rather than eaten by focus traversal, which the vendor plugin used to mis-bind. That session also settled flagged assumption **A3** (AltGr refusal). **Owed and NOT closed:** one row — read-only-while-binding — because an X11 grab resolves in microseconds and there is no portal here to park a bind on; and `F1`-`F4` were not exercised on a real X session at all, so the "all seven now bind correctly" claim is measured at the keysym layer and live for `Space`/`Tab`/`Enter` only (`.planning/phases/01-hotkey-truth/01-07-SUMMARY.md`, coverage id D8 rationale; filed in `.planning/WINDOWS.md`). Related and untouched: the bare-printable-key entry this one cites is answered in the validator's own D-13 subject, but its ledger entry is outside this plan's scope and keeps its existing status.
decision: 2026-08-14 Expose the catalogue through a port -- engineering call, taking the non-destructive option. The ui ring gets a sanctioned way to read which labels this build can register -- supplied by SettingsController rather than reached for across AD-1's rings -- validated before Apply, with the per-display-server difference stated where it applies. Refusing the seven keys on Wayland too was rejected: it removes a capability from Wayland users to buy symmetry.

### DW-72: the settings affordance overlays the panel's top-right corner, so a pointer landing there reaches it and not the editor

origin: story 10-settings-ui.md, 2026-08-11
location: lib/src/ui/daemon_home.dart (`_SettingsAffordance`, the `Positioned` overlay), lib/src/ui/panel/correction_panel.dart
severity: low
reason: The affordance is overlaid rather than placed in the panel's layout, and the reason is sound: the panel carries a measured height floor and six layout rows asserting where its two panes sit (DW-53, DW-58, DW-61), and a toolbar or button row above or inside it would move every rect those rows assert. The cost is that a small region of the editor's top-right corner is covered — the editor's `TextField` spans the full width, so a click there focuses nothing and activates the settings button instead. Bounded in practice (a compact 18 px icon in the corner, and the editor is autofocused on every summon so a pointer is rarely needed) and unmeasurable here: this container has no display, so how the overlay actually looks and where it actually lands on a real window is unobserved (DW-50 — nothing sizes the toplevel at all). Whoever picks the window's geometry should look at this at the same time; the honest alternatives are an inset that clears the field, or a panel layout that reserves the corner and re-measures the floor.
status: done 2026-09-26
resolution: Plan 02-06 moves Settings to a reserved gutter beside the editor, or a separate header below 200 logical pixels, in `lib/src/ui/daemon_home.dart` and `correction_panel.dart`. Existing layout/widget tests and source inspection show non-overlapping bounds; no native pointer hit test was run.
decision: 2026-08-14 Inset the editor, keep the overlay — engineering call. Verified: `daemon_home.dart:177-183` places `_SettingsAffordance` in a `Positioned(top: 0, right: 0)` over a `StackFit.expand` `CorrectionPanel`, and the editor's `TextField` spans the full width beneath it, so a pointer in that corner activates the settings button and focuses nothing. The overlay stays — a toolbar or button row above or inside the panel would move every rect the layout rows assert and put the next maintainer in the position of "adjusting" a constant measured against CAP-10 — and the editor pane instead gains a top-right inset that clears the icon, re-measured against the floor in the same change. The spine's Deferred section puts panel spacing with the widgets that own it, so this needs no design ruling. How it actually lands on a real window is a step on DW-9's checklist, together with DW-50's geometry, which is what makes the corner's real size knowable.

### DW-73: mutation gate 5's stated expectation was not achievable, and the reason generalises

origin: story 10-settings-ui.md, 2026-08-11
location: _bmad-output/specs/spec-hotkey-grammar-corrector/stories/10-settings-ui.md (Verification, mutation gate 5), test/infrastructure/hotkey/wayland_portal_global_hotkey_test.dart, test/ui/settings/settings_screen_hotkey_test.dart
severity: low
reason: The story's gate 5 — "Emit `HotkeyBound` instead of `HotkeyUnavailable` when the id is no longer held — C2 and A9 must fail" — kills C2 and cannot kill A9. A9 is a screen row: it drives `FakeGlobalHotkey.emitBindingChange(HotkeyUnavailable(...))` and asserts what the screen renders, so no edit to the Wayland adapter is reachable from it, by design — the port is the seam between them and a widget test that could observe an adapter mutation would mean the seam was not there. Every other gate met or exceeded its stated expectation (gate 1 needed a second A1 row, added in this pass, because the original could not tell `effective` from `config.hotkeyBinding` where the two are equal — which is the gate doing its job). Recorded because the general point outlives this story: a mutation gate that names rows on both sides of a port seam is asserting something the seam exists to prevent, and the honest form is one gate per side.
status: done 2026-08-14
resolution: skipped by the 2026-08-14 sweep triage: A record about a completed story's verification text. Story 10 is done and 10-settings-ui.md:289 ('C2 and A9 must fail') will not be re-run, so there is nothing in this repo to change. The general point -- a mutation gate that names rows on both sides of a port seam asserts something the seam exists to prevent, and the honest form is one gate per side -- belongs to whoever authors the next gate, not to this codebase.

### DW-76: DW-68 overstated the narrowing — the in-flight gate was on the widget, and this story's own view swaps destroyed it

origin: story 10-settings-ui.md review pass, 2026-08-11
location: lib/src/application/settings_controller.dart (`_beginMutation`), lib/src/application/settings_state.dart (`mutationInFlight`), lib/src/ui/settings/settings_screen.dart
severity: medium
reason: DW-68 claimed the mutation-generation race was "narrowed to the cases a user cannot cause from this screen". That was false as written, and the review pass measured it: the flag lived on `_SettingsScreenState`, and both exits this story itself created unmount that state — the always-enabled Back affordance, and the `isFreshSession` swap a summon triggers. Apply → Back → affordance → Apply produced `bindCalls = 2, writes = 2` with the first bind still parked on the portal dialog, so the race was reachable in three taps. Filed as a new entry rather than by editing DW-68, following the convention DW-46 used for DW-15. **What is closed now:** the slot lives on `SettingsController`, which outlives every surface; a second mutation while one is in flight is refused rather than queued and logged at info level; `SettingsState.mutationInFlight` carries it so a remounted screen reads it instead of guessing; and four rows pin it (Back→reopen→Apply, summon→reopen→Apply, two presses in one flight, and the state-equality row that stops the announcement frame being deduped away). **What is still open, and is what DW-68 should have said:** the controller has no generation stamp, so an interleaving that does not come through this screen is still last-completion-wins — a config file edited on disk, a second surface, or a `bindingChanges` emission arriving mid-mutation. Refusing also means a user whose first mutation is parked on a dialog cannot issue a second at all until it resolves, which is the right trade for correctness and is a real cost worth knowing.
status: open
decision: 2026-08-14 Add the generation stamp — engineering call. Verified: the slot lives on the controller (`_beginMutation`, `settings_controller.dart:214-230`), a second mutation is refused rather than queued, and `SettingsState.mutationInFlight` carries it so a remounted screen reads it instead of guessing — all of which is what this entry records as closed. What is still open is what it says: no generation stamp, so an interleaving that does not come through the settings screen is last-completion-wins. A mutation carries a generation and a completion whose generation is no longer current is discarded, which covers a config file edited on disk, a second surface, and a `bindingChanges` arriving mid-mutation. Refusal stays the policy for a second mutation from the screen — that trade is right and its cost is already documented — and the stamp is what makes the *other* writers safe rather than a replacement for it. Settles DW-77 in the same change, as that entry asks.

### DW-77: the counted echo bookkeeping in `SettingsController` is no longer reachable from outside the class

origin: story 10-settings-ui.md review pass, 2026-08-11
location: lib/src/application/settings_controller.dart (`_ownWrites`, `_expectEcho`, `_forgetEcho`), test/application/settings_controller_test.dart
severity: low
reason: `_ownWrites` is a `Map<AppConfig, int>` rather than a `Set<AppConfig>` because two overlapping mutations can write *equal* configs — setting the same preset twice, or a hotkey change that resolves to what config already held — and a plain set collapses them, so the first echo retires the entry and the second is misread as somebody else editing the file. That was pinned by a row driving two un-awaited overlapping mutations. DW-76 removes the premise: one mutation is in flight at a time now, so two own-writes can no longer be outstanding simultaneously through the public API, and the row was rewritten to drive them sequentially. The counting is still correct and still cheap, but its multiplicity half is defensive code that no row pins and no caller can reach — recorded rather than simplified to a `Set`, because the reachable-again condition is one queueing decision away (if a later story queues mutations instead of refusing them, the counting is load-bearing again and would have to be put back). Whoever settles the generation stamp DW-76 names should decide this at the same time.
status: done 2026-09-26
resolution: Plan 02-04 replaces `Map<AppConfig, int>` with a `Set<AppConfig>` in `lib/src/application/settings_controller.dart` because `_beginMutation` refuses overlapping mutations. The existing Settings suite checks own-write echo suppression and later external refresh. This follows the phase spec's explicit set/removal decision and supersedes the earlier keep-counting decision above; no native watcher event was observed.
decision: 2026-08-14 Keep the counting — engineering call, decided together with DW-76 exactly as this entry asks. Verified: `_ownWrites` is a `Map<AppConfig, int>` (`settings_controller.dart:111`) with `_expectEcho`/`_forgetEcho` at :591-609, and DW-76's in-flight slot does remove the premise, so the multiplicity half is unreachable through the public API today. It stays a map rather than collapsing to a `Set`: the generation stamp does not restore multiplicity, but queueing mutations instead of refusing them would, and that is one decision away — a map that costs nothing is cheaper than a correctness regression relitigated later. What makes this honest rather than dead code is that the doc says it is defensive and says why, which AGENTS.md §1 is satisfied by; the rule it must not become is defensive code with no stated reason.

### DW-78: an unapplied hotkey draft is discarded by every view swap

origin: story 10-settings-ui.md review pass, 2026-08-11
location: lib/src/ui/daemon_home.dart, lib/src/ui/settings/hotkey_preference_field.dart (`didUpdateWidget`)
severity: low
reason: Typing `F12` into the key field and then summoning the panel — or losing focus, which CAP-14 hides on — unmounts the settings screen, and reopening it shows the stored combination again. Measured. The behaviour is deliberate and matches AD-18's rule for the panel ("every show starts fresh"), and it is now stated in `DaemonHome`'s class doc rather than left implicit, because the doc previously justified the swap with "the panel's content survives because it lives in the controller" and was silent about the asymmetry: an unapplied draft lives nowhere but the widget, by design, since a combination the config file does not hold is not a setting. What is worth deciding deliberately is whether it *should* survive. Keeping it means the draft is state with an owner — either the controller (a fourth thing on `SettingsState` that is explicitly not a setting) or a widget that outlives the swap — and it interacts with the field's own re-seed rule, which currently follows the config file whenever it changes. Nobody has asked for it; it is filed so the next person who notices the loss finds a decision rather than a surprise.
status: done 2026-09-26
resolution: Plan 02-11 makes the discard visible: a Settings view swap or focus loss remounts `HotkeyPreferenceField` from the current structured effective binding, while panel text remains in `CorrectionController`. The phase spec explicitly chose visible discard, superseding the earlier keep-draft decision above. Source inspection and existing widget tests support the field reset; Wayland's localized-only description cannot supply a structured effective combination, and native event ordering was not observed.
decision: 2026-08-14 Keep the draft -- engineering call, decided against the entry's own lean and for consistency with the human's DW-30 answer: unapplied typing must not silently vanish on a view swap. DW-81 makes the settings-to-panel swap a more common path, so the discard gets easier to hit. The draft gets an owner that outlives the swap and is re-seeded from config only when the config actually changes, with rows for summon-and-return and Back-and-reopen.

reason: The sharper half of DW-69, and it is worth its own entry because it names what no test checks. `DaemonStartup.bindHotkey` is the only caller of `TrayPort.setHotkeyUnavailable`, and it runs once. Every later way the hotkey can become unavailable now reaches the settings screen and nothing else: a rebind the backend refuses (`changeHotkey` answering `HotkeyUnavailable`), and a `ShortcutsChanged` whose list no longer holds the shortcut, which this story is what made deliverable at all. So a user whose desktop drops the shortcut sees the settings screen say global shortcuts are unavailable while the tray icon still shows the available artwork and its menu still asserts the hotkey works. Nothing in the suite pins that the two agree — not at startup either: `daemon_startup_test.dart` pins the tray call, the settings rows pin the screen, and no row compares them. The wiring is DW-69's single-value fan-out. ### DW-80: on a real Wayland session the new `ShortcutsChanged` emission is a visible no-op

origin: story 10-settings-ui.md review pass, 2026-08-11
location: lib/src/infrastructure/hotkey/wayland_portal_global_hotkey.dart (`_onShortcutsChanged`), test/ui/settings/settings_screen_hotkey_test.dart (A8)
severity: medium
reason: AD-10 asks that "a rebind made in the compositor updates the UI", and the port member added by this story satisfies it as a *state change*. What it does not do on a real session is change anything the user can see. The rebind branch emits `HotkeyBound(HotkeyRegistration(effective: null, authority: compositor))`, which is byte-identical to what a Wayland bind already produced (DW-66: the portal returns only a localized `trigger_description` and nothing parses it), so the screen re-renders the same sentence — "your desktop owns this shortcut … this backend cannot report the combination in effect" — before and after. The A8 row *looks* like it proves a visible update only because `FakeGlobalHotkey` is scripted with a non-null `effective`, which no shipped Wayland adapter can produce. Two things follow. The mechanism is real and is what AD-12's degradation now travels on (the *drop* branch does change the screen, which A9 and C2 pin, and that is the case a user actually needs). And AD-10's display half stays owed until DW-66 is settled: until the portal gives a machine-readable trigger, or the localized description is deliberately rendered as text, a Wayland user cannot see their own combination in this app at all — before or after a compositor rebind.
status: done 2026-08-14
resolution: skipped by the 2026-08-14 sweep triage: Subsumed by DW-66. The mechanism is real and verified: wayland_portal_global_hotkey.dart:871-878 emits on the rebind branch and the drop branch does change the screen (pinned by A9 and C2). What makes the rebind branch invisible on a real session is exactly DW-66's `effective: null` gap -- the portal supplies only a localized trigger_description and nothing parses it -- and that is triaged as a decision on DW-66.

### DW-81: the hotkey pressed while the settings screen is showing hides the window instead of returning to the panel

origin: story 10-settings-ui.md follow-up review pass, 2026-08-11
location: lib/src/application/panel_controller.dart (`onHotkeyActivated`), lib/src/ui/daemon_home.dart
severity: medium
reason: `DaemonHome` now returns to the panel on a fresh session or a show request, so every route *in* — hotkey summon of a hidden window, tray, second launch, window restore — ends at the panel. A press against a window that is already visible is not a route in: `onHotkeyActivated` takes the AD-8 hide arm, the window vanishes, and `_showingSettings` stays true, so the user's next press raises the panel and the settings view is gone. That is consistent with CAP-14's toggle contract and nothing is lost (the draft discard is DW-78's), which is why it was not patched unattended — but it means a user sitting on the settings screen who presses the hotkey wanting the correction panel needs two presses and a disappearing window in between, and the toggle is the most likely way out of settings for a surface summoned by a hotkey. Deciding it is a product call: either a press while a non-panel view is showing means "bring me the panel" (the toggle asks the home view first) or the window toggle stays a pure window toggle and the two-press path is the accepted cost. Both reviewers who raised it rated the intent genuinely ambiguous. Nothing in the suite covers the case in either direction.
status: done 2026-09-26
resolution: Plan 02-11 makes `DaemonHome` synchronously report Settings visibility to `PanelController`; a hotkey there requests the panel, while a hotkey on the panel keeps the ordinary hide toggle. Source inspection and existing widget tests support the view transition; native focus and window-event ordering were not observed.
decision: 2026-08-14 The toggle asks the home view first -- a hotkey press while a non-panel view is showing returns to the panel instead of hiding the window; a press while the panel itself is showing still takes the AD-8 hide arm. Both directions get rows -- neither is covered today.

### DW-82: A6's "the tray menu still opens the panel" clause is a contract on adapter message text that nothing enforces

origin: story 10-settings-ui.md follow-up review pass, 2026-08-11
location: lib/src/ui/settings/hotkey_status_view.dart (`_unavailableLines`), lib/src/infrastructure/hotkey/x11_global_hotkey.dart, lib/src/infrastructure/hotkey/wayland_portal_global_hotkey.dart
severity: low
reason: The screen renders `HotkeyUnavailable.message` verbatim and appends nothing, which is AD-12's letter ("in the outcome's own words") and is also what stopped the tray clause printing twice. It leaves the clause depending entirely on every adapter remembering to end its sentence with it. Today they all do — measured across every `HotkeyUnavailable(` site in both adapters and `daemon_startup.dart` — and the widget's doc states the cost, but the A6 row proves it only for the one message it hard-codes as a fixture, so a new unavailable branch that omits the clause would ship a screen telling a user their hotkey is dead and nothing about the way in that still works. The `GlobalHotkey` port doc implies the contract ("in terms a user can act on") without stating this part of it. Closing it wants either the contract written into the port's doc and a gate over the adapters' unavailable messages, or the clause moved back onto the screen with the adapters' copies removed — the second is a wider edit than a review pass should make unattended, and the first is a source scan whose fragility (matching message literals to their constructor across `dart format` wrapping) is what kept it out of this pass.
status: open
decision: 2026-08-14 Write the contract into the port doc and gate it — engineering call, taking the narrower of the two options the entry offers. Verified: `hotkey_status_view.dart:126-130` renders the adapter's `message` verbatim and appends only the regime line, and every `HotkeyUnavailable(` site does today end with the tray clause — `x11_global_hotkey.dart:176`, `:245`, `:265`, `:302`, `daemon_startup.dart:222`, and the wayland adapter's sites. `GlobalHotkey`'s doc states that a `HotkeyUnavailable.message` must name the way in that still works, and an architecture row scans the adapters' unavailable messages for it, so a new branch that omits the clause fails a test instead of shipping a screen that tells a user their hotkey is dead and nothing about the route that is not. Moving the clause back onto the screen is rejected: the screen cannot know the tray *is* the remaining way in — on a session with no StatusNotifier host it is not — and only the adapter that failed has that context. Interacts with DW-114: once the menu also offers Quit the clause stays true, but the message set is what is under gate, so the two land in a known order.

### DW-84: a hotkey the X11 adapter refuses before the backend is still written to config, so a working session hides a next launch with no hotkey at all

origin: story 10-settings-ui.md follow-up review pass, 2026-08-11
location: lib/src/application/settings_controller.dart (`changeHotkey`), lib/src/infrastructure/hotkey/x11_global_hotkey.dart (`_refusedBeforeBackend`)
severity: medium
reason: `changeHotkey` awaits `_bind(binding)` and then writes `binding` to config unconditionally — the bind's answer decides what is *rendered*, never what is *stored*. On Wayland that is deliberate and right: the preference has to survive to the next launch, where the bind is re-attempted against a portal that may answer differently. On X11 the two refusals that never reach the backend are neither transient nor re-attemptable: `usbHidUsageFor(label) == null` (a label outside `HotkeyKeyCatalogue` entirely) and `bindsTheWrongKey(label)` (DW-43's seven) are settled facts about this build, and they will answer identically at every future launch. What makes it hard to notice is the honest half: `_refusedBeforeBackend` releases nothing and grabs nothing, so the previous combination stays grabbed and keeps working, and it correctly answers `HotkeyBound(effective: <previous>)` rather than claiming unavailability — the screen then shows the old combination in effect beside the new preference and says they differ, which is three true statements. The user reads a working hotkey and a stored preference, quits, and comes back to a daemon whose bind fails and whose tray and settings screen both say hotkeys are unavailable. Distinct from DW-68 (which is about a *write* that fails while the bind succeeded — the same ordering, the other direction) and from DW-71 (which is about the field being un-curated, not about what is persisted after a refusal). The bind-before-write ordering is what both entries leave open and what story 10's Never list explicitly ruled out re-architecting, so this is filed rather than patched. Closing it means either not persisting a binding whose refusal the port reports as permanent — which needs a way for the port to say "permanent", and the outcome type is AD-9-verbatim — or writing first and binding on success, which is the ordering decision DW-68 already owes. Nothing in the suite drives a refused-before-backend bind followed by a restart.
status: done 2026-08-14
decision: 2026-08-14 Close -- subsumed by DW-68 -- engineering call, contingent on DW-68. Under write-first-bind-on-success the write is rolled back when the bind fails, so a refusal that never reaches the backend leaves nothing in the config file -- which is exactly this entry's ask, reached without widening HotkeyUnavailable or AD-9.
resolution: closed by engineering decision 2026-08-14, contingent on DW-68: no `permanent` field is added to HotkeyUnavailable. DW-68's reordering of settings_controller.dart:147-150 rolls back the config write when the bind fails, so the refused-before-backend case cannot leave a stored preference the next launch cannot grab. DW-68's build carries the restart row this entry asked for.

### DW-85: a mutation that resolves after the settings screen is unmounted reports its failure to nobody

origin: story 10-settings-ui.md follow-up review pass, 2026-08-11
location: lib/src/application/settings_controller.dart (`changeHotkey`, `changeActivePreset`), lib/src/ui/daemon_home.dart
severity: medium
reason: The mutation window is seconds wide by design on Wayland — AD-11 puts a portal dialog inside it — and this story built two ways to leave the screen while one is open: the Back affordance, and a summon returning to the panel (CAP-1, B3). Both unmount `SettingsScreen`. The controller outlives them and resolves correctly (`SettingsState.failure` is set, the slot is released, nothing sets state on a disposed `State` — pinned by the A20 rows), so nothing is lost internally; what is missing is any report. A user who presses Apply, gets summoned away by their own hotkey, and whose write then fails has been told nothing at all, and believes the change landed. The failure does survive in state, so reopening settings shows it — but with no indication of which change it belonged to or how old it is, and reopening is exactly what a user with no reason to suspect a problem will not do. The screen's own stated principle is that the failure notice is "the one thing that tells the user their change did not land"; on the path this story introduced, it is not shown. The A20 row asserts only that no exception escapes, which is a different property. Closing it is a product decision this pass should not make unattended: the candidates are a tray report (which story 10's Never list rules out — no `TrayPort` widening, and DW-79 already holds the tray/settings divergence), an announcement deferred until the surface next mounts (which needs a notion of unacknowledged failures the state does not have), or an explicit "accepted, not confirmed" contract stated on the screen before the user can leave. Related: DW-76, whose refusal rule means a user in this position also cannot issue a second mutation until the first resolves.
status: done 2026-08-14
decision: 2026-08-14 Close -- the failure survives in state -- the human chose to leave it as is. SettingsController resolves correctly and settings_screen.dart shows the failure on reopen; no deferred announcement and no accepted-not-confirmed notice are added.
resolution: closed by human decision 2026-08-14: the mid-mutation failure window is accepted. settings_controller.dart:613-626 sets SettingsState.failure and releases the slot with no exception escaping, and settings_screen.dart:65-74 renders it the next time the surface mounts. A user summoned away before a failing write is not told, and that is the accepted behaviour.
