location: linux/packaging/com.divertedriver.HotkeyGrammarCorrector.desktop, linux/packaging/autostart/com.divertedriver.HotkeyGrammarCorrector.desktop, tool/install_desktop_entries.sh, test/architecture/desktop_entries_test.dart
severity: medium
reason: AD-11 makes the app-id entry a precondition of the Wayland hotkey binding at all, and this story shipped it, aligned the three spellings of the app id (`WaylandPortalGlobalHotkey.applicationId`, `APPLICATION_ID` in linux/CMakeLists.txt, the file basename), and pinned the agreement by test. What it did not do — and could not, in a container with no compositor, no `xdg-desktop-portal`, no session bus and no `desktop-file-validate` — is observe any of the four things that matter on a real session: that GNOME associates the registered app id with the installed file; that the bind survives *because of* it; that the autostart entry starts the daemon at login; and that an autostarted daemon plus a manual launch resolve to one instance through AD-14's lock. `desktop_entries_test.dart` reads file contents and runs the installer into a temporary XDG tree — a check of bytes and paths. The static half is now strong enough that a regression in the *strings* fails a test; the runtime half is entirely owed. Closing it means one session on GNOME (and ideally one on KDE): install the entries, press the hotkey, log out and back in, then launch a second copy by hand. Related to DW-9, which owes the analogous "no display here" observation for the hidden startup window.
status: done 2026-08-14
resolution: resolved by sweep bundle dw-runtime-observation-checklists
decision: 2026-08-14 Add a runnable manual checklist -- engineering call. The four runtime facts stay unobservable here (Xvfb supplies no compositor, no xdg-desktop-portal and no session bus), so the owed observation becomes a checkable procedure committed next to test/platform/desktop_entries_live_test.dart -- install, press the hotkey, log out and back in, launch a second copy -- with an expected result per step.

### DW-88: `.github/workflows/ci.yml` has never been executed

origin: story 11-packaging-and-sidecar-provisioning.md, 2026-08-11
location: .github/workflows/ci.yml
severity: low
reason: The workflow was authored here — a GitHub remote exists (`origin` → Sebreiro/grammar-fixer-app) and the ledger has wanted the merge gate enforced since story 2 — but no runner was reachable from this container, so nothing about it is verified: not that `subosito/flutter-action@v2` resolves Flutter 3.44.8, not that `flutter pub get` succeeds on a clean runner, not that the scoped `dart test` passes there. Every claim in this story about CI is a claim about what the file says. The first push that triggers it is the verification, and a first-run fix should be expected rather than treated as a regression. Two known scope decisions ride along: `flutter test` (which carries every `test/ui` and `test/platform` row) is deliberately outside the gate, as is the `live` smoke; and DW-46's cross-suite flakes mean a red run may be a false red until that entry is closed, which is exactly what makes an unwatched gate a liability rather than an asset. The workflow also runs `tool/provision_sidecar.sh` before the suite, which is itself unverified on a runner: it is the step that keeps the five fake-CLI rows from skipping into a green result, and it is the step most likely to be what fails first, because it is the only one that installs anything.
status: open
decision: 2026-08-14 Make it runnable before the first push -- engineering call, chosen because it is actionable without an outward-facing push. ci.yml gets a workflow_dispatch trigger and a documented local dry-run reproducing the runner's steps in order -- especially tool/provision_sidecar.sh, the only step that installs anything -- so the workflow is exercised before it first runs for real.

### DW-89: the packaging format is still undecided, and Flatpak would change the AD-11 handshake

origin: story 11-packaging-and-sidecar-provisioning.md, 2026-08-11
location: ARCHITECTURE-SPINE.md (Deferred: "Packaging format"), lib/src/infrastructure/hotkey/wayland_portal_global_hotkey.dart (`Registry.Register`), linux/packaging/
severity: medium
reason: This story shipped desktop entries and an installer, which is what AD-11 and AD-14 require, and deliberately chose no distribution format — deb, Flatpak and AppImage all remain open, and the spine defers the choice. The record that must not be lost with it: **a sandboxed Flatpak build must not call `Registry.Register`.** AD-11 step 1 registers the app id with the host portal registry precisely because an unsandboxed app has no other way to tell the portal who it is; inside a Flatpak the sandbox already supplies the app id, and registering again is wrong. So the adapter's first portal call is conditional on a fact the packaging decision determines, and choosing Flatpak is not a build-system change but an edit to the AD-11 sequence plus a revisit of the invariant itself. Also unresolved by the format decision: `tool/install_desktop_entries.sh` writes into the user's XDG directories, which is right for a developer install and is not how any of the three formats ship entries. Closing this means picking a format, then revisiting AD-11 with the spine.
status: done 2026-09-01
resolution: 2026-09-01 The committed set is **three formats** — `.deb`/tarball, Flatpak, and AppImage — with no primary build and none second-class. **This supersedes this entry's own `decision:` line one line below.** That 2026-08-14 answer named four targets (Flatpak, .deb, AppImage, and a native Arch package via PKGBUILD/pacman); the 2026-09-01 answer governs, and the native Arch PKGBUILD is dropped from the committed set. Both records stand in this file — the older line is preserved verbatim and is not rewritten — so a later reader is not left to guess which of two ratified answers won: the three-format record is the operative one. The Flatpak permission set recorded for D-19 is the narrowest that works, and it is deliberately almost empty: `--socket=wayland`, `--socket=fallback-x11`, `--share=ipc`, and `--talk-name=org.freedesktop.secrets` (the last for Phase 6's credential only). **No `--filesystem=` grant of any kind is taken**, and no `--talk-name=org.freedesktop.Flatpak`. Two of D-19's three stated needs require no `finish-args` at all: `~/.var/app/$FLATPAK_ID` is writable by default, and `org.freedesktop.portal.*` bus names are allowed by default — so the **GlobalShortcuts portal needs no grant**. The consequence ARCH-02 exists to record, unchanged from the superseded line because Flatpak is in both sets: because Flatpak ships, `wayland_portal_global_hotkey.dart`'s host portal registry `Register` call becomes **conditional** on a sandbox predicate — a sandboxed build must not register, since the sandbox already supplies the app id, while an unsandboxed one must — so AD-11's step 1 is a variable in the sequence rather than a constant, and the spine owes that change (filed for Phase 7 rather than hand-edited here). Recorded as an **open cost on the deferred packaging phase and not resolved here**: the shipped default correction provider is three processes deep (daemon → Python sidecar → `claude` CLI) and the spine's envelope lists a Python 3.11+ interpreter with `claude_agent_sdk` plus the `claude` CLI on `PATH` as runtime dependencies the app cannot supply itself, neither of which is reachable inside a Flatpak sandbox — so a working Flatpak needs both bundled into the runtime, or a `flatpak-spawn --host` escape that would contradict D-19 outright. [ASSUMED] that no such bundling exists today: reasoned from `assets/sidecar/` shipping as a Flutter asset and `tool/provision_sidecar.sh` creating a `.venv-sidecar/` outside the bundle, not verified end to end. Filed as its own flat entry against the packaging phase rather than left inside this closure. Also [ASSUMED], as an unverified premise of the permission set: that the Flatpak runtime redirects `XDG_DATA_HOME`/`XDG_CONFIG_HOME` into `~/.var/app/$FLATPAK_ID`, so `AppPaths.fromEnvironment` needs no change — standard Flatpak behaviour, not verified against `AppPaths` in this tree; if wrong, a Flatpak build writes to an unwritable path. Unchanged and still true in all three formats: `tool/install_desktop_entries.sh` remains a developer install, not a packaging step. Building the pipelines stays deferred — this closure commits to formats, not to manifests.
decision: 2026-08-14 Pick a format now -- all four -- the human named four targets rather than one: Flatpak, .deb, AppImage, and a native Arch package (PKGBUILD/pacman) for a direct launch on Arch-based systems. This is materially larger than the entry's build option and is not an implementation-session task -- it is its own epic. The load-bearing consequence, recorded here so it cannot be lost: because Flatpak is in the set, wayland_portal_global_hotkey.dart's Registry.Register call becomes conditional on the packaging -- a sandboxed build must not register, an unsandboxed one must -- so AD-11's step 1 is now a variable in the sequence rather than a constant, and the spine owes that change. tool/install_desktop_entries.sh remains a developer install, not a packaging step, in all four.

The Stack table cites `assets/sidecar/requirements.txt` and `.github/workflows/ci.yml` instead of restating their numbers, so `sidecar_pin_drift_test.dart` becomes a check that the citation resolves rather than a comparison between two writable copies of one fact. ### DW-91: `SidecarFailure` fixed the traceback; the SDK's concurrent-aclose race is upstream and still there

origin: story 11-packaging-and-sidecar-provisioning.md, 2026-08-11
location: assets/sidecar/claude_agent_sdk_sidecar.py (`stream_correction`), .venv-sidecar claude_agent_sdk 0.2.132 `_internal/client.py` (`process_query`)
severity: low
reason: The observed defect — every provider error writing a `SystemExit` traceback plus `RuntimeError: aclose(): asynchronous generator is already running` into an all-day daemon's log — is fixed and pinned by a row in `sidecar_fake_cli_test.dart` that asserts stderr is empty. The fix needed two halves, and the second one is worth recording because it is not obvious from the code: raising `SidecarFailure` instead of calling `sys.exit()` inside the `async for` was **not sufficient on its own** (measured: the traceback still appeared, now with `SidecarFailure` in place of `SystemExit`). What removes it is the explicit `await messages.aclose()` in a `finally`, which closes the SDK generator deterministically instead of leaving it to `asyncio.run`'s `shutdown_asyncgens`. The underlying cause is upstream: `InternalClient.process_query` awaits `inner.aclose()` in its own `finally`, and when the loop shutdown hook closes both generators concurrently, the inner one is "already running". So the sidecar now avoids the race rather than the race being gone; a future SDK version could reintroduce it through a different path, and the guard against that is the stderr row, not the code. No action is required unless the row goes red. One arm of the fix is also unexercised: `close_quietly` suppresses whatever the close raises and tolerates an SDK object with no `aclose` at all — both guards exist because anything raised in that `finally` would *replace* the `SidecarFailure` on its way out, and `main()` would then report `providerError: RuntimeError: aclose(): ...` while the stderr row stayed green. Neither guard is driven by a harness row, because the harness controls the CLI's output and not the SDK's teardown; they were verified by driving `close_quietly` directly against a no-`aclose` object and an `aclose` that raises. A row would need a seam the SDK does not offer.
status: done 2026-08-14
resolution: skipped by the 2026-08-14 sweep triage: Nothing is owed. The fix shipped and is verified: assets/sidecar/claude_agent_sdk_sidecar.py:182-183 closes the SDK generator deterministically with `await close_quietly(messages)` in a finally, and the guard against an upstream reintroduction is the stderr-empty row at test/infrastructure/correction/claude_agent_sdk/sidecar_fake_cli_test.dart:61-76. The entry itself states no action is required unless that row goes red; the two unexercised close_quietly arms were verified by direct probe and would need a seam the SDK does not offer.

### DW-92: the fake-CLI harness depends on `claude_agent_sdk` internals, and the pin is what keeps it honest

origin: story 11-packaging-and-sidecar-provisioning.md, 2026-08-11
location: test/support/fake_claude_cli.dart, test/support/fake_claude_cli/claude.py
severity: low
reason: The harness reaches past the SDK's public surface in two places, both forced rather than chosen. It builds a `claude_agent_sdk` view of symlinks omitting `_bundled` and puts it first on `PYTHONPATH`, because `_find_bundled_cli()` is consulted before `shutil.which("claude")` and the linux wheel bundles its own ~282 MB CLI — a stub on `PATH` alone is never spawned. And `claude.py` answers the `initialize` control request by hand, because the SDK blocks up to 60 s on it before sending any prompt. Both are private protocol details of version 0.2.132. An SDK upgrade can break either one, and the failure would look like a hang or a timeout rather than like an obsolete assumption. Three things keep the cost low: the version is pinned and now drift-checked (DW-90), the harness touches nothing inside `.venv-sidecar`, and the `AD-19: the SDK spawned the stub` row fails loudly if discovery ever reaches a different binary. Recorded so that whoever bumps the pin knows to run these rows first and reads a hang as a discovery change.
status: done 2026-08-14
resolution: skipped by the 2026-08-14 sweep triage: A note for whoever bumps the pin rather than deferred work. Both private-protocol dependencies are verified still present and both are forced rather than chosen: test/support/fake_claude_cli.dart:254-266 builds the `_bundled`-free package view because the linux wheel's bundled CLI is consulted before PATH, and test/support/fake_claude_cli/claude.py:75-85 hand-answers the initialize control request because the SDK otherwise blocks up to 60 s. The version is pinned and drift-checked (DW-90), and the discovery row fails loudly on a change; nothing is buildable until an SDK bump is actually attempted.

### DW-93: the sidecar's `providerUnavailable` import guard is still only reachable through a `live`-tagged row

origin: story 11-packaging-and-sidecar-provisioning.md, 2026-08-11
location: assets/sidecar/claude_agent_sdk_sidecar.py (module-level import guard), test/infrastructure/correction/claude_agent_sdk/claude_agent_sdk_sidecar_live_test.dart
severity: low
reason: This story closed the two branches the ledger named — the error `result` and the zero-delta fallback — and both now run in the routine gate. The third non-happy branch did not move: the module-level `except ImportError` that emits `providerUnavailable` naming the package is exercised only by the second row of the `live`-tagged file, which is excluded from every default run and additionally skips unless a stock interpreter that *cannot* import `claude_agent_sdk` happens to exist at `/usr/bin/python3`. It is cheap to reach — it needs no CLI and no network, only a second interpreter — and it is the failure a user with an unprovisioned host actually hits, so it is the branch whose message quality matters most in the panel. It stayed put because moving it means deciding how a row acquires an SDK-free interpreter without installing or mutating anything, which this story's Never list forbids; the plausible answer is an empty `PYTHONPATH` view plus an isolated-mode interpreter, i.e. an extension of the harness DW-92 describes rather than new machinery. It is also the only `fail()` call site outside `main()`: the file has four in total — the module-level import guard on this path, and the three handlers in `main()` — so the import guard is the one place `sys.exit` runs before an event loop exists.
status: open
decision: 2026-08-14 Reach it with an isolated interpreter — engineering call, taking the extension this entry itself proposes over new machinery. Verified: the guard is `assets/sidecar/claude_agent_sdk_sidecar.py:83-84` — `except ImportError as error: fail("providerUnavailable", f"cannot import claude_agent_sdk: {error}")` — and it is the one `fail()` site that runs before an event loop exists, which is why `fail` is annotated `NoReturn` (:56-67). The row runs the shipped sidecar under `python3 -I` with an emptied `PYTHONPATH`, so `claude_agent_sdk` cannot be imported by an interpreter that would otherwise find it: no second interpreter, nothing installed, nothing mutated, and it moves out of the `live` tag into the routine gate. It is worth the row because it is the failure a user with an unprovisioned host actually hits, which makes its message the one whose quality matters most in the panel.

- source_spec: `_bmad-output/specs/spec-hotkey-grammar-corrector/stories/11-packaging-and-sidecar-provisioning.md`

  evidence: `DefaultAppConfig.sidecarPath()`/`interpreterPath()` derive from `Platform.resolvedExecutable` plus an existence probe, but `JsonConfigStore._seed()` serializes the result verbatim on first run and `_read` never revalidates it. So a developer who runs from a checkout persists the repo-relative script path into their real XDG config; installing the packaged build afterwards leaves that path resolving against the session's launch directory, and every correction fails `providerUnavailable` until the file is hand-edited. Deliberately not fixed in story 11: a load-time re-derivation is a ConfigStore contract decision (AD-13's "malformed yields defaults plus a surfaced warning" says nothing about a value that was valid when written), and inventing a self-repair rule for one provider's settings is the kind of choice that should be made knowingly. Raised by the adversarial, edge-case and verification-gap lenses independently; story 11 corrected the doc comment that claimed otherwise and left the behaviour.

So the adapter's translation of a real sidecar's `error` line into `CorrectionFailed(providerError)`, and of its `done` line into the parser's terminal, is proven against fakes on one side and against the real script on the other, never joined. Closing it means either an injected environment/spawn seam on the adapter (a change to a shipped type for a test's benefit) or accepting the `live` row as the only joint, which is what stands today. ### DW-114: The tray menu offers no way to quit — a resident daemon a tray-only user cannot stop

origin: reported by the human, 2026-08-14
location: lib/src/infrastructure/tray/tray_manager_tray.dart:176-191, lib/src/domain/tray/tray_port.dart
severity: high
reason: `TrayManagerTray._menu()` builds exactly two lines and neither of them exits the app: an always-enabled "Open the panel" entry, and — only while the hotkey is unavailable — a deliberately disabled statement line. There is no Quit entry, and the gap is not merely a missing line in that list: `TrayPort` declares `install()`, `panelRequests` and `setHotkeyUnavailable(bool)` and carries no seam a quit could travel on, so closing this widens the port as well as the menu. The only way to stop the daemon today is a signal — `main.dart:447-449` installs handlers for SIGINT, SIGTERM and SIGHUP — which means `kill` from a terminal, a process manager, or logging out. The app is a resident tray daemon by design (SPEC:80), and the tray is explicitly the surface that keeps it reachable when the hotkey is not (SPEC:117), so the one surface guaranteed to be present offers no way out. Two things to settle when this is built rather than assumed: whether quitting confirms or is immediate, and that a user-initiated quit runs the same `DaemonLifecycle` teardown as a signal — which is the unbounded path DW-20 decided to bound, so the two should land in a consistent order.
status: done 2026-08-14
resolution: resolved by sweep bundle dw-single-exit-path-tray-quit
decision: 2026-08-14 Quit immediately, no confirmation — the human's call, on the gap they reported. Verified in full: `TrayManagerTray._menu()` (`tray_manager_tray.dart:176-191`) builds exactly two entries — an always-enabled "Open the panel" and, only while the hotkey is unavailable, a deliberately disabled statement line — `_onSelection` (:193-224) accepts only `_openPanelKey`, and `TrayPort` (`tray_port.dart`) is three members with no seam a quit could travel on. The menu gains an always-enabled Quit entry; picking it runs the same ordered `DaemonLifecycle.shutdown()` a SIGTERM does and then exits, with no prompt and no confirmation step. Nothing is lost by quitting — every correction is already in history (CAP-7) and copying is explicit (CAP-11) — which is the same reasoning the SPEC's Assumptions already use to justify a focus-loss hide needing no confirmation. Closing this widens `TrayPort` with a quit-request stream beside `panelRequests`, wired at the composition root to the same teardown the signal handlers reach (`main.dart:446-465`), so there is one exit path with two triggers: a signal and the tray. The window is deliberately not a third — DW-12's answer makes a window-manager close a hide rather than an exit. Lands with DW-20's per-step teardown timeout, so a user-initiated quit cannot hang with no signal left for the user to send.
evidence: `grep -rni 'quit\|exit' lib/ --include='*.dart'` finds no such menu entry or handler anywhere in `lib/`. The menu is enumerated in full at tray_manager_tray.dart:176-191 (`_openPanelKey` at :59, `_hotkeyUnavailableKey` at :60), and `_onSelection`:193-224 accepts only `_openPanelKey`, logging anything else as a seam that has drifted from the menu it was given. `lib/src/domain/tray/tray_port.dart` is three members and none is a quit. The report named the right mouse button specifically; what is verified here is the *menu's contents*, which are the same whichever button opens it. Which button raises the context menu is the host's behaviour, not this adapter's, and it was not observed — this container has no StatusNotifier/AppIndicator host, which is why `test/platform/tray_live_test.dart` is an unconditionally skipped test with a `fail()` body (same gap as DW-9/DW-26).

### DW-115: Ship a second correction provider — an OpenAI-compatible API — so CAP-8's provider choice has something to choose between

origin: requested by the human, 2026-08-14
location: lib/src/infrastructure/correction/provider_registry.dart:18-33, lib/src/domain/config/provider_config.dart, lib/src/ui/settings/
severity: high
reason: CAP-8 already promises this capability — "an operator can change which LLM backend serves corrections by editing configuration or the in-app settings, without touching application code" — and the architecture was built for it: `ProviderRegistry` is an id → factory map whose own doc says adding a provider is "adding one map entry here, never a `switch` in the correction pipeline" (AD-15), and `ProviderConfig.settings` is an opaque `Map<String, String>` whose doc already names "endpoint, key name" as things a provider's adapter owns. What is missing is a second entry in that map: the registry ships exactly one factory, `ClaudeAgentSdkCorrectionProvider`, so today the choice CAP-8 describes has one option. **This is an extension of the design, not a renegotiation of it** — no frozen block and no CAP is being changed. It is nonetheless epic-sized rather than a bundle, decomposing into four pieces that can land in order: (1) the provider adapter itself; (2) a secret-storage port and its adapter; (3) the settings surface; (4) the default config and preset entries that make the second provider selectable. Three constraints are inherited rather than chosen, and an adapter that misses any of them is a broken substitute rather than a variant (AGENTS.md §5, L): it must stream partial text (CAP-5), it must return the structured `suggestions[]` shape the history database also stores rather than a prose blob to be parsed (AGENTS.md §4.1.5), and it must report failure as a `CorrectionFailed` event rather than an escaping exception (CAP-13). The known risk to record now: OpenAI-compatible servers vary in how — and whether — they honour a JSON-schema response format, so the adapter needs a defined answer for a server that returns unparseable or unstructured output, and that answer is `CorrectionFailed`, never a silent single-variant fallback (AGENTS.md §8 rules out fallback and cascade).
status: done 2026-09-26
resolution: 2026-09-26 Closed by plans 02-15–02-18. `ProviderRegistry` now registers the config-selected OpenAI-compatible adapter beside the unchanged Claude default; the adapter uses a fresh bounded Chat Completions SSE request per correction, shares the register-tagged parser with Claude, emits structured variants, and closes its request/client on cancellation. Settings writes the Base URL and paired preset model, while credential lookup uses read-only Secret Service → `OPENAI_API_KEY` → a hand-placed config key and exposes only the winning source. D-15 keeps exactly four `CorrectionFailureKind` values; missing Base URL/model reports `providerUnavailable` on first correction, and other provider failures stay inside the existing four. AD-4 preserves an active correction on panel hide. This supersedes the 2026-08-14 default-OpenAI-address and config-token-field proposals: the shipped default has no live HTTP URL, and Settings has no key-value editor. Under owner-approved D-17, a later whole-file Settings save preserves an API key already hand-placed in `config.json`; this is an exception to the Phase 02 SPEC's literal daemon-never-writes-the-key criterion, so strict no-write acceptance is not claimed. No live OpenAI API or local Ollama endpoint, Secret Service session, or provider interoperability was observed; closure rests on source inspection and the existing local suite.
decision: 2026-08-14 Build, with three human answers — (a) **the API token lives in the desktop secret store**, not in the config file: the token is never written to `config.json`, which is a file the app itself invites users to open and edit, so this needs a new secret-storage port with a domain interface and an infrastructure adapter, in the AD-1 shape every other platform dependency uses. `package:dbus 0.7.14` is already a direct dependency (used by `wayland_portal_global_hotkey.dart`), so `org.freedesktop.secrets` is reachable without adding one, and absence of a secret service must degrade visibly the way AD-12 does rather than blocking startup (AD-19). (b) **The server address is a setting**, defaulting to OpenAI's, so any compatible service works — OpenRouter, Groq, Together, and local servers such as Ollama or LM Studio, which also gives the product an offline path it does not have today. (c) **The provider choice and the token field go on the settings screen**, written through to the config file like every other setting, because CAP-8 promises either surface and CAP-12 requires the two stay synced; a user must never have to open a text editor to switch providers.
evidence: `provider_registry.dart:18-33` builds `_factories` with exactly one entry, keyed `ClaudeAgentSdkCorrectionProvider.providerId`; `create()` at :37-38 returns null for any other id, and `default_app_config.dart:128-129` describes that one provider. `Preset` (lib/src/domain/correction/preset.dart) already carries `providerId` beside `model` and `systemPrompt`, so a preset naming a second provider needs no type change. `CorrectionProvider` is `Stream<CorrectionEvent> correct({required String text, required Preset preset})` — text in, stream out, with no `Uri`, header or key in the signature (AGENTS.md §4.1.1), so an HTTP provider fits behind it unchanged. `pubspec.yaml` carries no HTTP package; `dart:io`'s `HttpClient` is available to the infrastructure ring and adding a dependency is therefore a choice rather than a necessity. Not verified, because it does not exist yet: any claim about how a specific OpenAI-compatible server behaves.

- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-dw-36-ondone-backstop.md`
  summary: DW-36 added the `onDone` backstop at one AD-15 subscription, but seven sibling subscriptions carry the same onError-only backstop — copied from the same sentence — and none gained one; no test anywhere closes an input stream under a live consumer.
  evidence: `grep -rn onDone lib/` finds no AD-15 seam/port input subscription other than the one this spec added. The seven that guard error only: `daemon_lifecycle.dart:136` (`_listenForPanelRequests`, fed by `tray.panelRequests` and `startup.lock.showRequests` — two independently-disposed objects), `tray_manager_tray.dart:34`, `x11_global_hotkey.dart:36`, `panel_controller.dart:20`, `correction_controller.dart:49`, and `settings_controller.dart:56` and `:68`. The argument DW-36 accepted applies verbatim at each: a completed `panelRequests`/`showRequests` stream retires AD-12's tray fallback and AD-14's second-launch raise with no log line at all; a completed `presses`/`activations` stream retires CAP-1 for the session. `DaemonLifecycle` is the sharpest case — its own doc says it "takes a bare `Stream<void>` from the composition root and cannot see which implementation is behind it", which is precisely the argument for guarding completion. The sibling fakes have no `closeEvents()`-equivalent, so no existing suite could reach the case even if the arms were added. What needs deciding is whether this is a rule (adopt at all seven, with the `FakePanelWindow.closeEvents` row shape as the template) or a one-site judgement — and if the latter, the other sites should say so the way `x11_global_hotkey.dart:37-41` already qualifies its own backstop. Raised by the verification-gap lens.

- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-dw-2-spine-currency-refresh.md`
  summary: The Flutter toolchain version still has two ungated hand-maintained homes — `.devcontainer/Dockerfile`'s `FLUTTER_VERSION` ARG with its coupled `FLUTTER_SHA256`, and `pubspec.yaml`'s `flutter:` constraint floor — so a bump that edits only the cited `ci.yml` diverges silently, and `.github/workflows/ci.yml`'s own comment asserts this is "carried as deferred work" while nothing carried it.
  evidence: `.devcontainer/Dockerfile:111-112` holds `ARG FLUTTER_VERSION=3.44.8` beside an `ARG FLUTTER_SHA256` that must move with it; `pubspec.yaml:9` holds `flutter: '>=3.44.8'`. `grep -in 'devcontainer|Dockerfile|FLUTTER_VERSION|FLUTTER_SHA256'` over this ledger returned zero hits before this entry, while `ci.yml:84-88` and `ARCHITECTURE-SPINE.md`'s Stack note both tell a reader the remainder is tracked — the spine's wording ("further hand-maintained copies that no gate covers") is accurate, the workflow comment's ("carried as deferred work") was not, which is what this entry makes true. Only the *downward* direction fails loudly, via pub resolution; an upward bump of `ci.yml` alone leaves every devcontainer building a Flutter that CI does not gate on, with a stale SHA256 that makes the cause non-obvious, and every gate green. DW-90 closed on "one home each" for the two *spine* copies, which it achieved; these two are a different pair and were never in its scope — its own closing text counts three homes and names neither of these. Closing this is a fourth reader in `test/architecture/sidecar_pin_drift_test.dart` beside the three already there: assert the `ARG FLUTTER_VERSION` equals `flutterVersionInWorkflow(ci.yml)` and that the pubspec floor is satisfied by it. Raised independently by the adversarial, edge-case, verification-gap and intent-alignment lenses.
  status: open

- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-dw-2-spine-currency-refresh.md`
  summary: `GlobalHotkey.bindingChanges` is a broadcast stream with no replay and the port offers no synchronous current-registration accessor, so a compositor rebind that arrives while the settings screen is closed is dropped and AD-10 then renders the stale startup `bind()` result — the ordinary tray-daemon case, and the second half of CAP-12.
  evidence: AD-8 already solves this shape for the panel by pairing a synchronous `bool get isVisible` with `Stream<bool> get changes`, precisely so a consumer that subscribes late can still read current state; AD-9's `bindingChanges` has the stream and no field. A tray daemon spends almost all of its life with no settings screen mounted, so "subscriber attached" is the exception rather than the rule, and AD-10's Rule ("Subscribe to `ShortcutsChanged`; a change the compositor or the user originated reaches the UI on AD-9's `bindingChanges`, which is how the second half of CAP-12 holds") is satisfied only for a subscriber that was already listening. Fixing it means adding a member to a declaration AD-9 fixes verbatim, which the DW-2 refresh was explicitly forbidden to do, and it is a `lib/` change that spec's Never list also forbade. Raised as a HIGH by the adversarial lens and by the architecture Reviewer Gate's own adversarial-seams report, where it was recorded in neither the memlog nor a spine edit.
  status: done 2026-09-02
  resolution: 2026-09-02 Closed by plan 01-06 (`57749dc`). `GlobalHotkey` gained the member ratified at plan 01-01's decision gate on 2026-09-01 (option id `status-type`), with **every name as ratified and nothing renamed**: the port member is `HotkeyStatus? get current` (`lib/src/domain/hotkey/global_hotkey.dart:135`), the type is `HotkeyStatus` in the new file `lib/src/domain/hotkey/hotkey_status.dart`, and its fields are `outcome` (non-nullable `HotkeyBindOutcome`) and `backendDescription` (nullable `String`), with value equality over both so a consumer can dedupe. It is **synchronous and I/O-free** — it reads a cached field — which is the whole point: this entry's own cited AD-8 precedent pairs `bool get isVisible` with `Stream<bool> get changes` precisely so a surface that mounts late can read current state without awaiting a microtask, and a tray daemon spends almost all of its life with no settings screen mounted. Both adapters and all four test doubles answer the member in the same change, each through **one recording funnel** (`.then(_recordStatus)` on the queued bind, plus the push helper for backend-originated changes) rather than an assignment per return site, so "every outcome updates the cache" is a property of the shape instead of a rule to remember. **Class of change, which is what this entry needs on the record for Phase 7:** an **addition** to AD-9's verbatim declaration that leaves every declared *field* of `HotkeyRegistration` (still exactly `effective` and `authority`), `HotkeyBound` and `HotkeyUnavailable` untouched — the same class as the value equality added before it, and it rests on the in-tree precedent recorded at `lib/src/domain/hotkey/global_hotkey.dart:50-54`, the `bindingChanges` doc, which states that such an addition is "recorded in the deferred-work ledger and the story's Spec Change Log rather than hand-edited into the spine". Spine lines affected and **not** edited: the `GlobalHotkey` member list at `ARCHITECTURE-SPINE.md:280-300`, under the fixed-part rule at `:296`. The rejected alternatives, for the record: a replaying `bindingChanges` is not synchronous, so a mounting screen would still await a microtask and the semantics of an already-declared member would change; an application-ring cache in `SettingsController` is only as fresh as the last event it happened to be listening for, which is this entry's defect rather than a fix for it. HOTKEY-03's localized description rides the same member, which is what kept `HotkeyRegistration`'s declared field list out of the edit entirely.

- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-dw-2-spine-currency-refresh.md`
  summary: `bind()`'s returned outcome and the `bindingChanges` stream are two writers of one piece of state with no stated precedence, and the portal may emit `ShortcutsChanged` before the `BindShortcuts` reply, so both orderings are spine-conformant while the shipped code silently implements first-writer-wins.
  evidence: AD-9 declares both members and neither AD-9 nor AD-10 says which answer survives when they disagree. `lib/src/application/settings_controller.dart` resolves it in `applyStartupOutcome` without the spine stating the rule, so a second adapter — or DW-39's FFI registrar — could implement the opposite order and still satisfy every written invariant. This is a precedence rule missing from the spine rather than a defect in the code: what ships is a defensible choice that is simply unwritten. Recorded by the previous review pass of this spec but never filed, because that pass's invocation forbade it from editing this ledger.
  status: done 2026-09-02
  resolution: 2026-09-02 Closed by plan 01-08 (`b67efdf`, `eed99e1`). **The missing rule is now written where both members are declared**, in `GlobalHotkey`'s own doc: an event that reaches `bindingChanges` **supersedes** the return value of any `bind()` issued before it; `bind()`'s outcome is authoritative only for the transition it caused, and only until a backend-originated change is observed; a consumer holding a `bind` answer discards it and keeps the event (`lib/src/domain/hotkey/global_hotkey.dart:53-60`, with the short form plus a pointer on `bind` so neither member is a dead end). "Observed" is defined as the moment the consumer's listener runs, not the moment the backend emitted, and the same-turn tie breaks toward the backend. The mechanism the rule prescribes is implemented on the consumer: `SettingsController._backendChangeGeneration` is stamped when a rebind issues its bind and the rebind discards its **own** outcome if a backend-originated change bumped the counter while the bind was in flight — comparison is `!=` against a monotonic counter, so any move at all supersedes. The stamp is taken **before** the bind is issued, so the guard covers the whole `await`, not just the moment of assignment: a bind parked on a portal dialog for up to a minute can no longer overwrite a revocation that arrived while it waited. Two carried details worth the record: on the discard path `hotkeyBackendDescription` keeps the value that arrived *with* the surviving change rather than re-reading `GlobalHotkey.current`, so the outcome and the wording describe the same moment on any adapter lacking 01-06's adapter-side record guard; and a discarded outcome does **not** suppress the mutation's own `SettingsFailure` — the outcome field says what is in effect, the failure field says the user's rebind did not land, and both are true at once. The shipped counter's name is `_backendChangeGeneration`, not the plan's working `_bindGeneration`: it counts backend-originated changes observed, not binds issued. **The honest reachability boundary, stated in the same doc as the rule so nobody later reads it as a bug report:** the GlobalShortcuts documentation specifies no ordering, so a `ShortcutsChanged` arriving *before* a `BindShortcuts` reply is protocol-legal — and it is nevertheless **unreachable on the shipped Wayland adapter**, which subscribes to the signal only after `_bindShortcut` has returned (`_listenForShortcutSignals(client)` at `lib/src/infrastructure/hotkey/wayland_portal_global_hotkey.dart:488` and `:519`, both after the `await _bindShortcut(...)` at `:467`). No live startup defect is claimed; the rule exists for the reachable **rebind** window and for a future adapter, which is exactly what this entry asked for — "a precedence rule missing from the spine rather than a defect in the code". On X11 the guard is structurally inert, because an X11 grab is only ever changed by this app through `bind()` and that adapter's `bindingChanges` is an empty stream that closes. **Owed and NOT closed:** the mid-`BindShortcuts` compositor change has never been observed against a real portal — there is none in this container — so the guard is proved by a fake and by reasoning, not by a run (`.planning/phases/01-hotkey-truth/01-08-SUMMARY.md`; filed in `.planning/WINDOWS.md`). Spine lines affected and **not** edited: AD-9's doc text over `ARCHITECTURE-SPINE.md:280-300` and AD-10 at `:302`, handed to Phase 7 (ARCH-06).

- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-dw-2-spine-currency-refresh.md`
  summary: `HotkeyUnavailable` now carries three distinct meanings on one variant holding only a `message` — "no backend at all", "a working backend refused this one key", and "a live binding was revoked" — and this pass's AD-12 widening sharpened the overlap rather than creating it.
  evidence: AD-9's frozen block documents the value as no backend being able to bind at all; the widened AD-12 Rule now routes an unrepresentable key caught before the backend is touched and a refused X11 grab — both on a *working* backend — to the same value; and the same value arrives on `bindingChanges` when a compositor revokes a binding. A consumer cannot distinguish "never retry" from "retry with a different key" from "you just lost the one you had", and the only carrier is free text. A distinct variant is the fix and it changes both a declaration AD-9 fixes verbatim and the shipped sealed `HotkeyBindOutcome` — Block If territory for the DW-2 spec, which is why the architecture Reviewer Gate reported it rather than working around it, and why the widening deliberately stopped at making AD-12's rendering rule forbid the surfaces from claiming a cause they cannot know. Raised by the adversarial lens and by the Reviewer Gate across two passes.
  status: done 2026-09-02
  resolution: 2026-09-02 Closed by plan 01-04 (`34e588c`, `d923b13`), on the `cause-field` option ratified by the human on 2026-09-01. `HotkeyUnavailable` gained a **required, defaultless** `cause` beside the retained `message`, discriminated by `enum HotkeyUnavailableCause { noBackend, keyRefused, revoked }` — the final name, declared **beside** the class it qualifies in `lib/src/domain/hotkey/hotkey_bind_outcome.dart:51` rather than in a new file, following that library's own in-tree `enum HotkeyModifier` beside `final class HotkeyBinding` precedent. A consumer that wants to know whether retrying is futile now reads a field; nothing has to match on English. Equality and `hashCode` extend to `Object.hash(cause, message)`, so two outcomes differing only in cause cannot dedupe against each other and suppress a `SettingsState` rebuild. `lib/src/ui/settings/hotkey_status_view.dart` selects its first line from the cause rather than from the text of `message`, and cannot render a blank unavailable state. Defaultless is the forcing function: a fourth cause cannot be added without every construction site deciding what the user is told. Two deliberate limits: `keyRefused` is wider than the literal per-key case (a portal that answered unreadably has demonstrably NOT left the user without a backend, so `noBackend` would tell them to give up when retrying is not futile); and the three causes chose **no new affordance** — D-06 is explicit that the messages differ and the UI does not, and the button count in that view is still zero. **The count correction this entry needs on the record.** A sibling flat entry in this file records "20 shipped sites" from a 2026-08-14 `grep -rn 'HotkeyUnavailable(' lib/` — that is a line count, not a construction-site count, and it is left untouched in its own entry (append-only) and corrected here. Re-measured 2026-09-02: `grep -rn 'HotkeyUnavailable(' lib/` excluding the unrelated `setHotkeyUnavailable` hits returns **16** occurrences, of which **13 are real construction sites** — the other three being the declaration itself (`hotkey_bind_outcome.dart:86`) and two pattern matches (`hotkey_status_view.dart:86`, `settings_screen.dart:144`). **All 13 name a cause.** Note the number moved twice and the second move is also recorded rather than smoothed: plan 01-04's own plan text predicted **14** and found **13**, because plan 01-03's atomic rebind swap collapsed one of `x11_global_hotkey.dart`'s refusal branches first; and plan 01-10's action text, written from that stale snapshot, also says 14. The measured figure is 13. Also recorded, because a grep was structurally unable to prove the claim: a per-line `grep -v` can never see `HotkeyUnavailable(` and `cause:` together, since `dart format` wraps every one of these constructors across lines — the check actually run was multi-line-aware, and two sites needed a window wider than four lines because a doc comment sits between the constructor and its first argument (`wayland_portal_global_hotkey.dart:909` -> `cause:` at `:921`; `daemon_startup.dart:222` -> `cause:` at `:228`). Spine lines affected and **not** edited: AD-9's declared field list for `HotkeyUnavailable` at `ARCHITECTURE-SPINE.md:274-278`, whose `const HotkeyUnavailable({required this.message});` no longer matches the shipped constructor — handed to Phase 7 (ARCH-06). **Owed and NOT closed:** all three causes need a real session to observe. `keyRefused` needs a second X client taking the combination through a real Settings interaction; `noBackend` and `revoked` need real sessions this container cannot provide (`.planning/phases/01-hotkey-truth/01-04-SUMMARY.md`; filed in `.planning/WINDOWS.md`).

- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-dw-2-spine-currency-refresh.md`
  summary: `HotkeyBinding.modifiers` is a caller-supplied mutable `Set` held by reference behind a `const` constructor, so a caller that mutates its own set changes a supposedly immutable domain value — and the value equality AD-9 now mandates is exactly what then makes a `copyWith` produce an equal state and suppress the rebuild.
  evidence: `lib/src/domain/hotkey/hotkey_binding.dart:5-7` declares `const HotkeyBinding({required this.modifiers, required this.key})` over a `final Set<HotkeyModifier> modifiers` with no defensive copy and no unmodifiable type. AD-9's amended Rule mandates that `modifiers` compare as a set and hash unordered to match, and says nothing about the field being shared — so a reader who implements the Rule exactly still gets a type whose equality can be mutated out from under a consumer. The `const` constructor is what makes this awkward rather than trivial: a const constructor cannot defensively copy, so closing it means either a non-const factory taking `Set.unmodifiable` or documenting that callers must not retain the argument. Not fixed by the DW-2 refresh, whose Never list forbade changing any Dart under `lib/` and whose Always clause froze the declared fields of every verbatim-fixed type. Raised by the adversarial lens as M3(b).
  status: done 2026-09-02
  resolution: 2026-09-02 Closed by plan 01-09 (`ad6a3e1`) on the **`defensive-copy`** option — this entry's *mechanism* option, not its documented-caller-obligation option. `HotkeyBinding`'s constructor now copies into an unmodifiable set in its initializer list — `HotkeyBinding({required Set<HotkeyModifier> modifiers, required this.key}) : modifiers = Set<HotkeyModifier>.unmodifiable(modifiers);` (`lib/src/domain/hotkey/hotkey_binding.dart:6-7`) — following the in-tree precedent at `hotkey_grab.dart:17-35`, whose doc already named the price: "The constructor is therefore not `const`, which is the price." Both halves of the guarantee are asserted, not just one: the constructor copying stops a caller that still owns the set it passed in, and the field being unmodifiable stops a consumer that reaches into `binding.modifiers` directly and gets an `UnsupportedError`. Equality is unaffected — `setEquals` and `setHash` are order-independent, so an unmodifiable copy of the same elements compares and hashes exactly as the caller's set did. **WHO DECIDED, and it is not a human: the option was selected by the ORCHESTRATOR on 2026-09-02 under the user's instruction to complete the phase unattended, after the user declined the interactive gate. This is NOT a human ratification of a frozen-spine edit. Phase 7's ARCH-06 reconciliation MUST treat it as orchestrator-selected and re-confirm it with a human before the spine is edited to match** (`.planning/phases/01-hotkey-truth/01-GATE-ANSWERS.md` records the framing verbatim). The basis was requirement text rather than orchestrator preference: HOTKEY-09 reads "Defensively copy or wrap `HotkeyBinding.modifiers` ...", which mandates a mechanism — so `document-the-rule` (prose only, does not copy or wrap) would have left HOTKEY-09 unmet, and `unmodifiable-view-const` does not work at all because a `const` constructor cannot call `Set.unmodifiable`. **The honest scope, unchanged by the closure: this fixed NO live defect.** At the time it was written no caller retained a set it had passed in — the capture control hands over a fresh `{..._modifiers}` and the shipped default is a `const` set literal, immutable at runtime — and the field doc says so in those words so a later reader does not go looking for a bug that never existed. This is API hardening against a future caller. **Cost, re-counted rather than trusted, because a grep could not prove it.** The plan's model was short: 16 literal `const HotkeyBinding(` sites across 9 files (what the grep sees) plus **34** further `const` keywords across 10 files where `HotkeyBinding(...)` sat inside somebody else's `const` expression, which no literal grep can see — **50 non-declaration `const` keywords removed across 17 files**, eight of them undeclared by the plan. Clearing them cascaded, because a `const` variable turned `final` invalidates every const expression that referenced it: `dart analyze` went 40 issues -> 16 -> 7 -> 0. Recorded as the phase's sharpest verification lesson: `grep -rc 'const HotkeyBinding(' lib/ test/` printed **clean** at a point where `dart analyze` reported **40 errors**, so the grep is a cheap first pass and `dart analyze --fatal-infos` is the only sufficient check. Spine lines affected and **not** edited: AD-9's declared constructor at `ARCHITECTURE-SPINE.md:246`, `const HotkeyBinding({required this.modifiers, required this.key});` — handed to Phase 7 (ARCH-06) as the one edit of the four that still owes a human answer. Nothing is owed in verification terms: the guarantee is proved by an amended equality row that mutates the caller's set after construction and asserts both bindings still compare and hash equal, plus the `UnsupportedError`, and the `test(` count is unchanged at 983.

- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-dw-2-spine-currency-refresh.md`
  summary: AD-17's amended text asserts that the two infrastructure halves of the composition root may not import `application/` and never see a `ProviderContainer`, but no test scans `lib/src/infrastructure/**` for `application/` imports — the ring rule that this pass made load-bearing is enforced by prose alone.
  evidence: `test/architecture/ad1_import_rule_test.dart`'s own doc comment says "Three rings are checked here, one import rule each: `domain` reaches nothing but `dart:`, `application` never reaches infrastructure or Flutter widgets, and `ui` never reaches infrastructure" — there is no fourth scan. The DW-2 refresh is what moved the startup order, the teardown order and AD-5's `(CorrectionProvider, Preset)` resolution into the spine's description of that ring, so the constraint went from incidental to structural in the same pass that left it ungated; `daemon_lifecycle.dart:20` already carries a hand-written comment about what "the spine forbids", which is what an ungated rule looks like before it is broken. Closing this is one more scan in the existing gate, which was outside the DW-2 spec's task list. Raised by the adversarial lens.
  status: open

- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-dw-2-spine-currency-refresh.md`
  summary: The envelope's runtime-dependency list omits a StatusNotifier/AppIndicator host — the one dependency whose absence is neither hard nor visibly degrading but silent — even though the amended AD-12 now names that hole in its tray-fallback Rule.
  evidence: The list carries `libkeybinder-3.0-0`, `xdg-desktop-portal` plus a GNOME or KDE backend, the Python interpreter and the `claude` CLI, and characterises every entry as either hard (libkeybinder) or visibly degrading (AD-12, AD-19). A tray host fits neither: `tray_manager-0.5.3/linux/tray_manager_plugin.cc:117-130` never checks `app_indicator_new`'s result and answers `fl_value_new_bool(true)` regardless, so on a session with no host `install()` succeeds, no guard fires and nothing logs. The behaviour itself is already filed on this ledger against story 6; what is missing is the envelope row, which is the surface a packager reads. Surfaced by the architecture Reviewer Gate during this pass's AD-12 amendment; the amendment names the hole in AD-12 but the DW-2 spec's scope did not reach the dependency list.
  status: done 2026-09-26
  resolution: 2026-09-26 Closed by generated spine plan 02-21 (`e163b1c`). The Operational envelope's runtime-dependency list now names the StatusNotifier/AppIndicator host, and its next paragraph states the silent `tray_manager` success case when no host displays the indicator. This is source-inspected package behavior; no host-less native tray session was observed.

- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-dw-2-spine-currency-refresh.md`
  summary: The spine's new collection-equality clauses specify `List` (AD-2) and `Set` (AD-9) and leave `Map` unstated, while the shipped code already needs the Map rule and states it itself.
  evidence: `lib/src/domain/config/app_config.dart:67,75` compares `providers` with `mapEquals` and hashes it with `mapHash`, and `lib/src/domain/collection_equality.dart:65-67` documents `mapHash` as an order-independent hash agreeing with `mapEquals` — the same trap the AD-9 clause spells out for `Set` (`Object.hash` over a collection is an identity hash, under which two equal values hash differently and every `Map`/`Set` dedupe silently stops working). The set of collection kinds in play is exactly {List, Set, Map}; two are legislated and the third, which ships, is the implicit branch. Secondary, same clause: AD-2's collection sentence names `List<Suggestion>`, but no type in AD-2's own block has a collection field — the only `List<Suggestion>` is `CorrectionRecord.suggestions`, declared under AD-7 — so the clause is inert where it is stated and absent where it bites. Raised by the edge-case and adversarial lenses.
  status: done 2026-09-26
  resolution: 2026-09-26 Closed by generated spine plan 02-21 (`e163b1c`). AD-2/AD-9 now state collection equality and compatible order-independent hashing for shipped `List`, `Set`, and `Map` values, including `CorrectionRecord.suggestions` and config provider maps. The rule matches `AppConfig`'s `mapEquals`/`mapHash` implementation; it is a documentation reconciliation, not a new equality mechanism.

Created by the DW-2 refresh, whose Never list forbade changing any Dart under `lib/`, so it could not be fixed in the same pass. Both are comment-only edits. - source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-dw-2-spine-currency-refresh.md`
  summary: Spine currency items beyond the seven DW-2 was scoped to, all inside sections that pass rewrote: the `Dart SDK` row restates a number the newly-cited Flutter pin determines, the pin-renegotiation paragraph's EOL warning is now false rather than merely stale, CAP-5's *Lives in* path is missing a directory segment, and CAP-11's *Governed by* omits the AD that governs copying.
  evidence: (a) The Stack table's `| Dart SDK | 3.12.2 |` sits one row under the two converted to citations, and Flutter ships exactly one Dart SDK — so it is the second writable copy of a fact that the paragraph directly beneath it argues against, and bumping the cited `ci.yml` silently falsifies both it and `pubspec.yaml:8`'s `sdk: ^3.12.2`. (b) The pin-renegotiation paragraph warns that `drift_flutter 0.3.1` transitively resolves end-of-life `sqlite3_flutter_libs`/`sqlcipher_flutter_libs`; those packages are absent from `pubspec.lock` and, per DW-94, were empty tombstones that never carried native code, so the reasoning is false rather than out of date. (c) `ARCHITECTURE-SPINE.md:596` cites `infrastructure/correction/register_tagged_stream_parser.dart` while the file is at `lib/src/infrastructure/correction/claude_agent_sdk/register_tagged_stream_parser.dart` — the Structural Seed has the segment and the map row does not, which sends a builder to a top-level path where AD-15's per-provider confinement stops applying. (d) `ARCHITECTURE-SPINE.md:601` lists CAP-11's *Governed by* as `AD-6` alone, while AD-18's Rule ("Copying is never implicit in selection — selecting with 1/2/3 only highlights") is what actually governs a copy button. (a) and (b) were carried as memlog open questions by the DW-2 pass; (c) and (d) were marked "Fix in this pass" by its Reviewer Gate's currency report and then carried instead — both are one-token edits, and both are arguably frozen by that spec's "add/remove/reword any CAP" prohibition, which is the ambiguity that needs settling before they land. DW-94 owns the `drift_flutter` row itself and is not superseded here. Raised by the adversarial and intent-alignment lenses.

The generated Stack cites the pinned Dart SDK constraint rather than a stale literal, removes the false `drift_flutter` EOL warning, corrects CAP-5's parser path and CAP-11's copying governance, and reconciles the rewritten sections with shipped files. D-19 is the owner's 2026-09-24 ratification of `HotkeyBinding({required Set<HotkeyModifier> modifiers, required this.key}) : modifiers = Set<HotkeyModifier>.unmodifiable(modifiers);`; generated AD-9 now records this non-`const` constructor. The earlier 2026-09-02 orchestrator selection remains historical provenance, not the human authorization. Plan 02-21's later review findings and unobserved native behavior remain separately disclosed; this closure claims the named currency items, not a live architecture runtime check.

- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-dw-2-spine-currency-refresh.md`
  summary: The envelope's re-measure trigger for the `libkeybinder` hardness claim keys on a version nothing in the tree can observe changing — `hotkey_manager_linux` is transitive, the Stack table pins only the `hotkey_manager` facade, and no test reads either.
  evidence: The clause says "Re-measure when the plugin version moves" about `hotkey_manager_linux 0.2.0`. `pubspec.yaml` depends on `hotkey_manager 0.2.3`, whose own pubspec depends on `hotkey_manager_linux: ^0.2.0`, and `pubspec.lock` records it as `dependency: transitive` — so `flutter pub upgrade` can move it anywhere inside `^0.2.0` with no spine row changing, no `pubspec.yaml` line changing and no gate firing. `grep -rn hotkey_manager_linux test/` returns nothing. The Stack table also lists `| hotkey_manager | 0.2.3 |` with nothing stating that the measured library is its transitive Linux half, so a reader comparing the two numbers sees a contradiction that is not one. Closing this is either a test that reads the resolved version out of `pubspec.lock` so the trigger can fire, or restating the dependency structurally and dropping the version from the prose. Raised by the adversarial and edge-case lenses.
  status: done 2026-09-02
  resolution: 2026-09-02 Closed with the finding that the trigger's **subject left the tree**, not by re-keying it — there is nothing left to key it to. Three parts, each measured. (1) *The subject is gone.* The clause at `ARCHITECTURE-SPINE.md:584` says "Re-measure when the plugin version moves" about `hotkey_manager_linux 0.2.0`, a package `pubspec.lock` recorded as `dependency: transitive`, reachable only through the `hotkey_manager 0.2.3` facade's own `^0.2.0` constraint. Plan 01-02 (commit `6abf480`) deleted that facade from `pubspec.yaml`, which took `hotkey_manager`, `hotkey_manager_linux`, `hotkey_manager_macos`, `hotkey_manager_platform_interface` and `hotkey_manager_windows` out of the lock together. Verified 2026-09-02: `grep -c 'hotkey_manager' pubspec.lock` returns `0`, and `grep -rn 'package:hotkey_manager' lib/ pubspec.yaml` returns nothing. (2) *The claim the trigger guarded is retired.* The hardness clause exists because a library the app cannot supply is resolved before `main()`. Under the route plan 01-02 took, `libkeybinder-3.0-0` leaves the runtime-dependency list outright rather than being demoted, and its replacement `libX11.so.6` is already an unconditional `DT_NEEDED` of `libgdk-3.so.0` on every host that can run this Flutter app at all (`readelf -d /usr/lib/x86_64-linux-gnu/libgdk-3.so.0` names `libX11.so.6` and `libXext.so.6`; measured 2026-09-01, 01-RESEARCH.md § C7) — so no new hard dependency appears and none needs a re-measure trigger. Plan 01-02's own measurement, on a release bundle built eight seconds earlier: `readelf -d <exe> | grep -c hotkey_manager` went `1` -> `0`, `ldd <exe> | grep -c keybinder` went `1` -> `0`, `ls bundle/lib/ | grep -c hotkey_manager` went `1` -> `0`, and `ldd` over every `.so` in `bundle/lib/` finds no keybinder reference anywhere (`.planning/phases/01-hotkey-truth/01-02-SUMMARY.md`, coverage id D2). (3) *No re-keying was invented, and that is the closure, not an oversight.* HOTKEY-10's requirement text asked for the trigger to be re-keyed "to a version the tree can actually observe changing". After (1) and (2) there is no version, no package and no claim left to key it to: a test reading a resolved version out of `pubspec.lock` would read a line that no longer exists, and restating the dependency structurally would restate a dependency the build no longer has. Manufacturing a target so the requirement had something to point at would have converted an honest closure into a fabricated one, so it was not done. What the finding implies for the spine is filed as its own open entry (the envelope amendment, appended to this file 2026-09-02) rather than applied here — Phase 7 (ARCH-06) owns spine currency and this phase edited no line of `ARCHITECTURE-SPINE.md`. Recorded for a later reader: the envelope clause at `:584` still names `libkeybinder-3.0-0` and the Stack table at `:442` still pins `hotkey_manager 0.2.3`, both now false of the tree — which is precisely why the amendment is handed on rather than assumed done.

- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-dw-2-spine-currency-refresh.md`
  summary: The architecture run folder's `reviews/` reports ship as frozen mid-pass critiques with no disposition recorded on them, so an auditor reading the pass from those files is misled in both directions — findings that were fixed still read as live, and findings marked "Fix in this pass" were carried.
  evidence: In `reviews/review-currency-check-2026-08-14.md`, findings F2 (the mermaid `ROOT` node naming two parts, the Structural Seed missing three files), F4 (the missing `hotkey_bind_outcome.dart`) and F6 (the undated Stack heading) are all fixed in the shipped spine, while F7 (CAP-5's path) and F8 (CAP-11's *Governed by*) carry the disposition "Fix in this pass" and were not fixed. The same holds for `review-adversarial-seams-2026-08-14.md` M4 and `review-rubric-walker-2026-08-14.md` M-3/M-4, and one of the three reports records as a passing acceptance check that `deferred-work.md` was untouched — true when the gate ran, and no longer true of the tree those reports ship in. Three more such reports were added by the follow-up amendment pass. The Reviewer Gate contract requires each lens to write its full review to that subfolder, so the files are correct as records; what is missing is any marker distinguishing a record from a live finding list. Cheapest fix is a disposition header per report, or keeping them untracked as gate scratch. Raised by the intent-alignment lens.
  status: done 2026-09-26
  resolution: 2026-09-26 Closed by plans 02-22 (`8a81cff`, `35b35c0`) and 02-23 (`d98bf74`, `af5858f`). All eleven frozen review reports now carry dated finding-by-finding dispositions, preserving their original 2026-08-14 verdicts and distinguishing source-resolved items from open follow-ups and unobserved native or upstream behavior. Subsequent post-plan closures in the summaries address the narrow AD-12 wording findings; this entry closes the missing-disposition problem, not every review finding.

- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-dw-2-spine-currency-refresh.md`
  summary: AD-12's tray surface is written only at startup, so a settings-initiated failed bind and a compositor revocation reach the settings screen and never the tray — while AD-12's *Prevents* now covers exactly those "after the fact" refusals.
  evidence: `setHotkeyUnavailable` has exactly one production caller — `lib/src/infrastructure/system/daemon_startup.dart:184`, inside `bindHotkey`, itself called once from `lib/main.dart:240` at startup. `bindingChanges` reaches only `SettingsController`; nothing re-pushes the tray state. So the two paths the previous pass's AD-12 widening newly brought under the Rule — `lib/src/application/settings_controller.dart:432` (a rebind the user asks for and the backend refuses) and `lib/src/infrastructure/hotkey/wayland_portal_global_hotkey.dart:862` (a shortcut the compositor later revokes) — leave the tray asserting the hotkey is available while the settings screen says it is not, and a tray-daemon user who never opens settings sees no degradation at all. `settings_controller.dart:424`'s own comment ("the outcome by the tray and the settings screen") overclaims. Closing this needs a change under `lib/`, which the DW-2 spec's Never list forbids, or an AD Rule the tree currently fails. Raised independently by the edge-case lens and by this pass's architecture Reviewer Gate, which reported it as a blocker it would not resolve.
  status: done 2026-09-26
  resolution: Plan 02-03 forwards typed Settings hotkey-status changes through `lib/src/application/composition/daemon_graph.dart` to `tray_manager_tray.dart` after startup, including refusal, revocation, no backend, and restoration. Source inspection and the existing Settings/tray suite support the fan-out. The retained Wayland refusal lacking a typed refusal signal remains WINDOWS entry 41; no live compositor or tray host was observed.

- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-dw-2-spine-currency-refresh.md`
  summary: The three normative obligations AD-12 gained are ungated — no test covers "`bind()` never throws", "`HotkeyUnavailable` only when nothing is held", or "every message ends by naming the tray menu" across the 20 shipped sites that must satisfy them.
  evidence: `test/architecture/` holds nine gates and none reads AD-12; `grep -rn 'HotkeyUnavailable(' lib/` returns 20 construction sites across `x11_global_hotkey.dart` (5), `wayland_portal_global_hotkey.dart` (13), `settings_controller.dart` and `daemon_startup.dart`. The message contract is the mechanically checkable one — a source scan asserting every `HotkeyUnavailable` literal under `lib/` ends with the tray sentence, in the shape `composition_wiring_test.dart`'s text scans already use. Its absence has already cost something once: `composition_wiring_test.dart:129-132`'s reason string still says "four shipped strings" tell the user the tray still opens the panel, a count that drifted to 20 with nothing noticing. The never-throws obligation rests on a terminal `catch (Object)` plus two caller backstops (`daemon_startup.dart:208-228`, `settings_controller.dart:393-437`) that no test pins together. AD-12's Rule quadrupled in size across two passes with a Reviewer Gate but no gate in the suite. Raised by the adversarial lens.
  status: open

- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-dw-2-spine-currency-refresh.md`
  summary: Nothing gates the spine's code-shape claims, so the seventh drift will be as silent as the first six — reverting AD-9's `bind` return type to the stale signature this spec corrected leaves the whole architecture suite green.
  evidence: Reproduced: restoring `Future<HotkeyRegistration> bind(...)` to AD-9's fixed declaration block gives `dart test test/architecture/` 178 passed, 1 skipped — the same all-green result a real code change diverging from the spine would give. `grep -rln 'ARCHITECTURE-SPINE\|architecture-hotkey-grammar-corrector' test/ tool/ lib/` returns exactly one file, `test/architecture/sidecar_pin_drift_test.dart`, whose readers never leave the `## Stack` section; `lint_spine.py` checks structure only (a block is inspected for the *presence* of Binds/Prevents/Rule, and `version_pin` only for a non-empty cell) and `grep -rn lint_spine .github/ tool/` returns nothing, so it runs in no automated path. The two *version* facts DW-2 re-homed now have a gate; the six *code-shape* facts it corrected — AD-9's declaration block, the AD-2/AD-9 equality clauses, AD-17's composition shape, AD-12's widened Rule, the CAP-4/CAP-11 rows — are in the same unpinned state that produced the drift this spec exists to end, in a document closed at `status: final`. The spec's own first acceptance criterion ("the declarations they copy compile against `global_hotkey.dart` unchanged") is verified by human reading only. The cheapest closing move is one reader in the shape of the pin gate: extract AD-9's ```dart block and assert each fixed member line appears verbatim in the source, failing loudly rather than vacuously when the block stops parsing. Raised by the verification-gap lens.
  status: open

- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-dw-2-spine-currency-refresh.md`
  summary: `hotkey_status_view.dart`'s own doc comment is now the stale copy of the AD-12 premise this pass corrected in the spine — it says nothing is appended to the message, and the method beneath it appends a line.
  evidence: `lib/src/ui/settings/hotkey_status_view.dart:96-99` reads "The adapter's [message] is rendered as it stands and nothing is appended to it", while `_unavailableLines` at :126-130 returns two entries — the message and "Whether this app or your desktop would own the shortcut is not known until one is registered." The rest of that doc comment makes clear the intended claim is that nothing is appended *about the fallback*, and the appended line is deliberate and load-bearing ("the part that must not regress"). The spine's AD-12 Rule carried the same imprecision and was corrected in this pass; the code comment is now the only surviving copy of the false version, which inverts the usual direction of this spec's problem. Comment-only edit, but the DW-2 spec's Never list forbids changing any Dart under `lib/`. Surfaced by the adversarial lens and confirmed by the architecture Reviewer Gate.
  status: done 2026-09-26
  resolution: Plan 02-04 corrects the doc comment in `lib/src/ui/settings/hotkey_status_view.dart` to describe the appended shortcut-ownership line while leaving `_unavailableLines` behavior intact. The closure is a source comparison of comment and method; no runtime observation was needed or added.

  summary: `composition_wiring_test.dart`'s AD-12 reason string counts "four shipped strings" that promise the tray still opens the panel; there are twenty.
  evidence: `test/architecture/composition_wiring_test.dart:129-132` justifies pinning `TrayManagerTray(` in `main.dart` on the ground that "four shipped strings tell the user that the hotkey is inactive but 'the tray menu still opens the panel'; a placeholder here makes every one of them false". `grep -rn 'HotkeyUnavailable(' lib/` now finds 20 producing sites and every message ends with that sentence. The assertion is still correct and still green — only its stated reason is stale, which is exactly the failure mode that makes a reason string worse than none: a maintainer reads it as a measured count and trusts the number. Pre-existing drift, unrelated to this spec's tasks, surfaced while verifying AD-12's message contract.

- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-dw-2-spine-currency-refresh.md`
  summary: `test: 1.31.0` is a pinned direct dev dependency with no Stack row and no ledger entry, so the one divergence the Stack note tracks by name is not the only one.
  evidence: `pubspec.yaml:44` pins `test: 1.31.0` with a stated architectural reason (a direct dev dependency is required for `dart test` to run the pure-Dart domain tests without a Flutter binding, AGENTS.md §7), and its comment says outright "Not in the spine Stack table". The Stack heading note's claim is narrowly true as written — it says every *row* was checked against the graph and matches, with `drift_flutter 0.3.1` the one exception — but the note is what tells a reader they can stop reconciling, and a pinned direct dependency the table never mentions is the kind of thing they would expect it to. Two review lenses and this pass's Reviewer Gate independently read it as a second exception. The actionable half is that it has never been filed anywhere: either add the row or state that the table is not exhaustive of direct dependencies. Untouched in this pass because the Stack section is under the pin-drift gate and the change is a judgement about scope, not a correction.
  status: open

- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-dw-2-spine-currency-refresh.md`
  summary: No AD decides what happens when the config names a provider id this build does not ship — the code has an answer, the architecture does not.
  evidence: `lib/src/infrastructure/correction/active_correction.dart`'s `_providerFor` falls back to `unconfigured_correction_provider.dart` both when AD-15's registry lookup misses and when the config never described a provider, and that fallback yields one `CorrectionFailed(providerUnavailable, …)` then closes rather than blocking startup. AD-15 legislates the registry ("id -> factory lookup, no switch") and AD-19 the non-blocking degradation, but neither states the miss case, so the shipped behaviour is a convention rather than a decision — and a second provider added by a later story could reasonably resolve it the other way (refuse to start, or fall back to a default id). Surfaced by this pass's architecture Reviewer Gate while adding the fallback file to the Structural Seed; not taken because a new Rule is a new decision, beyond the two amendments this pass was authorised to make.
  status: done 2026-09-26
  resolution: 2026-09-26 Closed by plan 02-21 (`e163b1c`) after plan 02-18 wired the behavior. Generated AD-15 now states that an unknown configured provider ID logs a startup warning, selects `UnconfiguredCorrectionProvider`, and yields one `CorrectionFailed(providerUnavailable, …)` on correction without failing startup or falling back to another provider. This records the shipped rule in the architecture rather than inferring it from the registry.

The Seed reads as exhaustive precisely because two passes have now completed parts of it. Either reconcile it in full (its own pass — the Seed is a distillation target, so this is a memlog-and-redistill job, not an edit) or state above it that it is illustrative. reason: The follow-up-review damping cap (limits.max_followup_reviews = 1) was spent with the story finalized (status: done, verify green) while the review pass still recommended an independent follow-up. - source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-dw-9-runtime-observation-checklists.md`
  summary: `test/platform/tray_live_test.dart` still asserts the container has no `Xvfb`/`xvfb-run`, the claim measured false and corrected in four sibling suites, and both ledger ids it names are now closed.
  evidence: Its skip reason reads "there is no `~/.Xauthority`, no `XAUTHORITY`, and no `Xvfb`/`xvfb-run` to stand one up". Measured directly: `Xvfb`, `xvfb-run`, `xwininfo`, `xdotool` and `xprop` are all installed; only `wmctrl` and `desktop-file-validate` are absent. The DW-9 bundle corrected exactly this sentence in `hidden_window_test.dart`, `panel_visibility_live_test.dart`, `x11_hotkey_live_test.dart` and `settings_screen_live_test.dart`, and deliberately left `tray_live_test.dart` alone because the spec's Never list fences off tray claims — so the repo now states a fact and its negation in two files in the same directory. Separately, DW-9 and DW-26 are the only ledger ids that suite names and both are now `done`, so its runtime tray claims trace to no open entry. Correcting a statement of fact about the container is not "adding a tray claim to a checklist"; it was out of scope only because the bundle's enumeration did not name the entry that owns it.
  status: open

The rule the citations invoke — the one the whole skipped-`fail()`-row convention rests on — appears in no section of the file. This review corrected the two citations the DW-9 bundle itself introduced (both new procedures now state the principle directly instead), which leaves the six pre-existing suite citations pointing at the wrong section. The repo-wide fix is a choice this review cannot make: either add the rule to AGENTS.md as its own numbered section and repoint all citations at it, or drop the section number from every citation. Worth doing as one pass so the convention has a single documented home.

- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-dw-9-runtime-observation-checklists.md`
  summary: DW-9, DW-25, DW-26, DW-44 and DW-87 are all recorded `done`/`resolved by sweep bundle dw-runtime-observation-checklists`, but the runtime observations they own were never made — so nothing open now requires anyone to execute either procedure, and the six pinned suites point readers at closed ids.
  evidence: The bundle's own Problem statement is that these five entries "each owe a runtime observation that this container cannot make". What the bundle delivered is two manual procedures plus a pin suite: a written description of how to make the observations, not the observations. Every step in both procedures, and every one of the six skip reasons, still states its claim as unobserved and owed — correctly, because it is. Yet the entries that tracked the debt read as resolved, and `git log` shows the flips landing inside the story's own review commit (`5e67cba`) rather than in a separate orchestrator commit. The practical effect is that a reader who hits `hidden_window_test.dart`'s skipped row is sent to "deferred-work DW-9", finds it closed, and has no open item telling them the procedure is still unrun. This review could not fix it from either end: the spec's Never list forbids editing this ledger, and the invocation reserved existing entries' status and resolution to the orchestrator explicitly. The decision is which of two shapes to take — keep the five open with the checklists recorded as partial progress, or open one successor entry owning "execute both procedures on a real session" and repoint the six pointer clauses at it. Either way the ids the skip reasons cite have to be ones that are still open. Raised independently by the adversarial and intent-alignment lenses.
  status: open

This review corrected the premise in the three places it could reach: session checklist step 13, which had told the operator "nothing predicts a number here" and "there is nothing to diverge from", and the same clause in `correction_panel_live_test.dart` and `settings_screen_live_test.dart`. The distinction matters to whoever closes DW-50: "the panel has no geometry" and "the panel has the Flutter template's geometry" are different findings, and only the second explains why CAP-10 was ever verified at the 480x360 surface a layout test chose. The entry's `reason:` body still carries the false version, and existing entries are the orchestrator's to edit.
