- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-dw-19-daemon-startup-and-abort-path.md`
  summary: Installing `PlatformDispatcher.instance.onError` (DW-21) made the already-filed bare-logger hole on the abort path silent — a throw from `_abort`'s own `logger.error` on a broken stderr now returns `true` to the platform and `exit(1)` never runs, with nothing printed.
  evidence: `main.dart`'s `_abort` opening `logger.error` and `_releaseWithoutLifecycle`'s per-step `logger.error` are the only two logs in the file not routed through `_log`, and `StderrLogger._write` is an unguarded `_sink.writeln` that throws on EPIPE/EBADF — ordinary for a systemd-launched daemon whose journal socket went away. The escape already skipped `exit(1)` before this change (an error out of `main` does not end a Flutter process, whose GTK loop is already running), so the process already stayed resident holding the AD-14 address; what is new is that the new handler answers `true`, so nothing reports it at all. This bundle's DW-37 work also puts two new throwable steps (`disposeControllers`, `container.dispose`) into that unguarded catch. The root cause and its two-line fix are already filed as a parked entry from story 6's review pass ("`_abort` and `_releaseWithoutLifecycle` log through the bare `logger`… The fix is two `_log(() => ...)` wraps"); not taken here because this bundle's intent is the four 2026-08-14 decisions and a fifth fix is not among them. Raised independently by the adversarial and edge-case lenses.
  status: done 2026-09-26
  resolution: 2026-09-26 Closed by plan 02-13 (`f726aa4`). `lib/main.dart` routes abort diagnostics through the existing privacy-safe `_log` guard and places `exit(1)` in `_abort`'s `finally`, so a logging or cleanup throw cannot skip the fatal exit. This is source-order evidence; the real GTK process exit branch was not observed live.

- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-dw-19-daemon-startup-and-abort-path.md`

  evidence: `test/architecture/composition_wiring_test.dart`'s DW-21 row pins that both handlers are installed before `runApp`, that `_log(` and `runtimeType.toString()` each appear twice, that `return true;` is present and that the leaking spellings are absent. Nothing asserts the behaviour those tokens stand for: that a throwing `Logger` is swallowed rather than becoming an error reported from an error handler, that a null `details.library` does not itself blow up, or that the daemon actually survives a reported error. Reaching it needs the install to move behind a seam under `lib/src/`, which this bundle's intent forecloses — DW-21 names `main.dart` as the file the handlers are missing from, and the spec's Always list holds every fix to the file its entry names. It is the same shape as the four `main.dart` claims the source-scan idiom already carries, but unlike those it has a cheap escape: the function takes a `Logger` and touches nothing else. - source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-dw-19-daemon-startup-and-abort-path.md`
  summary: `DaemonStartup.begin` and the not-the-daemon exit sit outside `main`'s abort guard, so now that DW-21's `PlatformDispatcher.onError` answers `true` for everything, a rethrow from `begin` leaves a resident windowless process instead of a failed launch.
  evidence: `main.dart:66` awaits `DaemonStartup.begin(...)` and `:72` handles the `startup == null` branch; `main`'s `try` does not open until `:102`. `daemon_startup.dart` ends its own failure path in `_releaseAfterFailedStartup(...); rethrow;`, and its doc names `AppDatabase.file` on an undeletable data directory as a real way to get there. Before this bundle such a rethrow escaped `main` with no handler installed; now `_installErrorHandlers` catches it, logs one line and returns `true` — handled — so the isolate survives with a running embedder loop, no window, no tray and no graph. The address itself is not held (`begin` released it before rethrowing), so a later launch still becomes the daemon; the cost is one zombie per failed launch rather than an unreachable daemon. Not patched with the two `_log` wraps taken in the same review pass, and the distinction is deliberate: those were two wraps that changed no control flow, whereas closing this means choosing and writing a failure policy for a path that currently has none, extending `main`'s guard across the `exit(0)` branch it deliberately sits beside, and pinning the result — a fifth fix, not a correction to the four this bundle took. Raised by the edge-case lens.
  status: done 2026-09-26
  resolution: 2026-09-26 Closed by plan 02-13 (`0e5e28c`). `main` now runs `DaemonStartup.begin` and the secondary-instance exit branch inside the startup abort guard; `begin` retains ownership and release of an acquired address until it returns a startup object. A rethrow reaches abort by source inspection; native process ordering was not observed live.

- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-dw-19-daemon-startup-and-abort-path.md`
  summary: `single_instance_lock_test.dart`'s pre-existing `release frees the address for the very lock that held it` row is load-sensitive in the same way the already-filed gate-flake entry describes, and this review measured it failing at the baseline revision too — so the gate's moving number is not confined to the two suites that entry names.
  evidence: Measured this pass, deliberately against both trees. Unloaded, the row is solid: 8/8 clean at HEAD and 8/8 clean in a detached worktree at the baseline revision `91cfedc`. Under ten concurrent runs of the suite it fails 7/10 at HEAD and 5/10 at the baseline — a difference well inside the noise of two non-simultaneous load samples, and the baseline number is what settles it: the row flakes without any of this bundle's code present. The mechanism is the row's own `await pumpEventQueue()` at `single_instance_lock_test.dart:78`, which bounds the wait for a real socket round trip by a fixed number of event-loop turns rather than by an observable barrier; the six DW-19 rows added by this bundle avoid it by draining the peer socket instead, which is the shape that fixes this one. It surfaced here as the single failure in a full-gate run (856 pass / 2 skip / 1 fail) and cost this pass two rounds of investigation to distinguish from a regression in the `_recordShowRequest` rewrite it sits next to — which is the practical cost of leaving it: the rows most likely to flake are the ones nearest the code under review. Raised by this review's own verification, not by a lens.

Those two rows were added precisely because the previous pass found the partial-build teardown asserted by source text alone, and one of them had to be rewritten twice before it survived its mutation — so they are the strongest evidence the DW-37 fix has, and a merge check will never run them. Everything else guarding DW-37 is a source scan in `composition_wiring_test.dart`, which is gated but cannot execute anything. Closing this is a decision about gate cost (adding `flutter test`, or a narrower binding-capable job), which is why it is filed rather than taken. Raised by the intent-alignment lens.

- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-dw-20-bounded-teardown-and-window-timeouts.md`
  summary: `main.dart`'s `_releaseWithoutLifecycle` loop still awaits `graph.disposeControllers`, `panelVisibility.dispose` and `startup.database.close` unbounded, so the pre-lifecycle abort path can still hang before `exit(1)` — the same shape DW-20 just bounded on the other teardown path.
  evidence: `lib/main.dart:485-543` runs its steps under a bare try/catch with no bound, and two of them are the very futures DW-20 names by name: `graph.disposeControllers` reaches `CorrectionController.dispose()`'s unbounded `Future.wait(_pendingSaves)` (added to this loop by the immediately preceding commit `ae97e87`, DW-37), and `startup.database.close` awaits drift's background isolate. The loop's own doc promises the opposite — "a database that will not close must not stop the address going back" — and `_abort`'s `else` branch awaits it and then calls `exit(1)`, so a hang there leaves the AD-14 address held with no panel, no tray and no hotkey: the exact state `_abort` exists to prevent. Explicitly out of scope for the bundle that found it — spec-dw-20's Never list names this loop as "same shape, different entries" — and the non-adoption is now actively pinned by `test/architecture/composition_wiring_test.dart`'s budget row, which asserts exactly two sites take a bound. The bound limits the wait, not native work already issued; process exit and native release ordering remain source-inspection claims.

- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-dw-20-bounded-teardown-and-window-timeouts.md`
  summary: The shipped 5 s `_unresponsiveCallBudget` has never been exercised against a real window manager or a real stop signal, and the checklist mechanism this repo uses to hold such owings has no step for it.
  evidence: Every behavioural row injects a different bound — `_ampleBound`/`_ampleStepTimeout` at 5 minutes where the bound must not be reached, `_stallBound`/`_stallStepTimeout` at 1 s where reaching it *is* the behaviour under test — so the shipped value is exercised only as text, inside `composition_wiring_test.dart`'s range assertion. The two claims that decide whether 5 s is right are both about surfaces no test here can reach: whether a real compositor ever leaves a `window_manager` call unsettled for that long, and whether an eleven-step worst-case teardown finishes inside the grace its supervisor actually allows. The second is now doubly owed, because this review established the repo ships no `.service` unit at all — the daemon installs as an XDG autostart entry (`linux/packaging/autostart/`) — so the 90 s systemd default the ceiling is argued against is not the supervisor that currently stops this process. `test/platform/runtime-observation-checklist.md` plus `test/architecture/runtime_checklists_test.dart` are the adopted mechanism for exactly this, and `_sessionChecklist.claims` lists DW-9, DW-25, DW-26, DW-39, DW-44, DW-50 and DW-72 — not DW-20 or DW-28. ### DW-119: Follow-up review still recommended for dw-bounded-teardown-and-window-timeouts after the damping cap was spent
origin: review-budget-followup
source_spec: `spec-dw-20-bounded-teardown-and-window-timeouts.md`
severity: low
reason: The follow-up-review damping cap (limits.max_followup_reviews = 1) was spent with the story finalized (status: done, verify green) while the review pass still recommended an independent follow-up. The work was committed by bmad-loop run 20260814-084949-3773; this entry preserves the lingering recommendation for a deliberate later review.
status: open

- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-dw-114-single-exit-path-tray-quit.md`
  summary: A stop request arriving during startup's `bindHotkey` portal wait runs the whole ordered teardown *underneath* the rest of startup, so `applyStartupBindOutcome` then touches a disposed graph and the process exits through `_abort` with a spurious "startup failed" line — and, because both awaiters share one latched `shutdown()` future, the exit code is whichever of `exit(0)` and `exit(1)` resumes first.
  evidence: `_installSignalHandlers(lifecycle)` and `_installQuitHandler(...)` are both installed before `_finishStartup(...)`, deliberately and for the stated CAP-7 reason. `_finishStartup` pushes the menu at `tray.install()` and only then awaits `startup.bindHotkey(...)`, which `main.dart`'s own comment says "can sit on a portal dialog for seconds (AD-11)". A stop landing in that window runs `DaemonLifecycle.shutdown()` — disposing the controllers and the provider graph — while `bindHotkey` is still in flight; the tail then calls `graph.applyStartupBindOutcome(...)` against a disposed `ProviderContainer`, which throws into `main`'s guard and reaches `_abort` (`exit(1)`, "startup failed after the single-instance address was taken"). `shutdown()` is `_shutdown ??= _run()`, so `_abort` and the quit listener await the *same* future and resume in registration order — `_abort`'s doc says the exit code is what tells a launcher the launch failed, and on this interleaving a failed startup can exit 0. Pre-existing on the signal path, which has the identical shape and installation point; DW-114 does not introduce the hazard but adds a second, user-reachable trigger for it, which is why it is filed rather than patched. Fixing it is a startup-completion gate that has to cover both triggers (and settle which exit code wins), not a local edit. Raised independently by the adversarial and edge-case lenses in the follow-up review pass.
  status: done 2026-09-26
  resolution: 2026-09-26 Closed by plan 02-14 (`7e23f25`). A latched stop ends the startup bind wait and joins startup completion before ordered teardown; `applyStartupBindOutcome` is guarded so it runs only while the graph is live. The correction controller marks cancellation terminal before subscription cancellation, preventing late `onDone` from recording a false failure. AD-4 still preserves an active correction when the panel hides; hide is not a cancellation trigger. Native portal and stop callback ordering was not observed live.

- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-dw-114-single-exit-path-tray-quit.md`
  summary: The `## Not covered here` bullet this story added for DW-12's close-as-dismissal claim is registered nowhere in the checklist pin suite, so deleting or gutting it leaves every row green — the exact failure that set of pins was built to stop.
  evidence: `test/architecture/runtime_checklists_test.dart`'s `_sessionChecklist.notCoveredHere` (:720-732) holds eight distinctive phrases, and the new bullet at `test/platform/runtime-observation-checklist.md:629-640` matches none of them. The `absorbed`/`missing` checks (:96-124) read only `*Settles:*` lines inside numbered steps, so the bullet is invisible to those too. That set's own comment records this failing before — "The two bullets three skip clauses route claims to by name. Deleting either left every row green" — and the bullet exists solely to stop a reader treating group C (*The summon, the dismissal, and the echo*) as covering the close control when the dismissal it covers is the focus-loss one. The fix is one phrase added to `notCoveredHere`, and it is filed rather than taken because spec-dw-114's Never list bans touching `runtime_checklists_test.dart` outright, and two prior sweep bundles deliberately left it alone. Raised by the verification-gap lens.
  status: open

- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-dw-114-single-exit-path-tray-quit.md`
  summary: Both runtime claims this story owes — DW-12's close-with-the-toplevel-still-mapped and DW-114's Quit-actually-stops-the-daemon — are recorded only inside always-skipped `fail()` reasons whose owning ledger ids are now closed, so nothing open and no numbered procedure step requires anyone to ever observe them.
  evidence: `_sessionChecklist.claims` (`test/architecture/runtime_checklists_test.dart:714`) is {DW-9, DW-25, DW-26, DW-39, DW-44, DW-50, DW-72} — neither DW-12 nor DW-114. `test/platform/panel_visibility_live_test.dart:49-51` says outright that "the session procedure carries no entry for" the close claim and that DW-12 deliberately added none; `tray_live_test.dart` names only DW-9 and DW-26, both `done`. DW-12's entire premise — that `setPreventClose(true)` makes `close` arrive with the toplevel still mapped, so the adapter's own `hide` is what puts the panel away — rests on a reading of `window_manager_plugin.cc` and on no observation at all: every binding-free close row drives `FakePanelWindow`, which is mapped by construction and cannot be destroyed, so the suite endorses the arm without ever establishing its premise. Registering the two claims means new numbered steps, renumbering every citation below them, and extending the pin suite's claim set and group maps — which spec-dw-114's Never list bans and which is the same reason the DW-20 entry above was filed rather than taken. Raised independently by the adversarial and verification-gap lenses.
  status: open

- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-dw-31-panel-window-event-reconciliation.md`
  summary: CAP-14's focus-loss dismissal now depends on the raw `focus` name reaching the adapter, and no test CI runs establishes that it does — a `window_manager` bump that routed `focus` only to the typed callback would silently kill the panel's click-away dismissal with every in-gate row green.
  evidence: Since DW-33 the adapter answers a `blur` only when a `focus` first said the window held the keyboard, so the dismissal needs two raw names where it needed one. The only executable check that `focus` traverses the real seam is `test/platform/window_manager_panel_window_test.dart`'s event row, and `.github/workflows/ci.yml:104` runs `dart test` over `test/application test/architecture test/domain test/infrastructure` only, with `:17` recording that `flutter test` — which carries every `test/platform` row — "stays out on purpose". The in-gate pin, `test/architecture/panel_event_forwarding_test.dart`, scans `WindowManagerPanelWindow.onWindowEvent`'s own body for an unfiltered `_events.add(eventName)`; it asserts nothing about whether the package routes `focus` into that hook. The route exists today (`window_manager-0.5.2/lib/src/window_manager.dart:57`, `linux/window_manager_plugin.cc:973-977`) and `pubspec.yaml:40` pins `0.5.2` exactly, so this is bump exposure rather than drift — but the failure is silent and user-visible, and DW-12's `close` dependency has the same shape. The candidate fix is a source scan of the pinned package's dispatch added to the existing in-gate architecture pin, which is a new test pattern with its own fragility and deserves its own rows rather than a review-pass edit. Raised by the verification-gap lens in the 2026-08-15 follow-up review pass.
  status: open

- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-dw-31-panel-window-event-reconciliation.md`
  summary: The ban on renumbering checklist steps has now permanently exempted two rounds of runtime claims from ever being observed.
  evidence: A group C step for these claims would renumber steps 8-16 and every `_Citation` below them in `runtime_checklists_test.dart` — a file every sweep bundle's Never list forbids touching, so the step cannot be added without the ban being lifted first. DW-12 set the precedent and this is the second claim to inherit it; `## Not covered here` is now the section that grows while the numbered procedure does not. Raised independently by two lenses in the 2026-08-15 review pass and recorded in that spec's Review Triage Log at the time, because the then-current invocation forbade ledger edits outright; transcribed here under the follow-up pass's invocation, which permits new entries.

DW-12 and DW-32/DW-33 native claims remain unobserved. No checklist step was added or renumbered, and no runtime observation, test, gate, or CI rule was introduced.

- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-dw-31-panel-window-event-reconciliation.md`
  summary: A window event landing in `_apply`'s gap between the pre-`focus()` re-check and the call itself lets `gtk_window_present` re-map a window the mirror has given up on.
  evidence: `_apply` re-checks `_visible != intended` and *then* awaits `focus()`. A `minimize` (now unguarded), a `close`, or an external `hide` delivered in that gap writes the mirror to false, and the `focus()` already in flight then deiconifies and raises the window — mapped and focused with `isVisible` false. The correcting `restore`/`show` echo is swallowed if it arrives while `_outstanding` is still up. The follow-up pass found a second consequence of the same gap: while the mirror is wrong, the `focus` arm's `_visible` guard discards the genuine focus-in, and nothing re-arms `_focused` when a later echo corrects the mirror, so CAP-14 also misses that panel's next click-away. Narrow and usually self-correcting, but a fix means restructuring `_apply`'s re-check discipline, which is wider than that change. Recorded in the spec's Review Triage Log on 2026-08-15 under an invocation that forbade ledger edits; transcribed here under the follow-up pass's invocation.
  status: done 2026-09-26
  resolution: Plan 02-09 retains native request identity through timeout and settlement in `window_manager_panel_visibility.dart`, checks current intent at native call boundaries, and repairs a stale successful call after newer intent settles. Its existing adapter tests cover modeled event orders. The installed plugin's focus behavior was inspected, but real X11/Wayland event order and keyboard ownership were not observed.

- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-dw-31-panel-window-event-reconciliation.md`
  summary: Story 5's frozen `<intent-contract>` and its claim that DW-30-DW-33 were "implemented exactly as written" are now false for DW-32 and DW-33.
  evidence: `_bmad-output/specs/spec-hotkey-grammar-corrector/stories/5-clipboard-and-panel-visibility-adapters.md:65` still carries the widened row verbatim, and `:288` still asserts the boundary was honoured. The widening is recorded in `spec-dw-31-panel-window-event-reconciliation.md`'s Spec Change Log instead, because `_bmad-output/specs/` is out of scope for a sweep bundle and `SPEC.md` is never hand-edited. Whoever regenerates that surface needs to know the row moved. Recorded in that spec's Review Triage Log on 2026-08-15 under an invocation that forbade ledger edits; transcribed here under the follow-up pass's invocation.
  status: done 2026-09-26
  resolution: Plan 02-19 appends a dated disposition outside story 5's frozen `<intent-contract>`, identifying the old "exactly as written" claim as historical for DW-32/DW-33 and citing `_onBlur`/`_releaseDeferredBlur` as shipped. The original contract text remains for provenance; this is a documented correction, not a claim that the old wording was implemented literally. Native blur/focus order remains unobserved.

- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-dw-31-panel-window-event-reconciliation.md`
  summary: A focus-in the adapter caused itself is indistinguishable from the user's, so `_releaseDeferredBlur`'s `_focused` re-check can swallow the very dismissal DW-32 exists to answer.
  evidence: Reproduced in this container against `9fb62d0`. `_apply`'s show path awaits two calls — `_window.show` then `_window.focus` — and `windowManager.show()` is `gtk_widget_show`, preceded by `restore()` (`gtk_window_deiconify` + `gtk_window_present`) when the window was iconified, so it raises focus-ins of its own. One landing in the gap before `focus()`'s reply is strictly earlier than the release, and the `focus` arm admits it because `show()` already wrote the mirror true. Probe: panel visible and focused, a second `show()` (AD-14's second launch / AD-12's tray entry — DW-32's own flow) parked, `blur`, then that map's `focus` → calls end `[show, focus, show, focus]`, no `hide`, panel stays up; the same sequence without the map's focus-in ends `[show, focus, show, focus, hide]` and dismisses correctly. A second consequence of the same inability, same probe file: a compositor that focuses the newly mapped toplevel and then hands the keyboard back inside the round trip yields `[show, focus, hide]` — the panel dismissed at the moment it was summoned, DW-33's symptom reached through DW-32's latch, where the pre-change code dropped that blur and left the panel up. Both are the contract's literal rule ("the window must not have taken focus back in between") applied to a focus-in nobody attributed. Narrowing it means deciding a focus-in is ours rather than the user's: the `PanelWindow` seam carries no signal that says which, spec-dw-31's Never list forbids widening it, and a heuristic on `_outstanding` answers the click-back case wrongly instead — which is the case the re-check exists for. The corrected `_releaseDeferredBlur` doc now states this rather than claiming our own focus-in can never arrive first. Distinct from the `_apply`-gap entry above, which is about a *wrong* mirror; here the mirror is right and the attribution is missing. Probes kept at `scratchpad/probes/`. Raised by the edge-case-hunter lens in the 2026-08-20 follow-up review pass and confirmed by probe.
  status: done 2026-09-26
  resolution: Plan 02-09 adds expected self-focus tagging at `PanelWindow` in `window_manager_panel_window.dart`; `WindowManagerPanelVisibility` releases a deferred blur only for a user focus-in, not its own show/present focus. Existing fake adapter rows support the attribution policy. The plugin emits a bare focus event, so delayed or denied compositor focus may still be ambiguous; native ordering was not observed.

- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-dw-31-panel-window-event-reconciliation.md`
  summary: `FakePanelWindow` cannot model an iconified toplevel, so every DW-31 minimize/restore row asserts the window side as premise rather than claim and drops the pairing the rest of the suite relies on.
  evidence: `test/fakes/fake_panel_window.dart` carries one `bool visible` and no iconified state, and `emitEvent('minimize')`/`('restore')` change neither. The suite's own header calls `window.visible == visibility.isVisible` "that single pairing is what makes this suite able to fail for the real defect: the reverted attempt kept a correct mirror over a panel that was still on screen" — and the DW-31 rows are precisely the ones that cannot assert it, conceding in their own reasons that the fake "cannot model an iconified one, so this is the premise rather than the claim". The group therefore cannot distinguish "the mirror tracks the iconified window" from "the mirror happens to be false", which is the distinction between the shipped design and the reverted 2026-08-15 one. Candidate fix is small — a `bool iconified` field set from those two events plus an `onScreen => visible && !iconified` getter — but it re-measures every DW-31 row and the negative-control record's counts, so it deserves its own rows rather than a review-pass edit. Raised by the adversarial lens in the 2026-08-20 follow-up review pass, and independently in the pass before it, whose invocation forbade ledger edits; filed here under this pass's invocation, which permits new entries.
  status: done 2026-09-26
  resolution: Plan 02-12 gives `test/fakes/fake_panel_window.dart` separate mapped and iconified state and updates existing DW-31 minimize/restore rows in `window_manager_panel_visibility_test.dart` to assert the window side. This verifies the fake's claim under controlled events; live GTK or compositor delivery order was not observed.

- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-dw-31-panel-window-event-reconciliation.md`
  summary: A blur latched under a request that `_requestTimeout` abandons is released into a `hide` that races a window call still in flight.
  evidence: `_releaseDeferredBlur` fires from `_apply`'s `finally`, which is reached on the timeout path as well as the completion path — deliberately, and pinned as negative control 24, because a release hung off completion alone would leave the latch standing with nothing to reconsider it. The consequence is that the `hide` it issues can overlap a `show` or `focus` the window never answered, which is DW-28's accepted race reached through the new deferred route rather than a new defect: the abandoned call may still land afterwards and `gtk_window_present` maps a hidden toplevel. Documented in `_onBlur`'s paragraph and pinned by the DW-28-group row added for it; deliberately not changed, since bounding it means restructuring the abandonment discipline. Raised in the 2026-08-20 review pass under an invocation that forbade ledger edits; filed here under this pass's invocation, which permits new entries.
  status: done 2026-09-26
  resolution: Plan 02-09 keeps a timed-out `show` or `focus` registered as native work until the underlying future settles; a latched blur waits for that settlement and stale successful calls repair toward the latest intent before release. `window_manager_panel_visibility.dart` and its existing adapter tests support the modeled order. Native timeout and GTK callback ordering were not observed.

- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-dw-31-panel-window-event-reconciliation.md`
  summary: A latched blur does not suppress the show's trailing `focus()`, so `gtk_window_present` takes the keyboard from the application the user just clicked into and the release then hides the panel a moment later.
  evidence: Reproduced in this container against `36f9d47`, and pinned by the suite's own expectation: the DW-32 row at `test/infrastructure/panel/window_manager_panel_visibility_test.dart:1654` asserts `window.calls` ends `['show', 'focus', 'show', 'focus', 'hide']`, and the second `focus` is issued *after* the blur was latched. `_apply`'s pre-`focus()` re-check tests `_disposed || _visible != intended` only; a latched blur moves no mirror, so `_visible` still matches the intent and the call goes out. `_apply`'s own comment there gives exactly this reason for the re-check — "focusing now would steal the keyboard for a panel the user has already dismissed" — but only for a dismissal that moved the mirror, not for one held in `_deferredBlur`. The user-visible cost is narrow and real: keys typed in the gap land in a panel that is about to disappear, and where the keyboard goes afterwards is the window manager's choice rather than a return to the editor. Pre-change the blur was dropped entirely, so the trailing `focus()` was equally issued and the panel then stayed up — the call is not new, the disappearance after it is. The candidate fix is one clause (`|| _deferredBlur`) in that re-check, but it changes a pinned row's expected call list and lands in the same `_apply` re-check discipline the open `_apply`-gap entry above already says needs restructuring, so it belongs with that work rather than in a review pass. Raised by the edge-case-hunter lens in the 2026-08-20 second follow-up review pass and confirmed by probe.
  status: done 2026-09-26
  resolution: Plan 02-09 suppresses the show's trailing `focus()` when a deferred blur is latched in `window_manager_panel_visibility.dart`; existing adapter rows check that no focus call is issued for the modeled race. Source inspection supports the guard, while real compositor keyboard ownership after a late native present remains unobserved.

- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-dw-31-panel-window-event-reconciliation.md`
  summary: The `## Not covered here` bullet this change added for DW-32/DW-33 is unpinned, so deleting the three runtime claims it carries leaves the whole architecture gate green.
  evidence: Measured on `36f9d47` with the bullet restored from a scratchpad copy rather than `git checkout --`: `dart test test/architecture` reports 225 pass / 1 skip both with the bullet and with all 28 of its lines deleted. `test/architecture/runtime_checklists_test.dart`'s `_sessionChecklist.notCoveredHere` holds no phrase from it, and the `absorbed`/`missing` readers only inspect `*Settles:*` lines inside numbered steps, so the bullet is invisible to those too. Meanwhile `test/platform/panel_visibility_live_test.dart` tells a reader the boundary "is written up under `Not covered here`" — a sentence that would silently become false. Same mechanism and same one-phrase fix as the open entry above about the DW-12 close-control bullet, and blocked by the same ban: this spec's Never list forbids touching `runtime_checklists_test.dart` outright. Filed separately because the content is different and newer — this bullet is the sole home of three owed compositor claims (a summon that never gets the keyboard, a click-away during a second summon, and a window-manager re-map that hands the keyboard back), and the set now lacks a phrase for two bullets rather than one. Raised by the verification-gap lens in the 2026-08-20 second follow-up review pass and confirmed by measurement.
  status: open

The work was committed by bmad-loop run 20260814-084949-3773; this entry preserves the lingering recommendation for a deliberate later review.
status: open

- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-dw-30-panel-session-reason-and-marker.md`
  summary: CAP-2's success clause in `SPEC.md`, and AD-8's and AD-18's snippets in the architecture spine, are now falsified by the shipped code, and the only record of it lives in Dart doc comments.
  evidence: CAP-2 states the pre-fill unconditionally ("the panel opens pre-filled with the current clipboard text content") and AD-18 states "every `show()` starts a fresh panel session"; under the human's 2026-08-14 three-way rule only a *dismissed* departure re-seeds, so a summon after CAP-14's focus-loss hide — the panel's most common way of going away — reads no clipboard at all. AD-8's snippet still declares `Stream<bool> get changes` against a stream that is now four-valued. All three are true divergences rather than drift: the code is correct under the rule the human set, and the governing documents were not updated because `_bmad-output/` is off-limits to `lib/` and `test/` work. `panel_visibility.dart` records the AD-8 divergence in a doc comment and `correction_controller.dart` records the CAP-2 narrowing at `_onVisibilityChanged`, which is the most those files may do. The correction is the orchestrator's: regenerate CAP-2's success criterion, AD-18's rule and AD-8's snippet against the three-way rule. Until then a reader reconciling code against spec finds the code looking like the defect. Raised by the adversarial lens in the 2026-08-20 second follow-up review pass; filed here under this pass's invocation, which permits new entries.
  status: done 2026-09-26
  resolution: Plan 02-19 regenerates CAP-2's success clause from the approved three-way session rule: dismissal seeds fresh clipboard text, while iconify and focus-loss returns retain the session. Plan 02-21 re-derives AD-8/AD-18 in `ARCHITECTURE-SPINE.md` to match the shipped `PanelVisibility` states and controller behavior. The corrections are source/document comparison; they do not observe native gesture-to-event mapping.

- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-dw-30-panel-session-reason-and-marker.md`
  summary: The gesture-to-event-name premise the whole departure attribution table rests on is assumed rather than observed, and it now decides whether the user's typed text survives.
  evidence: Which real gesture arrives at the adapter as `minimize` rather than `hide` or `close` is what separates a session that survives from one that ends: a workspace switch or show-desktop gesture is assumed to raise GTK `minimize` (attributed `iconified`, session survives) and a window close control to raise `close` (attributed `dismissed`, session ends, clipboard re-seeded). Every row proving the table runs against `FakePanelWindow`, which emits whatever name the row hands it, so the mapping is verified nowhere against a real compositor. If one delivers a dismissal as `minimize`, the panel keeps work the user asked to be rid of; the reverse discards work they were keeping. It is booked as an owed claim in the skip reason of `test/platform/panel_visibility_live_test.dart`, but a skip string has no owner and no severity — the runtime observation checklist's numbered steps are DW-9's to own and were out of scope for this bundle, whose Never list forbids touching `runtime_checklists_test.dart`. Wants a numbered checklist step so the claim is tracked rather than parked in prose. Raised by the adversarial and verification-gap lenses across the 2026-08-20 review passes; the pass that first deferred it was invoked under a ban on ledger edits, so it is filed here.
  status: open

- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-dw-30-panel-session-reason-and-marker.md`
  summary: An abandoned `hide` whose late echo lands after a completed `show` is attributed `dismissed` even when the hide was issued under a focus loss, so a click-away plus a summon plus the echo can still discard the user's typed text.
  evidence: A focus-loss hide abandoned at `_requestTimeout` (DW-28) leaves its platform call in flight with `_outstanding` back at zero. The 2026-08-20 pass gated the `hide` arm on `_visible` and pinned it (negative control 31, and the DW-28-group row), which closes the case where the echo lands while the mirror still reads false. It does not close the case where the user summons the panel in between: `show()` repairs the mirror to true, the echo then passes the gate, and `_reconcile(PanelVisibilityState.dismissed)` reports a dismissal the user never made — the next summon clears the editor and re-seeds from the clipboard, which is DW-30's own defect through the one remaining door. The attribution is honest about where the window is (it really did unmap), and that is why it was not patched: fixing it means attributing a departure from the intent the abandoned call was *issued* under rather than from the event that echoed, i.e. carrying the departure alongside the in-flight request, which is a design change to the abandonment discipline rather than a review-pass edit. It also interacts with the open `spec-dw-31` entries about `_apply`'s re-check. Raised by the edge-case-hunter lens in the 2026-08-20 second follow-up review pass, and deferred in the pass before it under an invocation that forbade ledger edits.
  status: done 2026-09-26
  resolution: Plan 02-10 makes the owning hide request report `focusLost` or `dismissed` and ignores its unlabelled GTK `hide` echo in `window_manager_panel_visibility.dart`; `PanelController` forwards only a focus loss matching the current hidden mirror. Existing fake tests cover late echoes and byte-preserved editor state. Installed plugin source explains the unlabelled GTK callback, but its order relative to replies and later shows was not observed on X11 or Wayland.

- source_spec: `.planning/phases/01-hotkey-truth/01-01-PLAN.md`
  summary: 2026-09-01 — the human ratified `status-type` as the shape of `GlobalHotkey`'s current-registration member: one new member `HotkeyStatus? get current` returning a new domain type carrying an `outcome` plus a nullable `backendDescription`, synchronous, with no round trip and no microtask on the settings-screen mount path. This is the AD-9 port-surface addition HOTKEY-06 needs, and it also carries HOTKEY-03's compositor-supplied localized description text on the same member rather than on a declared field of an existing type.
  evidence: Option id `status-type`, ratified at the ARCH-02/HOTKEY-06 decision gate of plan 01-01. Class of change: an **addition** to AD-9's verbatim declaration, not a declared-field edit — no declared field of `HotkeyRegistration` (which still declares exactly `effective` and `authority`), `HotkeyBound` or `HotkeyUnavailable` is touched. The precedent it rests on is in-tree and explicit: `lib/src/domain/hotkey/global_hotkey.dart:50-54`, the `bindingChanges` doc, which records that such an addition is "recorded in the deferred-work ledger and the story's Spec Change Log rather than hand-edited into the spine". AD-9 lines touched: the member list at 281–300 within the frozen block, whose fixed-part rule is stated at `ARCHITECTURE-SPINE.md:296`. The rejected alternatives and why: a third declared field on `HotkeyRegistration` (spine 248–254) would have been a declared-field-list edit needing its own fresh ratification; a replaying `bindingChanges` is not synchronous, so a mounting settings screen would still await a microtask, which is precisely what FLAT-02's cited AD-8 precedent (`bool get isVisible` paired with `Stream<bool> get changes`) avoids, and it changes the semantics of an already-declared member rather than adding one; an application-ring cache in `SettingsController` is only as fresh as the last event it happened to be listening for, which is the defect FLAT-02 describes rather than a fix for it. Also note the read-back's real limit (D-05): no field of any GlobalShortcuts reply carries a machine-readable combination — `description` and `trigger_description` are the only shortcut properties in the Response, the `ListShortcuts` reply and the `ShortcutsChanged` signal — so `backendDescription` is displayed, never parsed into a `HotkeyBinding`. Stays open until plan 01-06 lands the code and plan 01-10 closes FLAT-02; this entry records the ratification only, and no `lib/` file was edited by plan 01-01.
  status: open

- source_spec: `.planning/phases/01-hotkey-truth/01-01-PLAN.md`
  summary: Phase 7's ARCH cluster (ARCH-06) owes an ARCHITECTURE-SPINE.md reconciliation for the four AD-9 declaration edits Phase 1 makes, plus AD-11's step 1 becoming conditional. Phase 1 deliberately edited **no line** of the spine: the in-tree precedent forbids hand-editing it, and this entry is the hand-off so the reconciliation is not discovered late or lost.
  evidence: Four AD-9 declaration edits, each with its spine line range. (1) The port-surface addition — `HotkeyStatus? get current` on `GlobalHotkey`, ratified 2026-09-01 as the entry above records: member list 281–300 within the frozen block 281–300. (2) `HotkeyUnavailable` gains a machine-readable `cause` discriminator (`noBackend` / `keyRefused` / `revoked`), plan 01-04, HOTKEY-08/D-06: 274–278. (3) HOTKEY-03's localized description carrier — **folded into edit (1)** by the `status-type` ratification, so spine 248–254 (`HotkeyRegistration`'s declared fields) is **not** touched; had `third-field` been chosen it would have been. (4) `HotkeyBinding` gains a defensively-copied or unmodifiable `modifiers` behind its `const` constructor, plan 01-09, HOTKEY-09: 239–243 — the edit CONTEXT.md filed under "Claude's Discretion" without noticing it touches the frozen block. Additionally, and outside AD-9: AD-11's step 1 (host portal registry `Register`) becomes conditional on a sandbox predicate as a consequence of the three-format packaging decision closed at DW-89 on 2026-09-01, so the AD-11 call sequence itself needs the same reconciliation. The governing rule is `ARCHITECTURE-SPINE.md:296` — "the declared fields and constructors above are the fixed part" — over the block at 235–300. Three of the four AD-9 edits are additions covered by the `bindingChanges` precedent at `lib/src/domain/hotkey/global_hotkey.dart:50-54`; the two needing fresh ratification are gated at the head of the plans that make them (01-04 and 01-09). Counting note for the reconciler: the roadmap names one such edit and CONTEXT.md's D-06 says three — there are four.
  status: open

- source_spec: `.planning/phases/01-hotkey-truth/01-01-PLAN.md`
  summary: A Flatpak that actually runs the shipped default correction provider needs the Python sidecar and the `claude` CLI reachable from inside the sandbox, and neither is — so the deferred packaging phase owes either bundling both into the runtime or a `flatpak-spawn --host` escape that would contradict D-19's narrowest-permission commitment outright. Filed against the packaging phase as an open cost, discovered while closing DW-89 on 2026-09-01 and recorded separately so it is not buried in that entry's resolution.
  evidence: The shipped default provider is three processes deep — daemon → Python sidecar → `claude` CLI — and the spine's runtime envelope (`ARCHITECTURE-SPINE.md:583`) lists a Python 3.11+ interpreter with `claude_agent_sdk` **plus** the `claude` CLI on `PATH` as dependencies the app cannot supply itself. Flatpak's default sandbox has "no access to any host files except the runtime, the app, `~/.var/app/$FLATPAK_ID`, and `$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR/app/$FLATPAK_ID`" [CITED: docs.flatpak.org — Sandbox Permissions], so neither host binary is reachable. The two ways out are not equivalent: bundling both into the runtime is compatible with the recorded permission set (`--socket=wayland`, `--socket=fallback-x11`, `--share=ipc`, `--talk-name=org.freedesktop.secrets`, no `--filesystem=`), whereas the escape route needs `--talk-name=org.freedesktop.Flatpak` plus `flatpak-spawn --host`, which is the opposite of D-19's commitment and must not be taken as a shortcut to make a pipeline easier to build. This blocks nothing in Phase 1 — no code here depends on it — but it materially changes what "ship a Flatpak" costs, and the packaging phase should not meet it for the first time mid-build. [ASSUMED] that no such bundling exists today: reasoned from `assets/sidecar/` shipping as a Flutter asset and `tool/provision_sidecar.sh` creating a `.venv-sidecar/` outside the bundle, **not verified end to end** (flagged assumption A6 of `.planning/phases/01-hotkey-truth/01-RESEARCH.md`). If that assumption is wrong, the cost recorded here overstates the work and this entry should close on that finding rather than on new bundling work.
  status: open

- source_spec: `.planning/phases/01-hotkey-truth/01-10-PLAN.md`
  summary: Phase 7's ARCH cluster (ARCH-06) owes the envelope amendment implied by HOTKEY-10's dissolution, filed 2026-09-02 when FLAT-11 closed: drop `libkeybinder-3.0-0` from the spine's runtime-dependency list at `ARCHITECTURE-SPINE.md:583`, delete the paragraph at `:584` asserting that dependency is hard today together with its "Re-measure when the plugin version moves" sentence, and remove the Stack table's now-false `| hotkey_manager | 0.2.3 |` row at `:442`. Phase 1 deliberately edited **no line** of the spine; this entry is the instruction, not a diff.
  evidence: Measured 2026-09-02 against the tree plan 01-02 (commit `6abf480`) left: `grep -c 'hotkey_manager' pubspec.lock` -> `0`, so the package the `:584` paragraph measures is not resolved at all; `readelf -d <release exe> | grep -c hotkey_manager` -> `0` and `ldd <release exe> | grep -c keybinder` -> `0`, so the runner→plugin→`libkeybinder-3.0.so.0` chain that paragraph describes as the mechanism of the hardness no longer exists (`.planning/phases/01-hotkey-truth/01-02-SUMMARY.md`, coverage id D2). Its replacement adds nothing to the list: `libX11.so.6` is an unconditional `DT_NEEDED` of `libgdk-3.so.0` (`readelf -d /usr/lib/x86_64-linux-gnu/libgdk-3.so.0`, measured 2026-09-01, 01-RESEARCH.md § C7), so `DynamicLibrary.open('libX11.so.6')` in `lib/src/infrastructure/hotkey/x11_key_grab_registrar.dart` returns a handle to an already-loaded copy on any host where the app starts. **Three further spine sentences are falsified by the same removal and are named here so the reconciler does not have to re-derive them** (beyond the two the closure text cites): `:300`, which says `hotkey_manager`'s Linux plugin builds the keybinder accelerator in C and no Dart under `lib/` builds one — the X11 registrar now resolves keysyms itself; `:451`, the note dating and scoping the `hotkey_manager` pin; and `:498`, the Structural Seed comment annotating `x11_global_hotkey.dart` as "# hotkey_manager / keybinder-3.0". **The one conditional that would change this amendment, recorded so Phase 7 need not re-derive it:** if a future route re-introduces keybinder (DW-39's superseded `decision:` line is the shape), the `:584` clause **changes rather than disappears** — `libkeybinder-3.0-0` becomes a *degrading* dependency, because `DynamicLibrary.open` makes its absence a caught error rather than a loader failure before `main()`, and it rejoins the set the envelope already characterises that way. Nothing in Phase 1 depends on this amendment landing; what it costs is a spine that tells a packager to ship a library the binary does not link.
  status: open

- source_spec: `.planning/phases/01-hotkey-truth/01-10-PLAN.md`
  summary: The Wayland portal app-id registration is never re-issued after an `xdg-desktop-portal` restart: `_registered` latches true once and is never reset, so after a portal restart the app-id association is gone and every later bind is liable to the discard this adapter already documents. Found while plan 01-05 had the portal surface open; **out of scope for Phase 1's ten requirements and filed rather than fixed**, because filing while the file is open is cheap and fixing is scope creep.
  evidence: `lib/src/infrastructure/hotkey/wayland_portal_global_hotkey.dart:258` declares `bool _registered = false;`, `:697` sets it true, and `:586` is the guard that then short-circuits `_registerApplicationIdOnce` forever — nothing resets it on any path. The adapter's own `_portalSender` doc at `:1091` calls an `xdg-desktop-portal` restart "routine on a session update" and works around it for the *sender*, but not for the registration. The upstream Registry documentation states that applications "should listen for `NameOwnerChanged` signals to re-register after portal service restarts" [CITED: flatpak.github.io/xdg-desktop-portal, `org.freedesktop.host.portal.Registry`]. Verified 2026-09-02 that nothing implements it: `grep -rc 'NameOwnerChanged' lib/` returns **0** across every file. Closing this needs a `NameOwnerChanged` watch — `_listenForShortcutSignals` at `:1174` is where it would go — plus a reset of `_registered` and a re-issue of step 1; it interacts with AD-11's fixed four-step sequence, which is why it is not a one-line change. Note the blast radius is bounded on an unsandboxed build only: inside a Flatpak the sandbox supplies the app id and plan 01-05's `PortalAppAppIdRegime` predicate skips the host registry entirely, so this defect cannot occur there. Recorded explicitly as **scoped out** by plan 01-05, not overlooked.
  status: open

- source_spec: `.planning/phases/01-hotkey-truth/01-10-PLAN.md`
  summary: Phase 7's ARCH cluster (ARCH-06) owes an AD-10 amendment: the spine's ratified stance requires the settings screen to show which regime — authoritative on X11, advisory on Wayland — is active alongside the effective combination, and plan 01-06 **dropped that label from the UI** on the user's decision D-03. This is an amendment to a **user-ratified** spine decision, not a drift correction, and must be reconciled as such rather than silently re-aligned in either direction.
  evidence: `ARCHITECTURE-SPINE.md:620` reads: "**Ratified by the user on 2026-08-06:** ... In-app settings are therefore **authoritative on X11 and advisory on Wayland**, and the settings screen shows which regime is active alongside the effective combination read back from the portal **whenever a combination is in effect**". Plan 01-06 (`810aac7`) removed the regime label from `lib/src/ui/settings/hotkey_status_view.dart` per D-03, on the reasoning that a user is told what is in effect and what they can change, and that naming the display-server mechanism is the app explaining its own plumbing. What replaced it is not silence: on X11 the screen renders the reported combination, and on Wayland it renders the compositor's own `trigger_description` verbatim under a line naming whose words it is — so the *consequence* of the regime is still on screen even though the *label* is not. **The reconciliation is therefore a judgement, not a transcription**, which is why it is handed to a phase with a human in the loop: either the spine sentence narrows to require the consequence rather than the label, or D-03 is revisited. Two related records the reconciler should read together with this one: plan 01-07's gate answer accepted, on record, that the capture control states a mechanism difference between the display servers out loud, which D-03's "one UI everywhere" argues against — weighed and accepted, and not to be suppressed to satisfy D-03 (`.planning/phases/01-hotkey-truth/01-GATE-ANSWERS.md`); and DW-66's closure of 2026-09-02, which records the same amendment from the other side.
  status: open

- source_spec: `.planning/phases/01-hotkey-truth/01-10-PLAN.md`
  summary: Phase 7's ARCH cluster (ARCH-06) owes an AD-12 amendment: the shipped adapter recorded that the portal **bind** path has no timeout *by intent*, and plan 01-05 bounded it under the user's decision D-17. The amendment must carry the **carve-out that preserves the original reason** — nothing is sent when a wait expires — or a later reader will re-derive a daemon that cancels portal dialogs the user is still reading.
  evidence: The stance being amended was explicit in the source: "Deliberately on the teardown path alone. The bind path has no timeout by intent: AD-11 makes a portal dialog the user must answer normal, and cancelling one out from under them would be worse than waiting" [VERIFIED at `lib/src/infrastructure/hotkey/wayland_portal_global_hotkey.dart:1300-1307` before plan 01-05; the paragraph was replaced by that plan, and `.planning/phases/01-hotkey-truth/01-RESEARCH.md` § C4 quotes it in full]. What landed (plan 01-05, `2967b89`): the name-owner lookup, the advisory `Register` and `CreateSession` are bounded on `main.dart`'s existing `_unresponsiveCallBudget`, threaded through `DaemonStartup.begin` into the adapter's constructor; `BindShortcuts` — the one step with a dialog behind it — is bounded on a **derived multiple** of the same number (`_requestBudget * _dialogBudgetFactor`, factor 12, one minute at the shipped five seconds), expressed as a named integer factor rather than a second `Duration` so there is still exactly one authored duration in the file. **The carve-out, which is the half that must not be lost:** nothing is sent on any timeout path — no portal `Close`, no retry, no second call of any kind — so the request stays live on the portal and a dialog the user is mid-way through answering is never withdrawn. An abandoned `BindShortcuts` leaves its session **tracked** rather than closed or forgotten, so a rebind closes it first and a late `Allow` still toggles the panel while the bind's own answer stays honestly unavailable. **Owed and NOT closed:** no Wayland claim here is from a run — this container has no session bus, no `xdg-desktop-portal` and no compositor — so that a portal really does leave a dialog on screen after this app stops waiting, and that a late `Allow` then produces a working press plus a `ShortcutsChanged`, remain owed (`.planning/phases/01-hotkey-truth/01-05-SUMMARY.md`; filed in `.planning/WINDOWS.md`).
  status: open

- source_spec: `.planning/phases/01-hotkey-truth/01-10-PLAN.md`
  summary: One hang path on the Wayland bind sequence is still unbounded, **deliberately**: `_closeSessionBeforeRebinding`'s `Session.Close` is a dialogless bind-path call that plan 01-05 left unbounded because bounding it would break the one-`Close`-in-flight invariant. Filed at plan 01-05's own explicit request so the exception to D-17 is a ledger entry rather than a line in a SUMMARY.
  evidence: `lib/src/infrastructure/hotkey/wayland_portal_global_hotkey.dart:1402` declares `Future<HotkeyBindOutcome?> _closeSessionBeforeRebinding(...)`, called from `:449` on the rebind path. It is the **fourth** dialogless bind-path call; plan 01-05's task list named three plus the dialog step, and this one was found during execution. The correctness cost of bounding it, in that plan's words: `_session`'s doc requires at most one `Close` in flight per session, and restoring `_session` after abandoning a `Close` that may still land would let `dispose()` send a second one. Closing this needs a way to track a session as "closing, unconfirmed", which is more machinery than plan 01-05 authorised. Recorded exactly as that plan asked: "Plan 01-10 has two entries to file, not one. The `NameOwnerChanged` re-registration defect the plan already names, and the unbounded rebind `Close` recorded above" (`.planning/phases/01-hotkey-truth/01-05-SUMMARY.md`). Note what is and is not at risk: the user-visible D-17 guarantee holds — the settings screen cannot hang on the bind steps that have no dialog behind them, and `BindShortcuts` itself is bounded — but a portal that accepts a `Close` and never answers can still park this one call indefinitely on the rebind path.
  status: open

- source_spec: `.planning/phases/01-hotkey-truth/01-10-PLAN.md`
  summary: An index for the measured facts that lived only in the doc comments of the two files plan 01-02 deleted, so a future reader wondering what the old `hotkey_manager` seam knew has one place to look. **No code work is owed by this entry** — every fact was checked on 2026-09-02 and each has a named surviving home; it is filed open as an index a documentation pass may close.
  evidence: Four first-hand measurements with upstream file:line citations sat in `lib/src/infrastructure/hotkey/hotkey_manager_registrar.dart` (242 lines) and `hotkey_registrar.dart`'s replaced doc, both changed by plan 01-02 (`6abf480`). AGENTS.md §1's "no dead code, git remembers it" is right about code and wrong about this: git does not surface a deleted comment to the next reader wondering why the registrar releases the way it does. Audited 2026-09-02, all four survive with a home. **Still binding, re-homed into the new registrar's doc:** (1) the plugin's native side keyed its keystring table with `std::map::insert`, which does **not** overwrite an existing key (`hotkey_manager_linux-0.2.0/linux/hotkey_manager_linux_plugin.cc:91-92`), so registering twice under one identifier left the first keystring in the map and both combinations bound — one press ran two handlers and AD-8's toggle showed and immediately hid — which is why an adapter must never hold more than one combination; now at `lib/src/infrastructure/hotkey/x11_key_grab_registrar.dart:44-69`, where plan 01-03 also **inverted the ordering** to acquire-then-release, on three facts measured against a live `Xvfb` (2026-09-01). (2) the plugin discarded `keybinder_bind`'s `gboolean` and answered `true` regardless (`:96`, `:99-100`); re-homed in the same doc. **Retired, and where each now lives:** (3) the keystring was built natively — `gtk_accelerator_name(keyval, mods)` in C from what the plugin was sent (`hotkey_manager_linux_plugin.cc:89-90`), which probed against GTK 3 yields `<Shift><Control>g` for Ctrl+Shift+G, a different spelling *and* order from AD-9's `<Ctrl><Shift>g` illustration — still in-tree with its citation at `lib/src/infrastructure/hotkey/hotkey_key_catalogue.dart:8-12`, and note it is now historical, since `X11KeyGrabRegistrar` resolves keysyms itself and no accelerator string is built anywhere. (4) the `keybinder_unbind`-on-uninitialised-`const char*` undefined behaviour, reachable from a path this project controlled when a `std::find_if` missed — carried in DW-42's own `resolution:` in this file with every plugin line (`:34`, `:41-42`, `:46`, `:106`, `:112-113`, `:115`), which is why DW-42 was left untouched by this phase and is cited rather than restated. The `uni_platform` reverse-table cause of DW-43 likewise survives, at `hotkey_key_catalogue.dart:120-146`, with the three-way 2026-09-01 measurement that showed the defect was the vendor package's and not X11's, keybinder's or GTK's.
  status: open

- source_spec: `.planning/phases/01-hotkey-truth/01-10-PLAN.md`
  summary: Confirmation and correction of plan 01-01's Phase-7 (ARCH-06) AD-9 hand-off entry, written 2026-09-02 **after** all four declaration edits actually landed. That entry was filed in prediction form before any code existed; this one records the shipped names, the current spine line numbers, and — the load-bearing correction — that **one of the four was orchestrator-selected, not human-ratified**. The earlier entry is left untouched and unrewritten; both stand, and this is the operative one for line numbers and names.
  evidence: Corrections, in the order the earlier entry lists its four edits. (1) *Port-surface addition* — shipped as `HotkeyStatus? get current` at `lib/src/domain/hotkey/global_hotkey.dart:135`, type `HotkeyStatus` in the new file `lib/src/domain/hotkey/hotkey_status.dart` with fields `outcome` and `backendDescription`; **nothing was renamed** from 01-01's ratification. Spine: the `GlobalHotkey` member list at `ARCHITECTURE-SPINE.md:280-300`, fixed-part rule at `:296`. Addition class — no declared field of `HotkeyRegistration`, `HotkeyBound` or `HotkeyUnavailable` touched by this one. (2) *`HotkeyUnavailable` gains a cause* — shipped as `enum HotkeyUnavailableCause { noBackend, keyRefused, revoked }` at `lib/src/domain/hotkey/hotkey_bind_outcome.dart:51`, with the field **required and defaultless** and equality extended to `Object.hash(cause, message)`. Spine: `:274` still reads `const HotkeyUnavailable({required this.message});`, within `:274-278`. **Human-ratified** 2026-09-01. (3) *HOTKEY-03's description carrier* — **folded into edit (1)** by the `status-type` ratification, exactly as the earlier entry predicted, so `HotkeyRegistration`'s declared field list (spine `:248-254`) is **not** touched and still declares exactly `effective` and `authority`. (4) *`HotkeyBinding`'s constructor* — **the earlier entry's wording is now wrong and is corrected here rather than edited there.** It predicted the type would gain "a defensively-copied or unmodifiable `modifiers` **behind its `const` constructor**"; what shipped **drops `const`** — `HotkeyBinding({required Set<HotkeyModifier> modifiers, required this.key}) : modifiers = Set<HotkeyModifier>.unmodifiable(modifiers);` at `lib/src/domain/hotkey/hotkey_binding.dart:6-7` — because a `const` constructor cannot call `Set.unmodifiable`. Spine: `:246` still reads `const HotkeyBinding({required this.modifiers, required this.key});`. **And it was NOT human-ratified.** The earlier entry says "the two needing fresh ratification are gated at the head of the plans that make them (01-04 and 01-09)": 01-04's gate was answered by the human on 2026-09-01; **01-09's was answered by the orchestrator on 2026-09-02 under the user's instruction to complete the phase unattended, after the user declined the interactive gate.** Phase 7 MUST re-confirm that one with a human before editing `:246`, and must not read the earlier entry's "gated" as "ratified" (`.planning/phases/01-hotkey-truth/01-GATE-ANSWERS.md`). Additionally, and outside AD-9, both still owed and confirmed shipped: **AD-11 step 1** at `ARCHITECTURE-SPINE.md:314` becomes conditional on a sandbox predicate — plan 01-05 (`cbb3354`) landed `PortalAppIdRegime`, so a sandboxed build must not call `org.freedesktop.host.portal.Registry.Register` while an unsandboxed one must, which makes step 1 a variable in a sequence AD-11 declares an invariant; and the spine's own deferred note at `:613` already anticipates it. A fifth AD-9-adjacent change this phase made that the earlier entry could not have predicted: plan 01-08 wrote the **precedence rule between `bind()`'s answer and `bindingChanges`** into the port doc (`global_hotkey.dart:53-60`), which AD-9 and AD-10 between them never stated — that is doc text rather than a declared field or constructor, so the `bindingChanges` precedent at `global_hotkey.dart:50-54` covers it, but the spine still owes the sentence. Three further spine amendments from this phase are filed as their own entries appended 2026-09-02: the envelope/Stack `hotkey_manager` removal, AD-10's regime-display rule at `:620`, and AD-12's bind-path timeout stance.
  status: open

### DW-122: the daemon dismissed its own panel on its own shortcut — CAP-14's toggle never hid a visible panel (G-01-13)

origin: `.planning/phases/01-hotkey-truth/01-12-PLAN.md`, raised as G-01-13 by `01-UAT.md` tests 13 and 14, 2026-09-10
location: lib/src/infrastructure/panel/window_manager_panel_visibility.dart, lib/src/infrastructure/panel/{keyboard_focus_witness,x11_keyboard_focus_witness,absent_keyboard_focus_witness}.dart, lib/src/infrastructure/system/daemon_startup.dart, lib/main.dart, test/platform/panel-toggle-observation.md
severity: high
reason: Once the panel was visible, the bound combination never hid it — the product's core gesture, half-broken, through a whole phase of green rows. The shape of the defect is why it survived: it is an **AND-gate**, and either half alone reads as healthy. **Half one, the self-inflicted dismissal:** the daemon's own passive grab generates a `FocusOut(NotifyGrab)` on the panel at the instant the combination is pressed, and `_onBlur` treated *any* focus-out as a CAP-14 dismissal, so the daemon dismissed its own panel on its own shortcut. **Half two, the race it then wins:** that dismissal raced the toggle's own activation and arrived first, so the panel unmapped and the toggle immediately re-mapped it — 8-16 ms apart, measured (`01-UAT.md` test 13; plan 01-11's captured stream timestamps `FocusOut mode=NotifyGrab` at `.865`, `UnmapNotify` at `.868` and `MapNotify` at `.879`). Because the two events straddle the beat, `xwininfo`'s `Map State` reports `IsViewable` on **both** sides of it, which is how a state oracle certified a flickering panel as fine and why the defect needed an event-stream oracle (`tool/uat/panel_toggle_probe.sh`, plan 01-11) to be seen at all. **A reorder-only fix was rejected on a measurement rather than on taste.** Both intents are asynchronous and land on the same event queue — the blur travels X -> GDK -> the platform channel -> the Dart main isolate, the activation travels X -> the grab worker's poll -> an isolate message -> that same queue — so there is no ordering guarantee available to be won; removing the registrar's 8 ms poll widens a 4 ms margin without closing anything, and it reopens T-01-09, since a blocked `XNextEvent` cannot notice a teardown message (`x11_key_grab_registrar.dart:585-591`). Bridging the gap with a latch plus a deadline was rejected for the same reason and by AGENTS.md 6 outright: papering over a race with a delay is not closing it. **How this stands beside DW-33, which is the entry a reader will reach for.** DW-33 asks *did this window ever hold the keyboard?* and answers a `blur` that arrives with no preceding `focus`. Its question is unchanged, its code is unchanged, and it still runs **first** — `window_manager_panel_visibility.dart:1042-1047` returns before any X round trip is spent, so DW-33's case never reaches the new one. The question added here is a different question: *has the keyboard actually gone anywhere?* Two distinct non-dismissals, and the second was put beside the first rather than in place of it. Neither subsumes the other: DW-33's case has no witnessed focus to compare against, and this one has a witnessed focus that never moved. Nothing about DW-33's reasoning was overwritten to make room for it.
status: done 2026-09-10
resolution: Closed by plan 01-12 (`dc6079b`, `961fd61`, `791ddb9`, `d78da63`) and proved live by plan 01-13 (`4a6cb5e`, `4a0e25c`). What was built is a question with a race-free answer rather than a wider race: a `KeyboardFocusWitness` seam (`lib/src/infrastructure/panel/keyboard_focus_witness.dart`) whose X11 arm answers *is the keyboard still where it was when we last held it?* from **`XGetInputFocus` alone** (`x11_keyboard_focus_witness.dart:93` for `focusUnmoved`, the symbol lookup at `:226`). That is race-free in both directions, because a keyboard grab does not move the input focus — which is precisely why X labels the focus-out `NotifyGrab` instead of sending a transfer — so no timer, delay or deadline exists anywhere on the path. `XGetInputFocus` was chosen over any window-taking Xlib call for a second reason: it takes no window argument and therefore cannot raise `BadWindow`, the one X error that would reach the grab worker's cross-isolate `Pointer.fromFunction` and be fatal to the VM (T-01-61). **The three edits, with evidence.** (1) *`_onBlur`'s fourth question* — `window_manager_panel_visibility.dart:1048-1050`, asked after DW-33's guard at `:1042` and before the outstanding-request latch at `:1053`; `_focused` is deliberately left `true` on the suppressed path (`:1049`, reasoning at `:973-980`), which removes the arm's dependency on a `FocusIn(NotifyUngrab)` coming back to re-arm it. Its reader is `_keyboardStillHere()` at `:1068-1080`, AD-15-backstopped: every uncertainty — no display, an unanswerable server, a focus reported `None` or `PointerRoot`, nothing witnessed yet, or a throw — answers `false`, so an unproven path behaves exactly as the adapter did before the seam existed. (2) *the same question at `_releaseDeferredBlur`* — `:1209` — so a blur latched during one of our own round trips (the DW-32 route) is reconsidered against the server rather than trusted from the moment it arrived. (3) *the wiring* — the display server is read exactly once, at `daemon_startup.dart:123`; `_focusWitnessFor` at `:333` gives X11 the real witness and every other host `AbsentKeyboardFocusWitness`, whose `focusUnmoved => false` is unconditional (`absent_keyboard_focus_witness.dart:27`); it is exposed at `daemon_startup.dart:190` and taken by `main.dart:136`; the panel adapter's parameter is required and defaultless at `window_manager_panel_visibility.dart:235`, and `recordFocusGained()` is called only from the `focus` arm under the existing visibility guard at `:896-897`. No verbatim-frozen declaration moved: `panel_visibility.dart`, `global_hotkey.dart` and `x11_key_grab_registrar.dart` all hash-compare equal to their blobs at the pre-fix anchor `7d42485`, so this closure owes no spine ratification. **The runtime evidence is cited, not asserted here.** It is `test/platform/panel-toggle-observation.md`, the committed record of a run on `Xvfb :99` under `openbox 3.6.1` against the release bundle on 2026-09-10, run twice with identical verdicts: `hide` moved `FLICKER` -> `HIDE` and `alternate` moved `SHOW,FLICKER,FLICKER,FLICKER` -> `SHOW,HIDE,SHOW,HIDE` against plan 01-11's recorded baseline, while `focus-steal` and `desktop-click` — CAP-14's two genuine dismissals — stayed `HIDE`. What this closure does **not** carry is filed separately and on purpose: DW-124 for the three residuals the fix accepts rather than closes, DW-125 for the one behaviour it deliberately changes, and DW-123 for what the run says about the premise three earlier closures rest on.

### DW-123: the premise DW-9, DW-26 and DW-44 were closed under is false — this container can observe the panel, and not observing it cost a major defect

origin: `.planning/phases/01-hotkey-truth/01-14-PLAN.md`, filed while writing DW-122's closure, 2026-09-10
location: this file (DW-9, DW-26, DW-44 — named, not edited), test/platform/{panel_visibility,correction_panel,x11_hotkey,settings_screen,tray,wayland_hotkey,desktop_entries}_live_test.dart, test/platform/runtime-observation-checklist.md, test/platform/desktop-session-checklist.md, test/platform/panel-toggle-observation.md
severity: high
reason: DW-9, DW-26 and DW-44 were all closed `done 2026-08-14` by pointing at one manual checklist, and all three closures rest on a single shared premise, stated in their own words: "this container has no compositor, no xdg-desktop-portal, no session bus and no reachable X display" (DW-9's `decision:`); "still unobserved and still unobservable here" (DW-26's); "`keybinder-3.0` grabs on an X display, this container reaches none" (DW-44's). Seven bodyless rows under `test/platform/` carry the same premise in prose, and one of them argues it a step further — that standing up `Xvfb` "would produce a result about Xvfb" rather than about the product. **That premise is false, and it was false in a way that cost something.** A live X session was stood up in this container on 2026-09-04 and measured through 2026-09-10: `Xvfb :99 -screen 0 1440x900x24 -nolisten tcp`, plus `openbox 3.6.1` as a real window manager, plus the built release bundle, produced a real X11 grab that caught a real `xdotool` (XTEST) keypress, a real mapped and focused panel, a real focus-loss hide on two independent gestures — and, the part that is not a technicality, **a major CAP-14 defect that a whole phase of green rows had not found**: G-01-13, the toggle that never hid a visible panel, now closed as DW-122. Standing up Xvfb did not produce a result about Xvfb. It produced the defect. The run is recorded at `test/platform/panel-toggle-observation.md` and its harness is `tool/uat/panel_toggle_probe.sh`. **The three closed entries are NOT reopened by this entry, and that is a deliberate call rather than an oversight.** This ledger is append-only: closing an entry flips its `status:` and adds a `resolution:` beneath it, and rewriting a closed entry's status line is precisely the violation DW-13 and DW-108 already record — reopening is not the closing motion the rule describes. So the refutation is filed here, naming all three, and their own text stands unedited and unrewritten. Where their text and this entry disagree, both survive and this one is the later finding. **This is a call a human may reverse.** Nothing here forecloses a decision that DW-9, DW-26 and DW-44 should be reopened and re-triaged as open work; if that is decided, this entry is the record of why it was not done unilaterally by the plan that found the contradiction. **What keeps this entry open:** the rest of the claims those two checklists carry have not been re-triaged against a live session, and some of them now plainly can be — every step that needs only a mapped toplevel, `xev` and `xdotool`. Plan 01-14's skip-text audit retires only the sentences a run has actually falsified; it does not re-triage the checklists themselves, and that re-triage is the work this entry owes.
status: open

### DW-124: what the G-01-13 fix accepts rather than closes — a contrivable focus id, an unbounded X round trip on the platform thread, and a host that cannot attribute its own focus

origin: `.planning/phases/01-hotkey-truth/01-12-PLAN.md` threat register (T-01-59, T-01-60), filed by plan 01-14 at that plan's request, 2026-09-10; `.planning/WINDOWS.md` 25 and 26
location: lib/src/infrastructure/panel/x11_keyboard_focus_witness.dart, lib/src/infrastructure/panel/absent_keyboard_focus_witness.dart, lib/src/infrastructure/panel/window_manager_panel_visibility.dart
severity: medium
reason: DW-122 closes G-01-13 on X11 and leaves three things standing. They are accepted, not fixed, and each is named here with its threat id so a later reader does not mistake an accepted risk for an overlooked one. **(1) A contrivable focus id (T-01-59, accepted).** The witness compares the focus window id X reports now against the one it recorded when the panel last gained focus, so another client on the same display could contrive for its own focus window to carry the recorded id and thereby suppress a *genuine* dismissal — the panel would stay up when the user meant it to go away. Accepted because X11 offers no inter-client isolation at all: any client that can do this can already read the panel's pixels and synthesize keystrokes into it, so the witness grants an attacker on that display no capability it lacks. **(2) A synchronous X round trip on the platform thread, unbounded (T-01-60, accepted on an argument and not on a measurement — that distinction is the point of filing it).** `XGetInputFocus` is called synchronously from the blur path with no timeout, deliberately, because bounding it needs a timer and DW-122's whole fix is that no timer exists anywhere on this path. The argument for accepting it is that GDK already makes synchronous round trips to the same server on the same thread for every frame, so a server that has stopped answering has stopped the daemon before this call is reached. **Nobody wedged an X server to check that.** It is reasoning, it is stated as reasoning in the witness's own doc, and a measurement would close this half of the entry. **(3) A host whose display server reports a focus that cannot be attributed to a window.** Where the server answers `None` or `PointerRoot`, or nothing has been witnessed yet, the witness answers "cannot tell" — `focusUnmoved` is `false` — the adapter dismisses exactly as it did before the seam existed, and **G-01-13 therefore persists on such a host**. That is the fail-safe direction chosen on purpose (suppressing a dismissal that should have fired leaves the user a panel that will not go away; a needless dismissal costs only what CAP-14 already cost), but it means the fix is conditional on the server being able to attribute its own focus, and no host has been found where it cannot. **The Wayland arm belongs here too.** It is wired to `AbsentKeyboardFocusWitness`, a null object that never suppresses (`absent_keyboard_focus_witness.dart:27`), so the claim that Wayland's dismissal behaviour is byte-for-byte what it was before `dc6079b` is a reading of the composition root — an argument — and not an observation of a running compositor. This container has no `xdg-desktop-portal`, no GlobalShortcuts backend and no session bus, so nobody has watched a panel on Wayland do any of this. All four items close on a real desktop session, not here.
status: open

### DW-125: a foreign client's global shortcut no longer dismisses the panel — a measured, deliberate behaviour change that no human has ratified

origin: measured by plan 01-12 on the `foreign-grab` probe route and re-measured by plan 01-13, filed by plan 01-14, 2026-09-10; `.planning/WINDOWS.md` 24
location: lib/src/infrastructure/panel/window_manager_panel_visibility.dart, tool/uat/panel_toggle_probe.sh (`foreign-grab`), test/platform/panel-toggle-observation.md section 3
severity: medium
reason: **Needs a human's judgment, and is filed in the same register as plan 01-09's gate answer** — decided in flight by an unattended run, recorded as such, and not to be read later as ratified because it is written down. The change, measured twice with identical results: a foreign client's global shortcut fired while our panel is up **no longer dismisses the panel**. Before DW-122's fix it did — that route was the diagnosis's smoking gun. The `foreign-grab` probe route read `UNMAP=1 MAP=0 FOCUS_BEFORE=4194308 FOCUS_AFTER=2097439 VERDICT=HIDE` pre-fix and reads `UNMAP=0 MAP=0 FIRST=none FOCUS_BEFORE=4194308 FOCUS_AFTER=4194308 VERDICT=NOTHING` post-fix: the panel stayed up and the focus never left it. This is not a defect in the discriminator and it cannot be narrowed by one. A foreign passive grab moves no focus, exactly as ours does not, so the two are **indistinguishable to a question about the focus** — that indistinguishability is the mechanism DW-122 chose deliberately, and the price of choosing it. The argument for the new behaviour is that a foreign grab moves no focus and the panel gets the keyboard straight back, so nothing the user did dismissed the panel and not dismissing it is arguably the more correct answer. **But that is an argument, and nobody has ratified it.** It is a visible product behaviour, not an implementation detail: on a desktop where the user has bound another application to a system-wide shortcut, pressing it now leaves our panel sitting on top of whatever that shortcut summoned. A human should decide whether that is the wanted behaviour. What a decision the other way would cost, stated so it is not re-derived: keying on whose grab it was rather than on the focus is the direction plan 01-12 rejected as unfixably racy (see DW-122), so restoring the old dismissal is not a small edit — it is a different fix.
status: done 2026-09-11
decision: 2026-09-11 Ratified as the wanted behaviour — human answer, given in response to this question being surfaced by the `/gsd-execute-phase 01 --gaps-only` orchestrator after plan 01-14 filed the entry. The panel **stays up** when a foreign client's global shortcut fires.
