The reasoning accepted: nothing the user did moved the keyboard off the panel — the focus never left it, measured `4194308 -> 4194308` — so the panel still holds the keyboard and still holds the user's in-progress edit, and dismissing it would discard text the user never aimed at. That reads directly onto the project's core value, that the daemon never loses the user's text. The cost accepted with it, stated so it is not re-litigated: on a desktop where the user has bound another application to a system-wide shortcut, pressing it leaves our panel sitting over whatever that shortcut summoned. The alternative was declined on its price rather than on its merits — restoring the old dismissal means keying on whose grab it was instead of on the focus, which is the direction plan 01-12 rejected as unfixably racy, so it is a different fix and not an edit.
resolution: ratified, no code change. The behaviour `dc6079b` introduced and plans 01-12 and 01-13 each measured on the `foreign-grab` route (`UNMAP=0 MAP=0 FIRST=none VERDICT=NOTHING`, recorded at `test/platform/panel-toggle-observation.md` section 3) is the behaviour the product wants; the discriminator at `window_manager_panel_visibility.dart:1048-1050` stands as written. What this closure does NOT settle stays open elsewhere and is not folded in here: DW-124 still carries the three residual risks the fix accepts rather than closes, including the unbounded synchronous `XGetInputFocus` accepted on an argument and not a measurement. This entry's own title still reads "that no human has ratified" because existing field text is never rewritten to agree with a later finding (DW-13, DW-108); the ratification is this decision and this resolution, not an edit to the heading.

### DW-126: two statements in the X11 grab worker that read as error reporting are no-ops, and the helper they call is dead — filed rather than fixed, by decision (WR-05)

origin: raised as WR-05 by `.planning/phases/01-hotkey-truth/01-REVIEW.md` (2026-09-11) and carried into `01-VERIFICATION.md` § Anti-Patterns Found; routed to this ledger rather than to a fix by the developer's ratified scope decision for the G-01-15/G-01-16 gap-closure run, and filed by plan `.planning/phases/01-hotkey-truth/01-20-PLAN.md`, 2026-09-11
location: lib/src/infrastructure/hotkey/x11_key_grab_registrar.dart (`:967`, `:1075`, `:531-533`, and the doc link at `:498`), .planning/phases/01-hotkey-truth/01-REVIEW.md § WR-05
severity: low
reason: **What is wrong.** Both sites are a bare expression statement — `_errorContext(error);` — whose returned `Map` nobody consumes: the worker isolate has no `Logger` and sends nothing up, so the map is built and dropped on the same line. The comments directly above them claim the opposite. At `:965-966`: *"Reduced through `_errorContext`, so nothing but the type is retained. The sentence the caller sees is authored here, not taken from the error."* At `:1072-1074`: *"Nowhere to report this from inside the worker, and a teardown step's failure must not stop the steps after it. Reduced so nothing but the type could ever be retained."* So a `DynamicLibrary.open` failure and the whole `_X11Bindings` construction failure (the `_openBindings` catch at `:964-968`) and every guarded teardown failure inside the worker (`:1071-1076`) are silently swallowed **under a comment asserting they were handled**. `_errorContext` is declared at `:531-533` and has no caller other than those two statements, so the helper is dead as well. CLAUDE.md forbids dead code outright and requires a comment to explain a real why; this is one construct breaking both at once, and the comment is the worse half — a swallowed error is a gap, a comment claiming it was handled is a false record. **The anchors, re-derived at this HEAD rather than taken from the review** (01-VERIFICATION.md records WR-05 as "not independently re-derived line by line", so this filing does that work once so the later cluster need not): `lib/src/infrastructure/hotkey/x11_key_grab_registrar.dart:967`, `:1075`, `:531-533`. **One anchor 01-REVIEW.md does not carry and a fix will trip over:** the `///` doc at `:498` reads "form — see [_errorContext]", a square-bracket doc link, so deleting the helper without re-wording that line leaves a dangling reference — the same analyzer-visible class of link plan 01-19 measured for `hotkey_key_catalogue.dart`'s `xdg_shortcut_trigger.dart` import, where the doc link alone was what kept the import alive. **01-REVIEW.md's proposed fix, carried here in substance so the later cluster inherits the work rather than the search:** drop both statements and the helper, and replace the comment with the true reason — there is nowhere to report this from inside the worker (no `Logger` there, no diagnostic tag on the owner port), the caller learns of the failure through the refusal reply the arm already sends, and the code should say that rather than perform a reduction it then drops, which reads as reporting and is not. The review's replacement arm, quoted: "`} on Object { // Nowhere to report this from inside the worker: there is no Logger here and // no diagnostic tag on the owner port. The caller learns of it through the // refusal reply below. Deliberately not reduced-and-dropped, which reads as // reporting and is not. return null; }`". **Why the severity is low rather than inflated.** 01-VERIFICATION.md:341 adjudicates it "Cosmetic relative to the goal", and that stands: no user-visible behaviour changes either way, the caller already learns of both failures through the modelled refusal, and the privacy property the comments were protecting (a vendor exception's payload never reaching a log) is satisfied trivially, because nothing is logged at all. What is being filed is a false comment and two dead constructs, not a lost error path. **Adjacency, stated so the later cluster does not treat this as the same work:** the file it lives in is also where CR-01, CR-03, WR-01 and WR-02 live, and all four of those are Phase 5's stated goal. This is not one of them and does not close with them; it is a text-and-dead-code defect that happens to share a file.
status: open
decision: 2026-09-11 Filed rather than fixed — the developer's ratified scope answer for this gap-closure run, recorded in the plans that run produced (`01-16-PLAN.md:269-270` "WR-05 is ledgered…"; `01-18-PLAN.md:9` "WR-05 is ledger-only by the user's ratified decision and is filed by plan 01-20"; `01-19-PLAN.md:76`, which forbids that plan from touching `x11_key_grab_registrar.dart` on the same grounds), committed as `375a6d5` and `8dab2e7`. **This is a decision and not an omission**, and the distinction is the reason this line exists: a later reader who finds two no-op statements under a comment claiming otherwise must not "fix" something a human already decided not to fix in this run, nor read the deferral as something that was merely missed. What the ledger cites for the ratification is those plan texts, not a transcript — the answer is recorded in the artefacts the planning run produced rather than quoted verbatim from the exchange, and this entry says so rather than implying a quotation it does not hold. Plan 01-20 was correspondingly forbidden from editing `x11_key_grab_registrar.dart`; `git diff --numstat -- lib/ test/` was empty at this entry's commit, which is the mechanical form of that decision being honoured.

### DW-127: the G-01-15 fix makes the stranded X11 passive grab visible without releasing it — a dead worker's connection still holds the combination for the life of the process

origin: `.planning/phases/01-hotkey-truth/01-16-PLAN.md` threat register (T-01-76), restated as an accepted residual by `01-16-SUMMARY.md` and re-stated with a directly observed symptom by `01-17-SUMMARY.md` and `test/platform/worker-gone-observation.md`; filed by plan `.planning/phases/01-hotkey-truth/01-20-PLAN.md` at both plans' request, 2026-09-11
location: lib/src/infrastructure/hotkey/x11_key_grab_registrar.dart (the worker's `Display` raw pointer, `_X11Worker.handle`, `_failPending` at `:323-331`), lib/src/infrastructure/hotkey/x11_global_hotkey.dart (the seam-refused arm plan 01-16 changed), test/platform/worker-gone-observation.md, tool/uat/worker_gone_probe.sh
severity: medium
reason: **The mechanism.** Killing the X11 key-grab worker isolate does not close the X socket it opened: the `Display` is a raw pointer held by that isolate with no finalizer, and a fatal throw on the worker's grab path runs no teardown over it. The X server therefore still holds this client's passive grab on the combination and keeps swallowing it system-wide, while nobody reads that connection's event queue — so the combination is taken from every other application and opens nothing. It survives until the process exits, at which point the server drops that client's passive grabs. **What the G-01-15 closure did and did not do about it, stated exactly.** It makes the state **visible**: `x11_global_hotkey.dart`'s seam-refused arm now reads the refusal's code, clears `_effective` and returns `HotkeyUnavailable(noBackend)` for a `workerGone`/`noBackend` refusal (plan 01-16, `b421a9c`), so the user is told the shortcut is not in effect instead of being told it is. It does **not release** the grab, because the connection that holds it is unreachable from the surviving isolate — which is the whole reason the fix is a report rather than a repair, and is why this residual is accepted rather than closed. **Owner: Phase 5**, whose stated goal is one bounded way to stop with no resident windowless process after an abort, no unbounded await before `exit`, and no teardown running underneath a startup still in progress. The linkage Phase 5 inherits: **CR-01 / WR-01** (a dead worker is never detected or replaced, and `handle` has no `on Object` guard), **CR-03** (the readiness handshake has no terminal arm), **WR-02** (`dispose()` racing an in-flight spawn leaks the worker). Releasing a stranded grab needs the worker-lifecycle replacement all four describe; it is not an edit to the adapter that reports it. **What was measured, and what is only reasoned — the halves are not the same and are not written in the same voice.** *Measured:* `test/platform/worker-gone-observation.md`, four runs on `Xvfb :99` under `openbox 3.6.1` against release bundles built at named revisions, recording `REV=4247446 REFUSAL_CODE=workerGone LOG=abandoned VERDICT=REPORTED_IN_EFFECT` before the fix and `REV=49c5f9f REFUSAL_CODE=workerGone LOG=refused VERDICT=REPORTED_UNAVAILABLE` after it. And one directly observed symptom of the strand itself, which until plan 01-17 was argued only from the source: the post-fix daemon's own shutdown log carries `"disposing the hotkey adapter did not finish within 5000 ms; it was abandoned…"` followed by `"releasing the global hotkey grab did not finish within 5000 ms; it was abandoned…"`. The daemon cannot release what the dead isolate holds, and it says so in its own journal. *Reasoned, not observed:* that the stranded grab then keeps swallowing the combination for every other application. **Nobody pressed the combination in a second application against a daemon whose worker was dead and watched it go nowhere.** The two abandonment lines prove the release path times out; they do not prove what the X server does with the grab in the meantime. That step is a reading of the code plus the absence of a finalizer — DW-124's register, "nobody wedged an X server to check that", and the same register applies here. **The observation reached this arm through a named fault injection, and that is stated rather than elided.** One hunk in one file, quoted verbatim in the record: a `throw StateError('worker_gone_probe.sh injected worker death for keysymName=$keysymName')` in `_X11Worker._grab` guarded by `keysymName == 'F9'`. Everything downstream of the isolate's death is the shipped chain with no fake on it. **A third residual of the same mechanism, filed here because it is the reason the injection was necessary: the `workerGone` arm is reachable in production but not triggerable from outside the process.** `_X11Worker.handle` carries no `on Object` guard and `Isolate.spawn` defaults to `errorsAreFatal: true`, so a Dart throw on the grab path really is fatal to the worker at exactly the moment a reply is pending (`_ask` registers `_pending[id]` before it sends, so there is an in-flight completer for `_failPending` to complete). But every throw the worker can actually take today is already guarded into a modelled refusal — `_openBindings` catches the `DynamicLibrary.open` and the whole `_X11Bindings` construction, `_openDisplay` reads a null pointer as a value. So the arm is genuinely reachable and no external input reaches it. The missing guard is **WR-01, Phase 5**; the injection route is `DISPLAY=:99 ./tool/uat/worker_gone_probe.sh --at <sha> --label <name>`, re-runnable by that phase against the very arm it is about to change.
status: open

### DW-128: after the G-01-15 fix a dead worker renders a cause line that overstates — the desktop does provide global shortcuts, this daemon's worker died

origin: `.planning/phases/01-hotkey-truth/01-16-PLAN.md` threat register (T-01-78), read off the live post-fix settings screen by plan 01-17 and recorded in `test/platform/worker-gone-observation.md`; filed by plan `.planning/phases/01-hotkey-truth/01-20-PLAN.md`, 2026-09-11
location: lib/src/ui/settings/hotkey_status_view.dart (`_unavailableLines` at `:164-175`, `_causeLine`'s `noBackend` sentence at `:193`, and the method's own deferral doc at `:159-163`), lib/src/infrastructure/hotkey/x11_key_grab_registrar.dart:281-284 (the registrar's own sentence), test/platform/evidence/worker-gone-rebind-sentence-pair.png
severity: low
reason: **What the screen says now.** With the seam-refused arm answering `HotkeyUnavailableCause.noBackend` for a `workerGone`, the settings screen renders, in this order: *"This desktop provides no global shortcuts, so no combination can be registered here."*, then *"the X11 hotkey worker stopped before answering, so no global shortcut is registered — the tray menu still opens the panel"*, then the standing ownership line. **The second line is accurate. The first overstates**: the desktop *does* provide global shortcuts on this host — the daemon held one moments earlier — and what failed is this daemon's own worker. **What is not wrong here, and deserves saying so rather than being buried under the finding** (the register 01-REVIEW.md's WR-09 uses for a restated pre-existing item): the registrar's sentence is carried through **verbatim** rather than replaced by the view's own paraphrase, which is exactly the behaviour plan 01-04 introduced and UAT test 1 read live; the tray is named exactly once across the whole pair (AD-12), only in that second line, because `_causeLine`'s three sentences deliberately never mention it; and **no "In effect:" line appears at all**, which is the G-01-15 closure itself showing on the screen. The honest comparison, stated so this entry is not read as a regression report: a pair that overstates in its first line and is accurate in its second has replaced a single line that was simply false. That is better, and it is not the same as being right. **Why this belongs to Phase 2 and not to this run.** `_unavailableLines` is `hotkey_status_view`'s text, and **SETTINGS-09 (FLAT-16)** owns whether that method appends a line of its own at all — the method's own doc says so in code at `:159-163` ("SETTINGS-09 (FLAT-16) in Phase 2 owns the question of whether this method should append a line of its own at all… Nothing here pre-empts its answer"). 01-VERIFICATION.md's `deferred:` block already assigns **WR-06** to **Phase 2** on precisely that reasoning, and this entry assigns the same owner rather than inventing a different one. **Adjacency, not identity:** WR-06 is the contradiction between the `revoked` cause line and the third, standing ownership line. This is the `noBackend` cause line, the first. Same method, same owner, same requirement, different sentence — and a Phase 2 pass that reshapes `_unavailableLines` should settle both together rather than close one and leave the other.
status: open

### DW-129: plan 01-03's must-have truth stated unconditionally what is only true while the backend is still there — the amendment, filed rather than applied (G-01-15)

origin: `.planning/phases/01-hotkey-truth/01-VERIFICATION.md` `gaps[0].missing` (2026-09-11), whose third missing item reads "Amend plan 01-03's truth (or file the amendment in the ledger) so the abandoned-rebind rule reads as conditional on the backend still being present, since the unconditional wording is what licensed the branch"; filed by plan `.planning/phases/01-hotkey-truth/01-20-PLAN.md` on the ledger branch that item offers, 2026-09-11
location: .planning/phases/01-hotkey-truth/01-03-PLAN.md `must_haves.truths`, second entry (**named, not edited**); .planning/phases/01-hotkey-truth/01-16-PLAN.md `must_haves.truths`, third entry (the replacement); lib/src/infrastructure/hotkey/x11_global_hotkey.dart (the arm the wording licensed); test/infrastructure/hotkey/x11_global_hotkey_test.dart (the row that pins the replacement)
severity: medium
reason: **The original truth, quoted from `01-03-PLAN.md` `must_haves.truths`:** "`HotkeyUnavailable` is returned from the X11 bind path only when nothing is held — when a previous combination is still in effect, a refused rebind returns `HotkeyBound` naming that previous combination and logs the abandonment." **Why it is wrong.** It states unconditionally what is only true while the backend is still there. A refused rebind does leave the previous combination in effect, because the registrar acquires before it releases — that is a real strength and it is plan 01-03's actual deliverable — but the reasoning holds only for a refusal the backend **answered**. A `noBackend` or `workerGone` refusal says the opposite: the connection that held the previous grab is gone, so the previous combination is either dropped by the X server or held-but-deaf, with nothing reading that connection's event queue. The truth's unconditional wording licensed the adapter to report that case as `HotkeyBound(previous)` anyway, which is exactly the defect 01-REVIEW.md raised as CR-02 and 01-VERIFICATION.md adjudicated as `gaps[0]` against the phase goal's first clause. **The amended truth, quoted from `01-16-PLAN.md` `must_haves.truths`:** "AMENDED 01-03 TRUTH (the unconditional wording is what licensed the defect, and this is its replacement): `HotkeyUnavailable` is returned from the X11 bind path whenever nothing is held OR the backend that held it is gone; a refused rebind returns `HotkeyBound` naming the previous combination only while that combination is still grabbed by a live backend." **This was a PLANNING gap and not an executor failure, and that clause is the entire point of this entry.** 01-03's executor implemented the truth exactly as written. 01-VERIFICATION.md verifies it as written and says so twice: in `gaps[0].reason` — "plan 01-03's must-have truth … states unconditionally what is only true while the backend is still there. The executor implemented the truth as written. The truth was wrong." — and at truth row 8 of the Observable Truths table, which marks 01-03 "✓ VERIFIED **(as written — and the wording is the licence for truth 0's defect)**" and closes "Executor did what was planned; see Gaps for the amendment this needs." The acquire-then-release ordering the truth asked for is intact in the code to this day. The defect was in the sentence, not in the code that satisfied it, and a reader who finds this later must not conclude that an executor cut a corner. **Why `01-03-PLAN.md` itself was not edited.** Plans 01-01 … 01-14 are executed and their SUMMARYs are final; amending a must-have truth in place would rewrite the record of what was actually planned and actually verified, leaving no trace that the phase ever shipped against the wrong sentence. So the amendment is filed and both texts survive: where they disagree, this entry is the later finding. That is the same motion DW-123 made for DW-9, DW-26 and DW-44 — naming what it refuted rather than rewriting it — and the motion this file's own header block made in its "Correction to the earlier parking" prose before either. The mechanical form of the fence was measured at this entry's commit: `git diff --numstat` over every `01-01-*` … `01-14-*` file in `.planning/phases/01-hotkey-truth/` is empty.
status: done 2026-09-11
resolution: Amended, filed, and pinned by a test rather than merely asserted. The corrected truth is live as the third entry of `01-16-PLAN.md`'s `must_haves.truths` and was satisfied by plan 01-16's adapter change (`b421a9c`): the seam-refused arm hoists `final cause = _causeOf(refusal);` and widens its early return to `stillInEffect == null || cause == HotkeyUnavailableCause.noBackend`, so a `workerGone`/`noBackend` refusal clears `_effective` and returns `HotkeyUnavailable(cause, message)` while `keyRefused`, `badRequest` and a rejection that is not a `HotkeyRegistrarRefusal` at all still return `HotkeyBound` naming the previous combination — 01-03's strength kept, not spent. The amendment is not merely asserted because it has a fail-first row behind it: plan 01-15 authored the expectation and recorded it RED at `fd3cba7`, verbatim `Expected: HotkeyUnavailable` / `Actual: HotkeyBound`, at a commit `git log` can prove predates the fix, and `git diff --numstat 4247446..HEAD` over that test file is `190 0` — zero deletions, so the row that went green is byte-identical to the row recorded red. It was then observed live end to end by plan 01-17 at two named revisions (`REV=4247446 LOG=abandoned VERDICT=REPORTED_IN_EFFECT`, `REV=49c5f9f LOG=refused VERDICT=REPORTED_UNAVAILABLE`, `test/platform/worker-gone-observation.md`). What this closure does NOT settle is filed separately and on purpose: DW-127 for the stranded passive grab the fix reports without releasing, and DW-128 for the cause line that now overstates for a dead worker.

### DW-130: WR-08's fork, decided — the always-false portal-trigger refusal was deleted rather than kept behind a new assertion

origin: raised as WR-08 by `.planning/phases/01-hotkey-truth/01-REVIEW.md` (2026-09-11), which left the fork explicitly open ("either delete the field, the enum value and the branch … or — if the field is meant as a guard against future divergence — keep it and add the assertion that makes it honest"); decided and executed by plan 01-19 (`de50896`); filed by plan `.planning/phases/01-hotkey-truth/01-20-PLAN.md`, 2026-09-11
location: lib/src/domain/hotkey/registrable_keys.dart, lib/src/application/hotkey_capture.dart, lib/src/ui/settings/hotkey_capture_field.dart, lib/src/infrastructure/hotkey/hotkey_key_catalogue.dart, test/infrastructure/hotkey/hotkey_key_catalogue_test.dart, test/infrastructure/hotkey/xdg_shortcut_trigger_test.dart, test/architecture/hotkey_confinement_test.dart
severity: low
reason: **The option taken: (a), delete the field, the enum value and the branch.** `RegistrableKey.hasPortalTrigger`, `HotkeyCaptureRefusal.noPortalTrigger`, the `if (!key.hasPortalTrigger)` branch and its user-facing sentence are gone; `HotkeyCaptureRefusal` now carries exactly four values and the validator's refusal chain keeps its previous order minus the removed link. **The reason is a measurement, not a preference, and it was taken BEFORE anything was removed.** Running the real catalogue prints `total=63 withoutPortalTrigger=0 missing=[]` — the flag was `XdgShortcutTrigger.keysymNameFor(label) != null` and the two vocabularies are identical by construction (26 letters, 10 digits, F1–F12, and the same 15 named keys in `HotkeyKeyCatalogue._namedKeys` and `XdgShortcutTrigger._namedKeysymNames`), so the predicate was true for all 63 keys and the branch was unreachable by any input a user can produce. Three existing build-time assertions were run green first and shown to dominate it: both directions of the label/keysym parity plus the same-order claim in `xdg_shortcut_trigger_test.dart`'s "parity with the X11 catalogue (AD-9)" group, and the per-key flag assertion in `hotkey_key_catalogue_test.dart`. Had any been absent or red the deletion would have been halted; the safety was measured before the fact rather than claimed after. **The second half of the reason, which is what actually settles the fork:** the two failure modes are not equivalent. A divergence caught by a test stops the build in front of the developer who caused it; a divergence caught by the deleted branch would have reached a **user**, as a sentence about a key they had just pressed. A guard strictly dominated by existing build-time assertions is not a safety net. This is also the standard this phase had already set for itself and was otherwise contradicting: plan 01-07's own `resolution:` in this file records removing `labelsThatBindTheWrongKey` and `bindsTheWrongKey` **outright rather than leaving them as an empty set**, because "an always-false predicate leaves branches that can never run, which AGENTS.md §1 rules out". **The option NOT taken, written down so a reversal is a knowing decision rather than a rediscovery:** (b) keep the field and the branch as a guard against future divergence, adding a catalogue row asserting the vocabularies agree so the branch is documented as a guard rather than as a reachable refusal. Reversing means re-adding the field and its constructor parameter, re-authoring the refusal sentence, and re-deciding its place in the ordered refusal chain — git restores the code, but the placement argument is the part that would otherwise be re-derived. Nothing outside this repository consumes `RegistrableKey`; no stored value, on-disk format or published contract changed, so the reversal is not one-way. **Where the guard lives now** — the invariant is real and is still checked, just at build time and in three places rather than at capture time in one: the replacement collected-list assertion in `test/infrastructure/hotkey/hotkey_key_catalogue_test.dart`, which runs against `HotkeyKeyCatalogue.registrableKeys()` (the vocabulary the composition root actually injects, never a hand-built `RegistrableKey`) and names *which* labels diverged rather than only the first; and the two parity rows in `test/infrastructure/hotkey/xdg_shortcut_trigger_test.dart` carrying the reverse direction and the same-order claim. The deleted guard's rationale was moved into that replacement row's `reason:` string rather than dropped, so it is read by whoever next sees the assertion fail. **Why no human gate was needed, stated so a later reader does not assume one was skipped:** `lib/src/domain/hotkey/registrable_keys.dart` appears in **no** verbatim-fixed `ARCHITECTURE-SPINE.md` block — it is not in that document at all — and it was created by plan 01-07 in this same phase. The frozen declarations (`global_hotkey.dart`, `hotkey_binding.dart`, `hotkey_bind_outcome.dart`, `panel_visibility.dart`) were held to the same mechanical standard plan 01-12 proved for its own two files, and `git diff --numstat` over all four across 01-19's commits is empty.
status: done 2026-09-11
resolution: Closed by plan 01-19, Task 1 (`de50896`). Six files, no net change in test-row count — the per-key flag loop was **replaced** rather than deleted. Sweep proved complete by grep rather than by reading: `grep -rn 'hasPortalTrigger\|noPortalTrigger' lib/ test/` returns 0 and `grep -rniE 'five (subjects|things)|the fifth thing' lib/ test/` returns 0, so no field survives with no reader and no doc line still counts five refusal subjects where there are four. One adjacent question was settled by experiment rather than by inference and is recorded here because a later reader will ask it: the `xdg_shortcut_trigger.dart` import in `hotkey_key_catalogue.dart` **stays** — rewriting the `[XdgShortcutTrigger]` doc link at `:186` to plain backticks makes `dart analyze` report `unused_import` at exit 2, so the square-bracket doc link is the sole thing keeping that import alive, and the two backtick mentions at `:58` and `:193` do not hold it. Gates at the closure: `dart analyze --fatal-infos` clean, 982 dart passed / 2 skipped, 165 flutter passed / 7 skipped.

### DW-131: the merge gate failed about two runs in three from nondeterminism inside one suite — the half `concurrency: 1` cannot reach, and the half nothing recorded

origin: raised as `gaps[3]` by `.planning/phases/01-hotkey-truth/01-VERIFICATION.md` (2026-09-11), whose third `missing:` bullet reads "A ledger entry. `dart_test.yaml`'s comment cites DW-15 for the concurrency half; the residual intra-file half is unfiled"; diagnosed, changed and measured by plan `.planning/phases/01-hotkey-truth/01-23-PLAN.md` (2026-09-14); filed by plan `.planning/phases/01-hotkey-truth/01-24-PLAN.md`, 2026-09-14
location: dart_test.yaml:11-26 (the 15-line comment, which cites DW-15) and dart_test.yaml:27 (`concurrency: 1`), test/infrastructure/hotkey/wayland_portal_global_hotkey_test.dart (`_settle()` at `:2293` with its replacement doc at `:2285-2292`, `_bindAndSettle` at `:2302`, and the rows observed failing at `:387` "A5 CAP-12…", `:1363` "A16 CAP-1…", `:1559` "A19d AD-11…", `:1686` "C2 AD-11, AD-12…"), test/support/fake_global_shortcuts_portal.dart (`_emitSettled` at `:351` and the five `emit*` methods that route through it at `:387`, `:412`, `:434`, `:457`, `:472`), test/infrastructure/system/single_instance_lock_test.dart:60, test/infrastructure/system/daemon_startup_test.dart:304, test/platform/merge-gate-flakiness-observation.md
severity: high
reason: **Why high rather than medium, since both readings are available and the lower one is tempting.** This falsifies no product behaviour — it is a test-harness defect, and 01-23 has now measured it and, on the half it reached, apparently closed it. What decides the severity against that is reach: "the suite is green" is the sole proof offered for several of this phase's verified truths, so a gate that fails about two runs in three does not merely inconvenience a developer, it makes the phase's evidence layer unsound, and every record that rested on one exit code has to be re-read as one draw. A defect in the instrument that measures everything else is more serious than a defect in any one thing it measured. The counter-argument is recorded here rather than suppressed so a later reader can disagree with the weighting without having to reconstruct it.
  **1. What was measured, and by whom.** `01-VERIFICATION.md` ran the exact scoped command (`dart test --exclude-tags=live test/application test/architecture test/domain test/infrastructure test/fakes_smoke_test.dart`) **seven times, serially, with nothing else running, and recorded 2 PASS / 5 FAIL**. Every failure was a *different* set of rows: run A — `wayland_portal_global_hotkey_test.dart` "A5 CAP-12: a rebind closes the old session…"; run B — the same file's "A16 CAP-1: a press reaches every listener exactly once", plus `single_instance_lock_test.dart` "AD-14: release frees the address…"; run C — "A5 CAP-12…" and "A19d AD-11: a ShortcutsChanged this build cannot read…"; run D — "C2 AD-11, AD-12: a shortcut the desktop no longer holds…"; run E — `daemon_startup_test.dart` "AD-14, CAP-1: the holder receives exactly one show request". At least five distinct rows in the portal suite, none reproducibly. **Run alone, that file passed 5/5**, which is why every single-file check had missed it. Then plan 01-23's own numbers, which are not restated here in this entry's voice but cited: the contended single-file oracle failed **5/5 before the change and 0/5 after**, and the post-change pass rate is **10 PASS / 0 FAIL over 10 serial runs** of the binding-free half (982 passed / 2 skipped each time) and **3 PASS / 0 FAIL over 3** of the Flutter-bound half (165 passed / 7 skipped) — every per-run row, the host conditions and the load average behind those ratios are in `test/platform/merge-gate-flakiness-observation.md` §3 and §4, which is the record this entry defers to rather than duplicates. That is the same motion DW-122 made for `test/platform/panel-toggle-observation.md` and DW-127 for `test/platform/worker-gone-observation.md`: the number lives in the observation record, the ledger cites it.
  **2. The mechanism, at the level the evidence actually supports.** `_settle()` in the portal suite was `pumpEventQueue(times: 50)`, and it is the barrier every observed-failing row waits on — 36 direct call sites in that one file plus 26 more through `_bindAndSettle`. `pumpEventQueue` yields a fixed number of event-loop **turns**, not an interval of time, and fifty turns of an otherwise empty queue consume no wall clock; what the rows are actually waiting for is a round trip over a real unix socket, through `package:dbus`'s in-process bus. **This was measured, not argued.** A row was temporarily instrumented to pump one turn at a time and count until the activation landed: **2, 2, 2, 3, 2, 2 turns on an idle machine; 255, 2, 2, 2, 2, 2 with one busy-loop subshell per core** — 255 against a budget of 50. It also explains the shape of every failure without exception: each is an `Expected: an object with length of <1> / Actual: []`, a row asserting on a list the signal never reached, never a wrong value. The verification's own hypothesis ("an un-awaited signal round trip rather than five independent bugs") is confirmed with one correction: the round trip is not un-awaited by mistake, it is waited for with a barrier that cannot express the wait. **Where this entry stops being measured and starts reading code:** that the *replacement* barrier is correct rests on two D-Bus ordering guarantees — a bus reads one connection in order, so a client whose `AddMatch` preceded its `Ping` has the match applied before the reply; a `DBusClient` dispatches into its signal streams in read order, so a client that answered the post-emit ping has already handed the signal on. Those are properties of the protocol and of `package:dbus`'s implementation as read, not as observed; what was observed is that 5/5 became 0/5 under contention, which is consistent with the reading and does not prove it. DW-124's register — "nobody wedged an X server to check that" — is the standard, and the honest statement here is that nobody read the bus's wire order to check this.
  **3. Why it went uncaught for so long, stated as a reasoning error and not as carelessness.** Every check of this suite was either a single-file run, where it passes 5/5, or a single full-suite run, where it fails about two times in three and the sample happened to be green. The prior verification recorded `974 passed / 2 skipped, exit 0` as a measurement and the brief for the re-verification quoted `982 passed / 2 skipped`; **each of those was one draw from a distribution nobody had sampled.** Nothing was skipped and no one cut a corner — an exit code was read as a property of the gate when it was an observation of one run, and the fix for that class of error is a ratio, which is why `gaps[3]`'s second `missing:` bullet demanded N≥10 rather than a rerun.
  **4. The relationship to DW-15, stated precisely and without touching DW-15.** `dart_test.yaml:11-26` cites DW-15 for a real hazard: the Dart VM allows exactly one abstract-namespace unix socket bind per *process*, `dart test` and `flutter test` run suites as isolates inside one process, and two suites that bind one concurrently see a bogus `alreadyRunning`. **That citation is correct and is not being corrected.** `concurrency: 1` at `:27` fixes it, across suites. What neither the comment nor DW-15 covers is nondeterminism **inside** one suite, which serialising suites cannot reach by construction — the portal suite fails while running alone in its slot, with no other suite anywhere near it. Same file, adjacent hazard, different mechanism, and this entry is the record of the second one. DW-15 is `status: done 2026-08-14` and stays exactly as written; nothing above asks it to change.
  **5. What this entry does NOT settle, named with frequency rather than rounded away.** (a) `test/infrastructure/system/single_instance_lock_test.dart:60` — "AD-14: release frees the address for the very lock that held it" — observed failing **once in the verification's seven full-suite runs (≈1/7)**. (b) `test/infrastructure/system/daemon_startup_test.dart:304` — "AD-14, CAP-1: the holder receives exactly one show request" — likewise **once in seven (≈1/7)**. Neither failed in any of 01-23's ten runs, and **that is not evidence that either is fixed.** Their mechanism is DW-15's one-bind-per-process hazard, which 01-23's work does not touch at all: the change is entirely inside the portal fake's emit path, and `git log 52cebe8..HEAD` over both AD-14 files is empty. And the arithmetic forbids the comfortable reading — at p = 1/7 per run, the chance of seeing zero failures in ten runs is about 0.21, so a clean ten is an unremarkable outcome for a defect that is exactly as present as it was. **Ten runs cannot distinguish "fixed" from "not sampled" at that rate.** Treating them as fixed because they did not appear would be the same error — a sample read as a measurement — that this entry exists to record, so they are carried forward as open residuals and this entry stays `open` on their account. Two further limits, from the observation record's §5 and repeated here because they bound what the 10/10 means: anything failing at less than roughly one run in ten is invisible to that sample by construction, and none of it says anything about GitHub Actions, where `.github/workflows/ci.yml` has still never executed.
  **6. PRE-EXISTING, re-measured at this HEAD rather than inherited — and the answer has changed, which is why it was re-run.** `01-VERIFICATION.md` recorded at its own HEAD `307f7f2` that none of `wayland_portal_global_hotkey_test.dart`, `daemon_startup_test.dart` or `single_instance_lock_test.dart` appears in `git diff --name-only 52cebe8..HEAD`, and that still holds for that range: re-run against `52cebe8..307f7f2` it returns nothing. **Re-run at this HEAD it no longer does**, and the reason is benign and is stated rather than glossed: `test/infrastructure/hotkey/wayland_portal_global_hotkey_test.dart` and `test/support/fake_global_shortcuts_portal.dart` now appear, from exactly one commit — `9eb8a47`, plan 01-23's own barrier change, the fix for this entry. `git log --oneline 52cebe8..HEAD` over both AD-14 socket suites is **empty**, and over `dart_test.yaml` is **empty**. So the defect was not introduced by the gap-closure set; the only in-range change to any of these files is the one made to address it.
  **One disagreement with an existing record, stated here and not written over there.** `01-VERIFICATION.md` `gaps[3].artifacts` gives the lock suite's path as `test/architecture/single_instance_lock_test.dart`. At this HEAD the file is at `test/infrastructure/system/single_instance_lock_test.dart` and there is no `test/architecture/single_instance_lock_test.dart` — `find test -name single_instance_lock_test.dart` returns the one path. The verification's row is otherwise exact (the file, the row title, the frequency), so this is a path slip and not a different file. It is corrected in this entry's `location:` and the verification report is left as written, which is the motion DW-129 made for plan 01-03's truth and DW-123 made for DW-9/26/44. **Tooling note for whoever re-derives these anchors:** the interactive shell in this environment shadows `grep` with ugrep 7.8.4, which anchors `$` anywhere in a BRE; every count and line number above was taken with `/usr/bin/grep` (GNU grep 3.11).
status: open
